# Decisions

Why things are as they are. Newest entries at the end of each section. The last section, "Lessons from the previous Design System", holds the lessons of BUILD-PLAN Appendix B, so the reasons survive.

## Versions

All dependencies are pinned to exact versions (`.npmrc`: `save-exact=true`). Each was the latest stable release on the date shown, unless the line says why not.

## Lint

- **2026-09-24 — Inline configuration is off.** `linterOptions.noInlineConfig: true`, `reportUnusedDisableDirectives: "error"` and `--max-warnings 0`: an `eslint-disable` comment produces a warning, and a warning fails the run. Exceptions go into `eslint.config.mjs`, where they are visible and protected (BUILD-PLAN rule 10).
- **2026-09-24 — TypeScript escape hatches.** `@ts-ignore`, `@ts-nocheck` and `@ts-expect-error` are errors outside test files. Test files (`*.test.ts(x)`, `*.spec.ts(x)`, `__tests__/`) may use `@ts-expect-error` with a description, to prove that a wrong call does not compile.
- **2026-09-24 — Line endings.** `.gitattributes` forces LF so Prettier gives the same result on Windows and on the Linux CI runner.
- **2026-09-25 — Colour and direction rules (P1.1).** `@veljaos/eslint-config` ships the plugin `liro` with `liro/no-raw-colors` and `liro/logical-properties`, both in its default configuration. They read every string literal, every piece of a template literal and every inline JSX `style` object. `no-raw-colors` reports Tailwind palette utilities (`bg-red-500`, `ring-offset-white`), hex literals, colour functions (`rgb(…)`, `oklch(…)`, `color-mix(…)`), named colours in `style`, and the `dark:` variant: themes switch through the tokens, and Tailwind's `dark:` follows the operating system, not `LiroProvider`. `logical-properties` reports `ml-`/`mr-`/`pl-`/`pr-`, `left-`/`right-`, `text-left`/`text-right`, `float-`/`clear-left|right`, `border-l`/`border-r`, `rounded-l|r|tl|tr|bl|br`, and physical `style` properties. Fixture tests: RuleTester cases plus `packages/eslint-config/fixtures/*.tsx`, whose `expect:` comments name the reports of each line. Limits: CSS files are not linted, and a string such as `"#add"` looks like a hex colour and is reported.
- **2026-09-25 — Radix under both names.** The import rule for consumers now also forbids `radix-ui` and `radix-ui/*`: Radix ships as the single package `radix-ui` as well as `@radix-ui/*`, and `@veljaos/ui` uses the single package from P1.5 on (AGENTS.md D10).
- **2026-09-25 — The repository lints itself with the shipped rules.** `eslint.config.mjs` imports `packages/eslint-config/src/index.ts` directly, because lint runs before the build in CI. Node 24 runs it by stripping types, so the package's own imports name `.ts` files (`allowImportingTsExtensions`) and use only erasable syntax (`erasableSyntaxOnly`). Exempt: `packages/tokens/**` (colour values live there) and `packages/eslint-config/src/**` (the rules and their tests quote forbidden classes); the fixtures are ignored.

## Typography and fonts

- **2026-09-25 — Fonts ship with @veljaos/tokens (P1.2).** `dist/fonts.css` holds every `@font-face` of the Fontsource `wght` stylesheets, unchanged except the file paths, and `tokens.css` imports it first, so consumers keep two CSS imports. Every face keeps its `unicode-range`: a browser downloads a subset only when the page has a character in it (the CJK families are split into about a hundred subsets each). The package grows to about 16 MB of font files; a page downloads only what its text needs. Families keep Fontsource's names (`Noto Sans Variable`, …), so they never collide with a font installed on the device.
- **2026-09-25 — One interface stack, CJK order by language.** `--liro-font-sans` is Noto Sans (Latin, Cyrillic, Greek), then Noto Sans Arabic and Hebrew, then the CJK families: SC, TC, JP by default; TC first for `zh-Hant`, `zh-TW`, `zh-HK`, `zh-MO`; JP first for `ja`. Han characters are shared but drawn differently, so `lang` decides. The variable is redeclared under `:lang()`, and `font-family` is set again on every element with `lang`, so the element with the attribute and everything inside it use the right order.
- **2026-09-25 — Base typography in `@layer base`.** `tokens.css` sets `font-family`, `line-height` and `letter-spacing` on `:root`, on every `[data-liro-theme]` and on every `[lang]`, and the body size (`--liro-font-size-md`, 14px) on `[data-liro-theme]` only: the root font size stays, so `rem` and Tailwind's spacing do not change. The rules sit in the lowest layer and have zero specificity (`:where`), so any utility or application style wins.
- **2026-09-25 — Per-script adjustment.** Arabic script (`ar`, `fa`, `ur`) and CJK (`ja`, `zh`) body text takes A.5's relaxed line height (1.6) and no letter spacing: negative spacing breaks the joining of Arabic letters, and CJK is set solid. The A.5 values stay as they are (`--liro-leading-base`, `--liro-tracking-body`); the adjustable ones are `--liro-leading-body`, `--liro-tracking-body-text` and `--liro-tracking-heading-text`, which the Tailwind sizes and headings use.
- **2026-09-25 — Tailwind type scale.** Tailwind's fonts, sizes, weights, line heights and letter spacing are removed and replaced: `font-sans|brand|mono`; `text-xs … text-xl` (with the body line height and spacing of the script); `text-h1 … text-h6` (size, line height, semibold, heading spacing); `font-regular|medium|semibold|bold` (A.5 calls 400 "regular", so `font-normal` no longer exists); `leading-tight|base|relaxed`; `tracking-heading|body|caps`. Tabular digits: Tailwind's `tabular-nums` (Noto Sans has the `tnum` feature).
- **2026-09-25 — Brand face.** Space Grotesk, then Inter, then the interface stack. Both are shipped: Space Grotesk has no Cyrillic or Greek, so a Cyrillic wordmark or status page falls back to Inter. Monospace (JetBrains Mono, Cascadia Code, system monospace) is not shipped: it is a stack of fonts the device may have. (Replaced on 2026-10-01: JetBrains Mono is shipped, see "Monospace is shipped".)
- **2026-10-01 — Monospace is shipped (P3.0a, decided by the owner).** `@veljaos/tokens` ships JetBrains Mono, the first family of A.5's monospace stack, from Fontsource as `JetBrains Mono Variable` (weights 100–800, one variable file per subset: Latin, Latin Extended, Cyrillic, Cyrillic Extended, Greek, Vietnamese), so variable names, case numbers and shortcut keys look the same on every device and in the tests. `--liro-font-mono` is now `'JetBrains Mono Variable', 'JetBrains Mono', 'Cascadia Code', ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, Consolas, monospace`. The reason is "Local baselines match CI" under CI: the system monospace made screenshots depend on timing.
- **2026-09-25 — The lang rule.** Text whose language differs from the page carries `lang`: `langAttribute(textLang, pageLocale)` and `useLangAttribute(textLang)` in `@veljaos/ui` return `{ lang }` when the language or the script differs (`sr-Cyrl` on a `sr-Latn` page, `ja` on a `zh-Hans` page), and nothing for a region alone. Serbian without a script counts as Latin (B.1). With `lang`, the browser applies the font's Serbian forms to Cyrillic italics (б, г, д, п, т) and the JP family to Japanese. Text in the other direction also needs `dir`; the Scripts story sets it on each sample.

## Buttons

- **2026-09-25 — Button weights (decided by the owner).** Carried over from the previous Design System, which was built on Mantine:
  - **primary emphasis = "filled":** the family colour as background, white text (`text.onAccent`); gray and orange fill with shade 7. Hover one shade darker, press two (blue 6 → 7 → 8; gray and orange 7 → 8 → 9).
  - **secondary emphasis = "light":** the family's subtle tint as background (blue: `brand.subtle` = blue0) with the family's text colour, no border; hover goes to the stronger tint (`brand.subtleHover` = blue1). **Exception:** the neutral family's secondary is **"default"**: raised surface, `border.default` (gray3) border, `text.primary` text; hover `surface.hover`.
  - **menu emphasis = "subtle":** no background, no border, the family's text colour; a background appears only on hover (`surface.hover` for neutral, the family's subtle tint otherwise). A menu-emphasis button standing alone outside a menu looks the same.
  - **Status badge** (P1.4): the tone's fg on the tone's bg; no border unless `withBorder` (1px, the tone's border colour); radius xs (2px); padding 2px 6px; size xs (12px); weight 600; line height 1.4; 4px between icon and label; never wraps.
- **2026-09-25 — Hover text one shade stronger (decided by the owner).** The contrast script measured the family's text on the stronger tint below 4.5:1: 4.08–4.29 in the light theme (primary, verify, document, positive) and 4.28/4.31 in the dark theme on the overlay surface (primary, destructive). On hover, light and subtle buttons therefore also take the stronger text, `fgHover`: one shade darker in light (7 → 8; caution 8 → 9), one lighter in dark (blue 4 → 3, the others 3 → 2). The tints stay exactly as decided.
- **2026-09-25 — Family tokens derived from the brand pattern.** A.6 gives only the filled shade. The other family colours follow the established brand tokens: the tint is shade 0 and its hover shade 1 in light, and the fill colour at 0.16 and 0.26 in dark (as `brand.subtle`); the text colour is `text.brand` for primary and the matching status tone's `fg` for the others (positive ↔ success, destructive ↔ danger, caution ↔ warning, document ↔ premium, neutral ↔ `text.primary`; verify, which has no tone, as the tones: teal 7 / teal 3). They are the `--liro-family-<family>-*` meanings and the `bg-family-*`, `text-family-*` utilities. `packages/tokens/src/contrast.ts` measures every text-on-background pair of every family and tone, in both themes, composited over every opaque surface (B.6); `pnpm build` fails below 4.5:1.
- **2026-09-25 — Button API.** `Button` takes `intent`, or `family` + `icon`; `label` is required in both, because the Design System has no text of its own (an intent brings family, emphasis, icon and confirmation, not words). No `color`, no `variant`: the types reject them (tested with `@ts-expect-error`). A family without an intent defaults to secondary emphasis, so a screen does not collect filled buttons by accident. `IconButton` takes the same props and draws only the icon; `CompactIconButton` makes the family optional (default neutral) and defaults to menu (subtle) emphasis, as Mantine's ActionIcon. Both use `label` as the accessible name and title. The confirmation of `delete` (and module actions) is P2.4; the button carries `data-confirms` until then. The disabled reason is shown by the unavailable action of P2.7.
- **2026-09-25 — Button sizes (decided by the owner; values read from Mantine).** The previous Design System used Mantine's theme `defaultRadius: 'md'` and Button size `'sm'`; icon-only buttons were ActionIcon with the default size `'md'`, neutral and subtle. Source: the npm package `@mantine/core` 9.6.2, `styles/Button.css`, `styles/ActionIcon.css` and `styles/default-css-variables.css`:
  - `--button-height-sm: 36px` (our `--liro-size-control-height`); `--button-padding-x-sm: 18px`; with a left section (the icon every Liro button has), `padding-inline-start: calc(var(--button-padding-x) / 1.5)` = 12px; the section's `margin-inline-end: var(--mantine-spacing-xs)` = 10px between icon and label; `font-weight: var(--mantine-font-weight-medium)` = 600; `line-height: 1`; `border: 1px solid transparent`; `border-radius: var(--mantine-radius-default)` = radius md, 8px.
  - Font size: the owner's theme `sm` = 13px (A.5 `sm`; Mantine's own default `sm` is 14px).
  - Disabled, every variant: `border: 1px solid transparent`, `background: var(--mantine-color-disabled)`, `color: var(--mantine-color-disabled-color)`; here `surface.disabled` and `text.disabled`.
  - ActionIcon: `--ai-size-md: 28px`, the same default radius and transparent border.
  - Icon size (from the owner, the old ActionButton source; not in Mantine's CSS): 15px in a button with text.
  - Button therefore has no `size` prop: a second size comes when a screen needs one and its values are known.
- **2026-09-25 — Two icon-only buttons (decided by the owner, from the old ActionButton, P1.3a).** An action without visible text is **`IconButton`**: the normal 36px button (control height) with 8px horizontal padding and a 16px icon, the label as its `aria-label` and title; family and emphasis as for `Button` (an intent's own emphasis, secondary for a family). The 28px Mantine ActionIcon is **`CompactIconButton`**: neutral and subtle (menu emphasis) by default, 16px icon, used **only in tight places** such as table row menus and close buttons. Both keep the target size above 24×24px.
- **2026-09-25 — Direction arrows mirror.** The `back` (ArrowLeft) and `next` (ArrowRight) icons are drawn mirrored in right-to-left, so "next" points forward in reading order (A.6 marks `back`; `next` is the same class of icon, B.7).
- **2026-09-25 — StatusBadge and toneFor (P1.4).** `StatusBadge` takes `label` and `tone`, with optional `withBorder` and `icon`, in the badge weights above; line height 1.4 is an arbitrary value (`leading-[1.4]`) because A.5 has no 1.4 and the owner gave it exactly. `toneFor(status, map, fallback = 'neutral')` reads the tone from a map the application gives as data; an unknown status (including inherited names such as `toString`) gets the fallback. The example map of A.7 lives only in the StatusBadge stories. Every tone's fg on its bg is measured by the contrast script in both themes over every surface (lowest: dark danger on the overlay surface, 4.66).
- **2026-09-29 — Long labels wrap (decided by the owner, P2.7d).** A button label is never cut with "…". It stays on one line while it fits; where it does not, it wraps with each line centred and the button grows in height from its minimum (36px, 30px for the small bar buttons). Two lines is the normal limit; a third appears only when even two do not fit (the owner, asked about that case: text is never hidden). A text button shrinks to its container (`min-w-0`, `max-w-full`), so it wraps only when it is alone on its line and still too wide; its line height is the font size plus 4px (`leading-[calc(1em+4px)]`), so two lines do not touch and a single line stays on exactly the same pixels (1.25 moved it by one). A text button is `h-fit`, so it is never stretched to the height of a flex row (a minimum height alone would let it stretch); the chevron half of SplitAction takes the height of the main half. IconButton and CompactIconButton keep their fixed size. In `ActionGroup` and `BulkActionBar` the actions before the main one move into the "More" menu first (BulkActionBar gained the menu here, with a 28px "More" button and the same one confirmation for a chosen action), so wrapping is the last resort; both measure the unwrapped widths in an invisible copy of the row.

## Tokens

- **2026-09-25 — One source (P1.1).** Every value and meaning is in `packages/tokens/src/tokens.ts`. The build (`scripts/build.ts`, run by Node with type stripping) generates `dist/tokens.css`, `dist/theme.css` and `dist/tokens.json` from it, so the three cannot drift apart. `src/tokens.test.ts` reads Appendix A from `BUILD-PLAN.md` and compares every ramp, meaning, tone, spacing step, radius, shadow, duration, easing, breakpoint, size and layer with it (the look is carried over 1:1).
- **2026-09-25 — Three layers in CSS.** Values (`--liro-blue-6`, `--liro-space-md`, …) are declared once on `:root`. Meanings (`--liro-surface-page`, `--liro-status-danger-fg`, …) are declared per theme and refer to values with `var()`; the light block also matches `:root`, so the light theme applies without a provider. Any element may carry `data-liro-theme`, so both themes can appear on one page (the Tokens page shows them side by side). The ramps are not Tailwind utilities: components cannot reach a value.
- **2026-09-25 — The Tailwind theme removes Tailwind's own look.** `theme.css` resets `--color-*`, `--radius-*`, `--shadow-*`, `--ease-*` and `--breakpoint-*` and puts the Liro ones in their place, so `bg-red-500` or `rounded-2xl` do not exist, and the breakpoints are A.4's (`md` = 62em). Utilities refer to the `--liro-*` variables through `@theme inline`, so each element resolves its own theme.
- **2026-09-25 — Each meaning reaches only the utilities it is for.** Tailwind 4 reads `--text-color-*` for `text-*`, `--background-color-*` for `bg-*` and `--border-color-*` for `border-*` and `divide-*` before the shared `--color-*`. Text meanings are `text-primary`, `text-secondary`, `text-tertiary`, `text-disabled`, `text-on-accent`, `text-brand`, `text-link`; surfaces `bg-surface-*`; borders `border-default|strong|subtle|brand|focus`, with `outline-focus` and `ring-focus`; brand backgrounds `bg-brand-solid|solid-hover|solid-active|subtle|subtle-hover` with `text-brand-on-solid`, and `brand-accent` for any utility; tones `text-status-<tone>-fg`, `bg-status-<tone>-bg`, `border-status-<tone>-border`, and `bg-|fill-|stroke-status-<tone>-solid`. So `text-brand-solid` and `text-status-danger-solid` do not exist (A.2: `brand.solid` is a background; B.6: `solid` is never text).
- **2026-09-25 — shadcn/ui variables map onto Liro meanings.** `--background` page, `--foreground` text primary, `--card` raised, `--popover` overlay, `--primary` brand solid (on it brand onSolid), `--secondary` and `--muted` sunken, `--muted-foreground` text secondary, `--accent` hover surface, `--destructive` danger solid, `--border` border default, `--input` border strong, `--ring` border focus, `--radius` radius md. They are declared on `:root` and on every themed element, so each resolves in its own theme. Because text meanings win for `text-*`, `text-primary` is the primary text colour (not shadcn's brand blue), `text-secondary` the secondary text colour, and `text-destructive` the danger text colour; P2.1 adapts the few primitives that use `text-primary` or `text-white`.
- **2026-09-25 — Spacing uses Tailwind's scale.** With `--spacing: 0.25rem`, `p-1` … `p-16` are exactly A.3's 4 … 64px. Named spacing (`p-xs`) was not added: `max-w-xs` and the other `max-w-*` utilities would read `--spacing-xs` before the container sizes. `--liro-space-*` exist for CSS. Sizes are utilities: `h-header`, `h-control`, `h-control-sm`, `max-w-content`. Layers are variables only: `z-(--liro-layer-modal)`.
- **2026-09-25 — Motion.** The default `transition` uses `--liro-duration-base` and `--liro-ease-standard`; `ease-standard|decelerate|accelerate` exist. With `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, every `--liro-duration-*` is 0ms.
- **2026-09-25 — tokens.json** has `color` (ramps and common), `meaning.light|dark` (every meaning and tone, resolved to a colour), `space`, `radius`, `shadow.light|dark`, `motion`, `breakpoint`, `size` and `layer`, for renderers without CSS.
- **2026-09-25 — Three meanings added (P2.1, decided by the owner).** `surface.inverse` (gray9 / gray2) and `text.onInverse` (white / black), for the tooltip: the previous Design System's Mantine tooltip is drawn inverted, and no meaning of Appendix A had those values. `border.control` (gray6 in both themes): the boundary of inputs, checkboxes and radios and the off track of a switch. Mantine draws them with gray-4 (the switch track gray-3), about 1.5:1 against white; WCAG 2.2 AA 1.4.11 asks for 3:1, so this **deliberately departs from the old Mantine look**. gray6 measures at least 3.13:1 (dark, on the overlay surface) and 3.75:1 (light, on the sunken surface) on all five surfaces; `contrast.ts` checks it with a 3:1 minimum, and text.onInverse on surface.inverse with 4.5:1. Focus stays border.focus (blue). The three rows were added to Appendix A.2, which the tokens test reads. Utilities: `bg-surface-inverse`, `text-on-inverse`, `border-control`, and `bg-control` for the switch track.

## Primitives

- **2026-09-25 — How the primitives were added (P2.1).** The shadcn CLI 4.21.0 generated all 24 components of the plan in a scratch Vite project (style `radix-vega`, `--rtl`, which already writes logical classes). Each file was copied into `packages/ui/src/primitives/` and adapted: shadcn's colours replaced by Liro meanings, its sizes by the Mantine 9.6.2 defaults the previous Design System used (below), `variant`/`size` props replaced by the Liro family, emphasis and shape, every text of its own (`"Close"`, `"Command Palette"`, react-day-picker's English labels) replaced by a required prop, and the types made to pass `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`. shadcn's `toggle` is not kept: the toggle group renders its own items. Nothing in `primitives/` is exported from `src/index.ts` (AGENTS.md D10). Each primitive has an internal story under "Internal/Primitives", checked by axe in light/dark × ltr/rtl like every story.
- **2026-09-25 — One source for the button look.** The colours of Appendix A.6 by family and emphasis and the Mantine sizes of the three shapes moved from `components/button.tsx` to `primitives/button.tsx` (`buttonClassName`), unchanged; Button, IconButton and CompactIconButton use it, and so do the close button of a dialog, the actions of an alert dialog and the buttons inside other primitives.
- **2026-09-25 — Overlays render inside the provider.** Radix portals render into the document body by default, outside the element that carries `data-liro-theme`, `dir` and `lang`: a dialog in the dark theme would have been light. LiroProvider now renders an empty `display: contents` element after its children, and every portal of the primitives renders into it (`provider/portal.ts`, internal).
- **2026-09-25 — `cn` knows the Liro theme.** `primitives/cn.ts` is clsx plus tailwind-merge extended with the Liro type scale, weights, leading, tracking, easing and sizes. Without it, `font-regular` was read as a font family (and removed `font-sans`) and `text-h1` as a colour (and removed `text-primary`). Tested in `cn.test.ts`.
- **2026-09-25 — Animations.** Mantine's transitions, written as keyframes in `packages/ui/src/styles.css` (`animate-liro-*`): fade 150ms for popover, menu and select (our base duration), 100ms for tooltips (fast), fade-down 30px for the modal, slide with fade for the drawer, fade for the backdrop and height with opacity for a collapsible, 200ms each (Mantine's modal duration; no motion token has it, so those elements also carry `motion-reduce:animate-none`). Easing: our standard curve. Stories with an open overlay wait for the animations to end before the accessibility check.
- **2026-09-25 — Primitives do not rely on a reset.** `@veljaos/ui/styles.css` still ships no preflight, and Storybook runs without one, so the browser's own styles showed through: native buttons kept their grey background and border (a calendar day measured 2.02:1 in the dark theme), and `w-full` with padding overflowed, because elements are content-box without preflight. Every primitive drawn on a `<button>` starts from `BUTTON_RESET` (`primitives/classes.ts`: no margin, border, background or padding, the Liro font), and `styles.css` sets `box-sizing: border-box` on the elements of the primitives only (`:where([data-slot])`, zero specificity, in `@layer base`) — not a reset of the application.
- **2026-09-25 — Open overlays and hidden triggers.** While a select is open, Radix hides everything outside its list with `aria-hidden`, the trigger included; the trigger then leaves the tab order (`tabIndex -1`), so nothing hidden stays reachable (axe `aria-hidden-focus`), and Radix returns the focus to it on close. The separators of a select and of a command list are decoration (`role="none"`): a listbox may contain only options and groups. cmdk names its list "Suggestions" in English; `CommandList` requires a `label`. Stories hold an open select and an open submenu with a controlled `open`: Radix closes a select when the window resizes (as a full-page screenshot does) and a submenu when the focus moves.
- **2026-09-25 — No opacity for disabled.** Mantine dims disabled inputs, menu items, tabs and calendar days with opacity 0.5–0.6; the primitives use surface.disabled and text.disabled instead, as Button does (Appendix B.6).
- **2026-09-25 — Highlighted menu item.** The item under the pointer or the keyboard takes surface.sunken. Mantine uses gray-1; surface.hover (gray0) is 1.04:1 against white, too faint to show where keyboard focus is. Options of a select and a command list are filled with the brand colour when chosen with the keyboard, as Mantine's Combobox does.
- **2026-09-25 — Right-to-left, primitive by primitive (P2.1).** Checked in the stories in right-to-left and in the code:
  - **Sheet — fixed.** shadcn's sides are physical (`left`, `right`) and stay on the same side of the screen in right-to-left. The side is now logical (`start`, `end`, `top`, `bottom`) and it slides in from that side; default `start`, as Mantine's drawer, which also follows the direction.
  - **Calendar — fixed.** shadcn does not pass `dir` to react-day-picker: in right-to-left the left and right arrow keys moved to the previous and next day the wrong way round (Appendix B.7). The calendar now passes the provider's direction, and react-day-picker swaps the keys. Its default navigation always draws the chevrons left and right, so they are mirrored in right-to-left (`rtl:-scale-x-100`, as the Button arrows); its `around` layout, which swaps them itself, is not offered, so they cannot turn twice.
  - **Progress — fixed.** shadcn moves the filled part with `translateX`, which fills from the left in both directions. The filled part now has an inline size, so it grows from the leading edge (B.7: a bar is a metaphor for reading).
  - **Dropdown menu.** Radix reads the direction from the provider: a submenu opens to the left in right-to-left, and ArrowLeft opens it. Its chevron is drawn mirrored (`rtl:-scale-x-100`, as the Button arrows).
  - **Select, tabs, radio group, toggle group.** Arrow keys follow the direction through Radix; the select's chevron sits at the inline end; the vertical tab list's line is at the inline end.
  - **Switch.** The thumb moves along `inset-inline-start`, so it starts at the leading edge in both directions (shadcn used `translate-x` with an `rtl:` correction).
  - **Scroll area.** The vertical scrollbar sits at the inline end (Radix reads the direction). The viewport is focusable, so it can be scrolled with the keyboard.
  - **Dialog, alert dialog, popover, tooltip, command.** Centred, or placed by Radix from the trigger; nothing directional. The dialog's close button sits at the inline end.
- **2026-09-25 — Values taken from Mantine defaults (P2.1).** From `@mantine/core` 9.6.2 `styles/*.css` and the components' `defaultProps` (`esm/components/*`), with the owner's theme (radius md 8px, font sizes of A.5) and Liro meanings for every colour:
  - Input.css, size 'sm': height 36px, padding 12px (36 / 3), radius 8px, 1px border, 13px text; the border takes the focus colour on focus. Textarea: 5.5px vertical padding (multiline), no resize. Input.Label: 13px, weight 600.
  - Checkbox.css and Radio.css, size 'sm': 20px, radius 4px (checkbox: `radius: 'sm'` in its defaultProps, which overrides the theme's md) or full, 1px border; the mark 60% of the box (checkbox), an 8px dot (radio); 100ms transition.
  - Switch.css, size 'sm': track 38 × 20px, thumb 14px, 2.5px from the edge, 150ms.
  - Combobox.css, size 'sm', and OptionsDropdown: dropdown padding 4px; option padding 6px 10px, 13px, radius 8px; chevron 18px in a 34px right section; check 0.8em, 8px gap, before the text; options at most 220px high; group label weight 600 at 0.85 × 13px (12px here, the smallest A.5 size).
  - Popover.css and Popover defaults: padding 12px 16px, 1px border, radius 8px, no shadow, 8px from the target, fade 150ms.
  - Menu.css: dropdown padding 4px; item 13px, padding 6.67px (10 / 1.5) 12px, radius 8px; label 12px, weight 600, padding 5px 12px; divider 1px with 4px margins; submenu item end padding 5px; check column 12px with 8px after it.
  - Tooltip.css and Tooltip defaults: padding 5px 10px, no wrapping, 5px offset, arrow 4px, fade 100ms. The owner's theme: radius 4px, 12px text, with an arrow, 300ms open delay.
  - Modal.css, ModalBase.css and Modal defaults: width 440px (size 'md'), 5dvh from the top and 5vw from the sides, shadow xl, radius 8px; header at least 60px with 16px padding (11px at the end); body 16px padding; title 14px, weight 400, line height 1; fade-down 30px, 200ms. Actions: Group, 16px gap, at the end.
  - Drawer.css and DrawerRoot: 440px (size 'md'), full height, no radius, shadow xl, slides from its side, 200ms.
  - Overlay.css: a backdrop over the whole screen (colour: surface.backdrop).
  - CloseButton: the 28px CompactIconButton look ("Two icon-only buttons").
  - Tabs.css, variant 'default': 2px line; tab padding 10px 16px, 13px, line height 1, radius 8px on the outer corners; 10px between icon and label.
  - SegmentedControl.css, size 'sm' (for the toggle group): padding 4px, radius 8px, items 13px, weight 600, padding 3px 10px, radius 4px, 1px separators, shadow xs on the pressed item; colour transition 150ms here (our base duration; Mantine 200ms).
  - Divider.css: 1px.
  - ScrollArea.css and defaults: 12px scrollbars, 2.4px padding, thumb fully rounded with a 44px grip, shown on hover and hidden after 1000ms.
  - Skeleton.css: radius 8px, opacity 0.4 → 1 → 0.4 over 1500ms.
  - Avatar.css, size 'md': 38px circle, bold initials at 38 / 2.5 = 15.2px (16px here, the nearest A.5 size).
  - Progress.css, size 'md': 8px high, radius 8px, width transition 100ms.
  - Collapse defaults: 200ms, height and opacity.
  - `@mantine/dates` 9.6.2 (Day, WeekdaysRow, CalendarHeader): days and header controls 36px, radius 8px, day text 36 / 2.8 ≈ 12.9px (13px here), weekday names 13px regular with 6px under them, month name weight 600 with 10px under the header, chevrons 60% of the control, 0.5px around each day; days of other months shown.
- **2026-09-25 — Open for later steps.** The error look of a field (Mantine: red border and red text) is left to Field (P2.2): status.danger.border does not reach 3:1 as a control boundary. Mantine highlights "today" in the calendar only when asked (`highlightToday` is false by default); DateField (P2.3) decides. The calendar's caption is "month year" from `format.monthName`; a locale that writes the year first (Japanese: 2026年9月) may need a month-and-year format in P2.3. Weekday names are `format.weekdayName(…, 'short')` ("Sun"); Mantine shows two letters.

## Fields

- **2026-09-25 — P2.2 split.** P2.2 is split into P2.2a (Field, TextField, TextAreaField, SelectField, CheckboxField, RadioGroupField, SwitchField) and P2.2b (ComboboxField with asynchronous search, MultiSelectField), each with its own pull request (BUILD-PLAN rule 6).
- **2026-09-25 — Read-only is text, not disabled (decided by the owner).** A read-only field shows its value as plain text in the full text colour, with no border and no field background, at the same height (36px) and inner padding (12px) as an editable field, so a form that mixes both stays aligned. The value can be focused, selected and copied; the control is `readonly` (or `aria-readonly` for checkboxes, radios and switches), and its title is `messages['field.readOnly']`. A read-only checkbox, radio or switch keeps its colours and state but cannot change. **The previous Design System rendered read-only as disabled; this deliberately replaces that.** Disabled stays greyed (surface.disabled, text.disabled) and shows its reason, when given, as text under the field, linked with `aria-describedby`. Numbers and amounts will keep tabular digits and their alignment in read-only (P2.3).
- **2026-09-25 — The error look (decided by the owner).** An invalid field's border takes `status.danger.fg` (red7 light / red3 dark; the utility `border-status-danger-fg`, added to the Tailwind theme for this) and the message under the field uses the same colour with an icon. Colour is never the only signal: the message is always shown, the control is `aria-invalid`, and the message is linked with `aria-describedby`, so a screen reader reads it with the field. `contrast.ts` measures the border at 3:1 or more on every surface in both themes. `status.danger.border` (red2) was too light for a control boundary. Moving the focus to the first invalid field on submit belongs to the form (P3.5).
- **2026-09-25 — Field layout (Mantine Input.Wrapper and InlineInput).** Label 13px, weight 600; the required mark is Mantine's " *" in the error colour, hidden from assistive technology (the control is `required`) and titled with `messages['field.required']`. Description, error and disabled reason: the label size minus 2px, which is 11px, so 12px here (the smallest A.5 size), line height 1.2 (A.5 tight, 1.25, here). The control sits 5px under a description and 5px above an error. Checkbox, switch and radio options: the label 12px after the control, 13px on a 20px line, description and error 5px under it; radio options 16px apart. A radio group is named by its label element through `aria-labelledby` (Mantine renders it as a div, not a label).
- **2026-09-25 — Switch thumb dot (P2.1 correction).** Mantine's switch draws a dot in the thumb (`withThumbIndicator`, on by default), 40% of the thumb in the track's colour; P2.1 had missed it. Added with the switch fields. Also corrected: a disabled switch that is on was still filled with the brand colour; it now takes surface.disabled like an off one, and the thumb's side shows the state.
- **2026-09-28 — ComboboxField and MultiSelectField (P2.2b).** Both are an input with a list under it, following the WAI-ARIA combobox pattern: the input keeps the focus, arrows move the active option (announced through `aria-activedescendant`), Enter picks it, Escape closes. The list is our own, not cmdk: cmdk moves the focus into its own input and filters by its own score, while a field must keep the focus, submit a value and let the application's search decide the results. Up and down wrap around and skip disabled options, as Mantine's Combobox (`loop`); Home and End go to the first and last usable option. `aria-expanded` is true only while options are shown: a combobox that says it is expanded must control a listbox (axe `aria-required-attr`), and "loading" and "nothing found" are status messages under the list (`role="status"`), outside the listbox.
  - **ComboboxField:** one choice. With `onSearch`, the application searches after typing pauses for `searchDelay` (default **300ms**, approved by the owner on 2026-09-28; Mantine has no debounce) and passes the results as `options`, with `loading` while it works; without `onSearch`, the field filters `options` itself, ignoring case and accents in the page's locale. Clearing the text clears the choice; leaving the field otherwise restores the chosen label. Read-only shows the chosen label as text, like TextField.
  - **MultiSelectField:** several choices from a known list, as pills inside the field; the list stays open while choosing, a click or Enter toggles an option, Backspace in the empty typing area removes the last pill. Read-only shows the pills without remove buttons, as a list. One hidden input per value when `name` is given.
  - **Values from Mantine 9.6.2** (`styles/Pill.css`, `styles/PillsInput.css`, size 'sm'; approved by the owner on 2026-09-28): pill 22px high, 12px text, padding 0.8em, fully rounded, 8px between pills (PillGroup), the pill's colour gray-1 (here the neutral tone, `status.neutral` fg on bg); remove button 2em wide with 0.1em / 0.3em inline padding, rounded on the end side; the typing area at least 100px wide and 1.6em high; the field 5.5px top and bottom (multiline input). The list uses the Combobox values of P2.1. **One departure (approved by the owner: the minimum target size wins over the Mantine value):** Mantine's remove button is the pill's height (22px); ours is 24px high, 1px beyond the pill on each side, invisible, so the target is 24 × 24px (Definition of done, section 6). Its width is now a fixed 24px: `2em` had measured 26.7px, because a `<button>` does not inherit the font size (Mantine sets `font-size: inherit`, so its 2em is 24px); the baselines of the MultiSelectField stories changed by that.
  - **Messages:** `field.loading`, `field.noResults` and `field.remove(label)` (the remove button's accessible name).
- **2026-10-01 — One gap between label and control (P3.2a, decided by the owner).** The control now sits 4px (spacing xxs) under the label block, with or without a description, in every field drawn with `Field` (all stacked fields; checkbox, switch and radio options keep their label beside the control). Before, a label alone had no gap at all (only its 1.45 line height), so "Type F-114 to confirm" sat on its input. **Mantine 9.6.2** (`Input.mjs`, `get-input-offsets.mjs`): the input gets `margin-top: calc(var(--mantine-spacing-xs) / 2)` = 5px only when a description (or an error) stands above it, and nothing under a label alone, the same inconsistency; xxs is the spacing token nearest to its 5px. The 5px above an error is unchanged.

## Numbers, money and dates

- **2026-09-28 — P2.3 split.** P2.3 is split into P2.3a (NumberField, MoneyField), P2.3b (DateField, DateRangeField) and P2.3c (PeriodField with `yearStartMonth` and presets), each with its own pull request (BUILD-PLAN rule 6).
- **2026-09-28 — Currency position (decided by the owner).** MoneyField puts the currency where `format.money` puts it for the locale, so an amount looks the same in a field and in text: Serbian `1.234,56 EUR`, English `EUR 1,234.56`; in right-to-left the side flips with the direction. The side is read from `format.money` itself (`currencyFirst`), so an application's own `format` is followed too. If the currency becomes selectable, the selector takes the same position.
- **2026-09-28 — Alignment (decided by the owner).** Number and money fields are start-aligned in forms, as Mantine's NumberInput and the previous Design System. Inside the editable grid (P3.4) and any table cell editor they are end-aligned, like table numbers. Tabular digits in both.
- **2026-09-28 — DateField's calendar (decided by the owner).** The calendar opens only from a calendar button at the end of the field (at the other side in right-to-left), with an accessible label, or with Alt+ArrowDown from the field. The focus moves into the calendar when it opens and back to the field when it closes; Escape closes it. Typing a date never opens it. **This deliberately departs from Mantine's DateInput**, which opens the calendar when the field gets the focus (`@mantine/dates` 9.6.2, `DateInput`: `onFocus` and `onClick` open it, `trapFocus: false`): for fast keyboard entry, and because the calendar must be reachable by keyboard (WCAG 2.1.1).
- **2026-09-28 — Unreadable input (decided by the owner).** When the user leaves a number, money or date field with text that cannot be read ("abc"), the text stays in the field for correction, the value is null (never 0, Appendix B.4), and the field shows its own message from the provider (`field.invalidNumber`, and `field.invalidDate` in P2.3b; English defaults), with the error look of "Fields" (danger border, message under the field, `aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`). It also tells the application through `onValidityChange(false)`, so a form can block saving; `onValidityChange(true)` when the text is readable again or the application sets a new value. The application's own `error` is shown instead of the field's message. **This departs from Mantine**, whose DateInput and NumberInput restore the last valid value on blur (`fixOnBlur`), so an unaccepted entry could go unnoticed.
- **2026-09-28 — NumberField and MoneyField (P2.3a).** No input mask (Appendix B.3): the user types and pastes freely; the text is read by `format.parseNumber` on leaving the field or on Enter, and shown again through `format.number` with `decimals` (zeros added, never rounded; MoneyField defaults to `format.moneyDecimals`). The value is a decimal string or null; `onChange` is called only when it changes, and a hidden input submits it under `name` (not the text shown). A new value from the application replaces text being typed; the value the field itself just reported does not. The text is laid out left to right in every direction (`dir="ltr"` on the input, aligned to the field's start with `rtl:text-end`), because the bidi algorithm moved the minus sign of "-42,00" to the end in a right-to-left field (see "Provider"). The right-to-left alignment and padding follow the provider's direction (`useLiro().direction`, AGENTS.md D11), not CSS: Tailwind's `rtl:` variant matches any element under `[dir=rtl]`, so a left-to-right field inside a right-to-left page (the "Currency position" story) was aligned and padded as right-to-left; and `:dir(rtl)` cannot be used either, because Vite's CSS minifier (Lightning CSS) rewrites it into a list of right-to-left languages (`:lang(ar), :lang(he) …`), which never matched in Storybook. An application sets the direction through LiroProvider; the Arabic stories of these fields do the same with a nested provider. `inputMode="decimal"` (Mantine's NumberInput) brings the numeric keyboard on phones. The currency is a second `<label>` of the input: pressing it puts the caret in the amount, and the input's name includes it ("Amount EUR"). Unit tests read every Appendix B.3 form through the field's logic on a dot-comma and a comma-dot screen, and read back every text the field shows in every scheme.
- **2026-09-28 — Values taken from Mantine defaults (P2.3a).** `@mantine/core` 9.6.2: NumberInput `inputMode` 'decimal' (`esm/components/NumberInput/NumberInput.mjs`); the currency as an input section (`styles/Input.css`): at least the input height minus 2px wide (34px), content centred, in the dimmed colour (here text.secondary), no padding between it and the text beside it. The input itself is Input 'sm' as in P2.1.
- **2026-09-28 — DateField and DateRangeField (P2.3b).** A date is typed (read by `format.parseDate` on leaving the field or on Enter) or picked in the calendar; the value is YYYY-MM-DD and a hidden input submits it. The typed-entry logic of NumberField moved to `use-entry.ts`, shared by all four fields. The calendar opens on the chosen date's month, else on the month of the provider's `today` (tested with a `today` different from the device's date) and focuses the day in its tab order (the chosen day, else today), found in the popover's open event: `autoFocus` is forbidden by `jsx-a11y/no-autofocus`. Closing it (a pick, Escape, a press outside) returns the focus to the field. "Today" is not highlighted (Mantine's `highlightToday` is false by default). DateRangeField is one field "start – end" with two typing areas, named for assistive technology by the field's label plus `messages['field.rangeStart']` / `['field.rangeEnd']`; in the calendar the first day pressed is one end and the second the other, in either order. Each end's typing area is as wide as its text (`field-sizing: content`, at least 8 characters), so the field reads "start – end" as Mantine writes it: two equal halves left a gap before the dash. The room up to the calendar button is empty. Both typing areas are `dir="auto"` (a date in Latin digits, which has no strong direction, is laid out left to right; a date that Intl writes with right-to-left marks, Arabic, stays right to left) and have 4px at their inline end: Chromium on Linux draws hinted text a pixel or two wider than it lays it out, and an area exactly as wide as its text cut the last digit of the end date (not reproducible on Windows, whose renderer does not drift). The start padding of the field is on the frame, in the page's direction. Either end may be open. An end before the start is an error of its own (`messages['field.invalidRange']`) and makes the range invalid (`onValidityChange(false)`); it is never swapped silently. New messages: `field.invalidDate`, `field.invalidRange`, `field.openCalendar` (the calendar button's name), `field.rangeStart`, `field.rangeEnd`, and `calendar.previousMonth`, `calendar.nextMonth`, `calendar.navigation` (the calendar's own labels).
- **2026-09-28 — `DateField` the type renamed `DatePart`.** The provider exported a type `DateField` (`'day' | 'month' | 'year'`, the order of a locale's numeric date) since P1.5; the plan names the component `DateField`, so the type is now `DatePart`. A breaking change of an alpha type, stated in the changeset.
- **2026-09-28 — Values taken from Mantine defaults (P2.3b).** `@mantine/dates` 9.6.2: the dropdown opens under the field, aligned to its start (`DateInput` and `PickerInputBase`: `position: 'bottom-start'`); a range is written "start – end" (`DatesProvider`: `labelSeparator: '–'`, with a space on each side in `getFormattedDate`); one month shown (`numberOfColumns` 1). The calendar button is Mantine's usual right-section ActionIcon: the 28px compact button, neutral and subtle, with a 16px icon (lucide `CalendarDays`), in a section at least 34px wide (`@mantine/core` Input.css, as MoneyField's currency). The calendar is the P2.1 calendar in the P2.1 popover (padding 12px 16px).
- **2026-09-28 — PeriodField carried over from the old PeriodPicker (decided by the owner, P2.3c).** It replaces the plan's month/quarter/year picker. The trigger is a neutral "default" button (Buttons: raised surface, `border.default`): a 15px calendar icon at the start, a 14px chevron down at the end, the current period as its label in regular weight. It opens a popover (radius md, shadow md, under the trigger at its start) with two parts side by side: on the start side a 190px column of presets, separated by a `border.subtle` line, as small buttons aligned to the start — the active one in the light weight (primary family, secondary emphasis) with semibold text, the others subtle and neutral (menu emphasis) in regular weight — with an optional "Clear" (`clearable`: no period, meaning all periods) under a divider; on the other side the caption "Custom range" over a range calendar, where the second day pressed applies the range and closes the popover. Default presets, in order: today, this week, this month, last month, this quarter, last quarter, year to date, last year (`presets` chooses and orders them). A preset is active when the value is exactly its range (the first such preset). Presets follow the business year (`yearStartMonth`), the quarter basis and the provider's `today` and `weekStartsOn`; tested with a July business year, a `today` other than the device's date, weeks across a year end and leap years. The value is a range of YYYY-MM-DD dates, or null for all periods; `startName`/`endName` submit it. On a narrow screen the two parts stack, presets first (the popover would not fit a phone side by side).
- **2026-09-28 — Business year and quarter names (decided by the owner).** A business year is named by the new `format.businessYear(startYear, startMonth)`: "2026" when it starts in January, "2025/26" otherwise; the application can replace it per language. The trigger names a whole month ("March 2026", `format.monthName` and the year), a whole quarter (`messages['period.quarter'](quarter, yearName)`, English "Q1 2025/26"), a whole business year, one day, or "start – end". Quarters follow `quarterBasis`: 'business' (default) counts Q1 from the business year's first month (a July start: Q1 = Jul–Sep, Q2 = Oct–Dec, Q3 = Jan–Mar, Q4 = Apr–Jun); 'calendar' always starts Q1 in January, for tax periods such as quarterly VAT. New messages: `period.quarter`, the eight preset names, `period.clear`, `period.all`, `period.customRange`.
- **2026-09-28 — Values taken from Mantine defaults (P2.3c).** Where the owner's description gives no value: the small preset buttons are Mantine Button size 'xs' (`Button.css`: 30px high — A.4's small control — 14px horizontal padding, 12px text); the caption is Mantine Menu.label (`Menu.css`: 12px, weight 600, dimmed, padding 5px 12px); the presets column has the Menu dropdown's 4px padding, the calendar side the Popover's 12px 16px; the divider is Menu.divider's 1px line with 4px margins.
- **2026-09-28 — A fresh calendar on every opening (P2.3b correction).** Reopened quickly, a closing popover is still mounted (it fades out for 150ms), and its calendar kept the month it last showed. Each field counts its openings and keys the calendar by that count (`useCalendarOpen`), so it always opens on the chosen month, else on today's.
- **2026-09-28 — MonthField (P2.3d, decided by the owner: the old AccountingPeriodSelect, a month grid).** One month; the value is YYYY-MM and a hidden input submits it. The look is Mantine's MonthPickerInput, the owner having given only "a month grid". The arrows move between months, left and right measured from the leading edge (Appendix B.7), up and down by a row of three, and cross into the previous or next year; one month is in the tab order (roving tab index), and Enter or Space chooses. The grid opens on the chosen month, else on the provider's `today`, with the focus on that month; closing returns the focus to the trigger. A button cannot be `aria-invalid` (`jsx-a11y/role-supports-aria-props`), so the trigger of MonthField and PeriodField shows an error with the danger border, and the message is linked with `aria-describedby`. New messages: `calendar.previousYear`, `calendar.nextYear`.
- **2026-09-28 — Values taken from Mantine defaults (P2.3d).** `@mantine/dates` 9.6.2: the trigger is a button drawn as an input (`PickerInputBase`: Input 'sm', text at the start, placeholder in the placeholder colour), without an icon; the dropdown opens under it at its start; the header has 36px previous and next controls with the year between them, weight 600, 10px above the grid (`CalendarHeader`); twelve months in four rows of three (`MonthsList`, `getMonthsData`), each a `PickerControl` 36px high and 36 × 7 / 3 + 1.5 = 85.5px wide, 13px, radius md, `surface.hover` on hover, `brand.solid` when chosen, 0.5px around each (`withCellSpacing`); short month names in the grid (`monthsListFormat` "MMM"), the long name and the year as each month's accessible name.
- **2026-10-01 — List items are border-box (P3.2a).** ComboboxField's and MultiSelectField's options are full width with padding but carry no `data-slot`, so without a reset they were content-box and wider than their list by the padding: the list scrolled sideways, and the scrollbar covered the highlighted option, whose fill was cut at the end (the CommandPalette had the same fault earlier). The shared classes `OPTION` and `MENU_ITEM` (and the command item) now say `box-border` themselves, so every list built on them is right in an application without preflight. **A check prevents it coming back:** the story tests (`tests/stories.spec.ts`, `sidewaysOverflow` in `tests/support.ts`) fail when an open listbox, menu, command list or floating content, or any scrolling element inside one, is wider inside than its box (`scrollWidth > clientWidth`), in all four modes.

## Overlays

- **2026-09-28 — P2.4 split.** P2.4 is split into P2.4a (Dialog, Drawer, Popover, Tooltip, DropdownMenu, and the B.8 rules in AGENTS.md D14) and P2.4b (ConfirmDialog, its delete preset, IrreversibleConfirmDialog), each with its own pull request (BUILD-PLAN rule 6).
- **2026-09-28 — The public overlays (P2.4a).** `Dialog` and `Drawer` take `title` (which names them), `description`, content, `actions` (the main action last), an optional `trigger`, controlled or uncontrolled `open`, and `dismissible` (default true; false keeps them open against Escape and a press outside and hides the close button, for while an action runs). The close button's name is `messages['dialog.close']`. `Drawer` slides from `side` 'start' (default, as Mantine's drawer) or 'end'. `Popover` takes a `trigger`, content, an optional `label` and `align` (default 'center', Mantine's 'bottom'). `Tooltip` takes a `label` and one target, which keeps its own accessible name: the tooltip only adds a description (`aria-describedby`), so nothing a user needs lives only in it; `side` top (default, as Mantine), bottom, start or end, the last two following the provider's direction. `DropdownMenu` takes a `trigger` and its entries as data: items (label, `onSelect`, optional icon, shortcut, `disabled`, `destructive`), headings and separators; `align` default 'start' (Mantine 'bottom-start'). A destructive item is drawn as Mantine's Menu.Item with color="red": the text in `status.danger.fg`, `status.danger.bg` under the pointer or the keyboard.
- **2026-09-28 — The menu is not modal.** Radix's dropdown menu is modal by default: it hides the rest of the page with `aria-hidden` while open, and its trigger stayed focusable inside that (axe `aria-hidden-focus`). Mantine's Menu hides nothing, so `DropdownMenu` passes `modal={false}`; the focus still moves into the menu and back to the trigger.
- **2026-09-28 — Buttons pass other attributes through (P1.3 correction).** A Radix trigger (`asChild`) gives its button `aria-expanded`, `aria-haspopup`, `data-state`, event handlers and a ref. `Button`, `IconButton` and `CompactIconButton` read only their own props and dropped the rest, so a Button could not open a dialog or a menu. They now pass every other attribute to the `<button>`; their look, label and type stay their own. The types still offer no `color` or `variant`.
- **2026-09-28 — Overlay stories.** An overlay renders into its provider's container with a fixed position, outside the story's elements, so a `<div dir="rtl" lang="ar">` around it has no effect and a narrow wrapper does not narrow it. The Arabic and Japanese stories of overlays use a nested provider with that locale (`story-frames.tsx`, not part of the package), and the phone-width stories a 390 × 844px frame with a transform, which is the containing block of its fixed descendants. Storybook's viewport setting does not resize the page in the Playwright tests. The dialog's width limit is now `max-width: 90%` instead of `90vw` (P2.1 correction): on a page both mean 90% of the viewport, but `vw` ignored the frame, so the phone-width story showed a 440px dialog in a 390px frame.
- **2026-09-28 — ConfirmDialog is the old ConfirmModal (decided by the owner, P2.4b).** Carried over exactly: the title row is an 18px icon (default TriangleAlert, lucide's AlertTriangle; `icon` replaces it) and the title, bold (700) at 13px, both in the tone's `fg` colour. The tone comes from the action's family — primary → info, verify → info, document → neutral, positive → success, destructive → danger, caution → warning, neutral → neutral — and is warning without an intent or family; `tone` overrides it (`confirmTone`, tested). The text is `text.secondary` at 13px. The buttons sit at the end, 12px apart and 16px under the text: Cancel in the neutral "default" weight (`messages['dialog.cancel']` unless `cancelLabel`), then the confirm button filled (primary emphasis) in the action's family colour with its icon, the main action last. The dialog is centred on the screen with radius lg (12px); otherwise it is the P2.1 dialog (440px, shadow xl, the fade). While confirming — `onConfirm` returned a pending promise, or `loading` — the confirm button shows a loader and is `aria-busy`, Cancel is disabled, the close button is hidden, and neither Escape nor a press outside closes it; it closes when the promise resolves and stays open when it rejects. It is an `alertdialog`. `DeleteConfirmDialog` is the old DeleteConfirmModal preset (named like the other dialogs): the delete intent (danger tone, red button, Trash2), and the title, text and button label from `messages['confirm.deleteTitle' | 'confirm.deleteMessage' | 'confirm.deleteLabel']` unless given. `IrreversibleConfirmDialog` (BUILD-PLAN P2.4) adds a text field labelled `messages['confirm.typeToConfirm'](confirmText)` and enables its button only when the typed text, without spaces at either end, equals `confirmText`.
- **2026-09-28 — The loading button (Mantine Button `loading`).** Button.css and Loader.css 9.6.2: the content keeps its place at opacity 0 (so the button keeps its size and its accessible name; `visibility: hidden` removed the name) and an "oval" loader is centred: 20px (36 / 1.8), a 2.5px ring (size / 8) in the text colour with one quarter open, one turn in 1.2s, linear (`animate-liro-spin`). Mantine also lays a 15% white (light) or black (dark) tint over a loading button; that is a raw colour (AGENTS.md D6) and no meaning has it, so it is left out.
- **2026-09-28 — Local test runs and parallel pages.** On the owner's 16-thread machine Playwright runs 8 workers; with several overlay stories open at once, some stories timed out at 30s, and each passed alone and with 2 workers (as on the CI runner, which has 4 cores). The CI configuration is unchanged; locally, run the overlay stories with `--workers=2`.
- **2026-10-01 — Colours never mix in a confirmation (P3.2a, decided by the owner).** The confirm button always follows the dialog's tone (`confirmFamily`, tested): the action's family when it gives that tone, otherwise the tone's own family — warning → caution, danger → destructive, info → primary, success → positive, neutral → neutral, premium → document. Before, a confirmation without an action showed a warning title over a blue (primary) button, and a `tone` that overrode the action's kept the action's colour. The stories' icons follow Appendix A.6: void is caution with Ban (it showed Send), approve is positive with CheckCheck (it showed Send). A new story shows a tone overriding the action.

## Actions

- **2026-09-28 — P2.7 split.** P2.7 is split into P2.7a (ActionGroup, UnavailableAction), P2.7b (SplitAction) and P2.7c (BulkActionBar), each with its own pull request (BUILD-PLAN rule 6). It builds on P2.4 (menus, tooltips, ConfirmDialog).
- **2026-09-28 — The previous Design System's actions (decided by the owner).** Carried over exactly; everything not listed is the Mantine 9 default with Liro tokens; every physical side becomes logical:
  1. **ActionGroup:** a wrapping row with gap xs (8px), centred vertically, aligned to the END by default (the main action sits at the end of a form, where the eye and the thumb expect it); `align="start"` only when the group sits next to text. The old one did not overflow into a "More" menu; that is added as the plan requires, keeping the main action visible last.
  2. **SplitAction:** the main button and the menu button joined into one control — the main button's end corners square, the menu button's start corners square and overlapping the main button by 1px (margin-inline-start −1px) so default-weight borders do not double; the separator a 1px line on the menu button's start side in `currentColor` at 30%, so it works for every family and weight without a colour token; the menu button of the same family and weight, 8px horizontal padding, ChevronDown 16px, `aria-label` "<more options>: <main label>"; the menu opening bottom-end (mirrored in right-to-left), with an arrow and the "pop" transition; items with a 16px icon; destructive items in the danger fg colour; the main button's icon 15px, as every text button.
  3. **Unavailable action:** the old system showed the reason in a tooltip (240px wide, several lines, with the arrow) that also opens on focus and on touch, since a disabled button sends no mouse events. That tooltip stays, and the visible reason text the plan requires is added.
  4. **BulkActionBar:** appears with a slide-down transition (140ms) when at least one row is selected; a panel with a border, radius md, padding xs, background `brand.subtle`, border colour `border.brand` (neutral since 2026-10-02, P3.6: see "Polish from the owner's review"); at the start a clear-selection icon-only button (cancel intent, its name from the messages), then the count "N selected" at 13px, semibold, `text.brand`, in an `aria-live="polite"` region (the only way a screen reader hears the count change); when the whole result is larger than the selection, a "Select all N" underlined text button (12px, `text.brand`); at the end the actions, small, each can be disabled with a reason, and all disabled while loading; actions that require confirmation open ONE ConfirmDialog for the whole selection, with the count in its text ("Apply to N items?") from the messages; the row wraps on narrow screens.
- **2026-09-28 — Two technical adjustments (P2.7).** The SplitAction separator is drawn as a 1px pseudo-element line on the menu button's inline start, in `currentColor` at 30% opacity, rather than as an inset `box-shadow` with `color-mix()`: the lint rule `liro/no-raw-colors` forbids colour functions in classes (AGENTS.md D6), and a shadow's offset is physical and would not move to the other side in right-to-left. The look is the same. The unavailable action's button is `aria-disabled="true"` rather than `disabled`: a disabled button takes no focus, so its tooltip could not open on focus; `aria-disabled` keeps it focusable, pressing it does nothing, and the button primitive draws `aria-disabled` exactly as `disabled` (no hover or press change).
- **2026-09-28 — ActionGroup and UnavailableAction (P2.7a).** `ActionGroup` takes `actions` as data (key, label, `intent` or `family` + `icon`, `emphasis`, `onClick`, `unavailableReason`) and `align`. Overflow: every action and the "More" button are laid out once invisibly (`aria-hidden`, `inert`, `visibility: hidden`) to measure them; a ResizeObserver on the row decides how many fit (`visibleActions`, tested): all, or the main action and as many before it as fit beside "More" (an IconButton with the `more` intent, named by `messages['action.more']`), the rest going into its menu from the start; the row still wraps when even that does not fit. An unavailable action in the menu is a disabled item. `UnavailableAction` takes the button's intent or family and a `reason` (a string, from the application): the visible text is `messages['action.unavailable'](reason)` ("Unavailable: …", 12px, `text.secondary`, beside the button, wrapping under it on a phone) and is the button's `aria-describedby`; the tooltip shows the reason, 240px wide (`w-60`) and wrapping, on hover, on keyboard focus and on touch (a touch opens it instead of pressing).
- **2026-09-28 — SplitAction (P2.7b).** `SplitAction` takes the main action (`label`, `intent` or `family` + `icon`, `emphasis`, `onClick`, `disabled`, which disables both halves) and the menu's `entries` (as DropdownMenu's). The halves are the button primitive in the same family and weight, joined as the owner specified; the separator is the 1px pseudo-element line of "Two technical adjustments". The chevron button is named `messages['action.moreOptions'](label)` ("More options: Save"). The menu is not modal (as DropdownMenu), opens `bottom` / `end`, and has Mantine's 7px arrow (a triangle 7√2 wide, the overlay surface, bordered on its two slanted sides in `border.default`) and the "pop" transition (`animate-liro-pop-in|out`: from scale 0.9 and 10px lower, transparent; 150ms, the menu's duration). Items take 16px icons.
- **2026-09-28 — Values taken from Mantine defaults (P2.7b).** `@mantine/core` 9.6.2: Popover `arrowSize` 7 and `arrowOffset` 5 (Radix places the arrow at the content's edge by its own rule); transitions.ts `pop` (`scale(.9) translateY(10px)`, opacity 0 → 1, transform-origin centre) with the Popover's duration 150ms; the menu itself is the P2.1 menu (Menu.css).
- **2026-09-28 — BulkActionBar (P2.7c).** `BulkActionBar` takes `count` (it renders nothing below 1), `total` with `onSelectAll`, `onClear`, `actions` (as ActionGroup's, plus `confirm`) and `loading`. As the owner specified: it slides down in 140ms (`animate-liro-slide-down`: Mantine's 'slide-down', from 100% higher and transparent; none with reduced motion); `border.brand` border, radius md, 10px padding, `brand.subtle`; at the start a CompactIconButton with the cancel intent (the old icon-only buttons were Mantine ActionIcons) named `messages['bulk.clear']`, the count `messages['bulk.selected'](count)` (13px, semibold, `text.brand`) in an `aria-live="polite"` region, and `messages['bulk.selectAll'](total)` (12px, `text.brand`, underlined) when the result is larger than the selection; at the end the actions in Mantine's 'xs' size (30px, 14px padding, 12px text and icon) through the shared `ActionButton`, an unavailable one as a small UnavailableAction, all disabled while `loading`. An action asks first when its `confirm` says so, else when its intent confirms (`asksFirst`, tested: delete asks): ONE ConfirmDialog for the selection, titled `messages['bulk.confirmTitle'](count)` ("Apply to 24 items?"), with the action's intent or family and its label on the button; `onClick` may return a promise, which the dialog waits for. The counts in the English messages are grouped as `table.count` ("1,234").
- **2026-09-28 — ActionButton (P2.7c).** ActionGroup, UnavailableAction and BulkActionBar draw an action with one internal `ActionButton` (the button primitive with the action's family, weight, icon and label; `small` is Mantine 'xs'), so a bar's actions can be small while `Button` keeps its one size. UnavailableAction takes `small` for bars.

## Display

- **2026-09-28 — P2.8 split.** P2.8 is split into P2.8a (Card, SectionCard, KeyValueList), P2.8b (PersonAvatar, PersonName), P2.8c (DateText, DateRangeText, DueDate, NumberText, MoneyText) and P2.8d (SettlingValue, with the Playwright test of the plan's "Done when"), each with its own pull request (BUILD-PLAN rule 6). P2.8d adds a test file under `apps/storybook/tests/`, a protected file: the owner merges it.
- **2026-09-28 — The previous Design System's display pieces (decided by the owner).** Carried over exactly; everything not listed is the Mantine 9 default with Liro tokens:
  1. **Card:** radius lg (12px), with a border, padding md; Paper surfaces radius lg. **SectionCard:** the raised surface, `border.default`, radius lg; a header only when there is a title or actions: padding md (sm at the bottom under a description); at the start an optional 18px icon in `text.secondary`, then the title as h4 and an optional description (12px, `text.secondary`) 2px under it; at the end the actions, gap xs, not wrapping; title and actions aligned to the top; a `border.subtle` divider under the header (`withDivider`, on by default); the body padded md with md between its children, or `flush` for no padding (a table).
  2. **KeyValueList:** a grid, one column on phones and `columns` (default 2) from the sm breakpoint, md spacing; each item a label above its value, 2px apart. The label 12px, semibold, `text.secondary`, upper case with the caps letter spacing — but only in scripts with letter case (Latin, Cyrillic, Greek): never upper case or letter-spaced Arabic, Hebrew, Chinese or Japanese (letter spacing breaks Arabic joining). The value 13px, `text.primary`, breaking long words, a block (values may contain badges); an empty value shows "—". `numeric` items take tabular digits; `fullWidth` items span all columns. Loading: skeletons (label 12 × 90px, value 16 × 150px).
  3. **PersonAvatar:** the primary (blue) colour, Mantine's light avatar style, radius xl (neutral since 2026-10-02, P3.6); a photo when given; decorative by default (empty alt, `aria-hidden`) unless `alt` is given. Initials: the first letter of the FIRST and the LAST word ("Ana Marija Jovanović" → AJ), per lesson B.9 — **a deliberate change:** the old code took the first two words.
  4. **DateText:** the date in tabular digits; an empty value "—" in `text.secondary`; an optional tooltip with the weekday and the long date (300ms delay); optionally dimmed (`text.secondary`). **DateRangeText:** "from – to" with an en dash. **DueDate** (with the provider's `today`): settled → a success badge "Settled"; overdue → a danger badge whose TEXT shows the number of days overdue (**a deliberate change:** visible, not only in a tooltip, as the plan asks), the tooltip the due date; due today → a warning badge "Due today"; due within `warningDays` (default 5) → a warning badge "Due in N days" with the date in the tooltip; otherwise the plain date. **NumberText / MoneyText:** tabular digits, an empty value "—" in `text.secondary`. **SettlingValue:** new, as the plan describes (no old equivalent).
- **2026-09-28 — Card, SectionCard, KeyValueList (P2.8a).** `SectionCard` is a `section`; its title's heading level is 4 by default and `headingLevel` fits it into the page's outline; icon and title stand 16px apart (Mantine Group's default gap, which the owner did not change). `KeyValueList` is a description list (`dl`, with `dt` and `dd` in a `div` per item, which HTML allows); an empty value is `null`, `undefined`, `''` or `false` (a zero is a value); while `loading` it is `aria-busy`. Whether a label is upper case is decided by its language in CSS (`styles.css`: `:lang()` of Arabic-script, Hebrew and CJK languages turns `text-transform` and the caps letter spacing off), so it follows the provider's locale or any `lang` attribute around it — the labels of a mixed-language page included.
- **2026-09-28 — PersonAvatar and PersonName (P2.8b).** `initialsOf(name, locale)` (tested) takes the first letter of the first and of the last word — whole code points, upper case in the page's locale (Turkish "i" → "İ") — so "Ana Marija Jovanović" is AJ and a single word gives one letter. `PersonAvatar` takes `name`, `src`, `alt` and `size` ('md' 38px, 'sm' 26px): the P2.1 avatar with the owner's look (`brand.subtle` behind `text.brand`, radius xl); the initials are bold, 16px ('md', Mantine 38 / 2.5) or 12px ('sm', Mantine 26 / 2.5 = 10.4px; A.5's smallest); the photo covers it and the initials stay while it loads or when it fails (Radix). Decorative (`aria-hidden`, empty `alt`) unless `alt` is given, then `role="img"` with that name. `PersonName` takes `name`, `description`, `src` and `withAvatar` (default true); Mantine defaults: a Group 16px apart, the name 13px regular in `text.primary`, the line 12px in `text.secondary`, both truncated with an ellipsis; the 26px avatar beside one line, 38px beside two.
- **2026-09-28 — DateText, DateRangeText, DueDate, NumberText, MoneyText (P2.8c).** Every value is formatted only by the provider's `format` and has tabular digits; an empty value (`null`, `undefined`, `''`) is "—" in `text.secondary`. `DateText` is a `time` element with the ISO date in `dateTime`; `withWeekday` adds a tooltip with `format.dateLong(value)` (**new in LiroFormat**: the weekday and the date in words, Gregorian with Latin digits like `format.date`, e.g. "Monday, September 28, 2026"); `dimmed` writes it in `text.secondary`. `DateRangeText` joins two DateTexts with " – ". `DueDate` takes `value`, `settled` and `warningDays` (default 5) and compares with the provider's `today` (`dueState` and `daysBetween`, tested, a `today` other than the device's included): a StatusBadge with `messages['due.settled' | 'due.overdue'(days) | 'due.today' | 'due.inDays'(days)]`, the overdue days in the badge's own text (the owner's deliberate change); the badges carry the due date in a tooltip, on a plain button so the keyboard reaches it (a focusable `span` is not allowed by `jsx-a11y/no-noninteractive-tabindex`). `NumberText` (`value`, `decimals`) and `MoneyText` (`value`, `currency`, `decimals`, default `format.moneyDecimals`) are `bdi` elements, so in right-to-left text the minus sign and the currency stay where the number puts them; an amount never wraps apart from its currency (the no-break space of `format.money`, and `white-space: nowrap`).
- **2026-09-28 — SettlingValue (P2.8d).** As the plan describes: the last confirmed value stays unchanged while `pending` (the application passes only confirmed values: never a blank, a spinner instead of the number, or an intermediate value); tabular digits and a reserved width — the number keeps at least the widest width it has had, or `reserveChars` from the start, aligned to its end — so nothing reflows, in either direction; the new value is announced once, politely, when it settles, in a live region of its own (the visible number is not live, so typing does not make it chatter; the first value on the page is not announced); `unavailableText` when no value can be shown, "—" when there is none. The value is formatted by `format.money` (with `currency`) or `format.number`, never rounded. **The indicator (decided by the owner):** a small dot (6px) in `text.tertiary` at the number's start, in a slot that is always reserved, visible or not, so showing or hiding it never moves the number; shown only when the value has been pending for more than 300ms (`SETTLING_DELAY`), so fast answers show no dot and nothing flickers; pulsing (the skeleton's 1500ms pulse), shown statically with `prefers-reduced-motion`; decorative (`aria-hidden`) — screen readers hear only the settled value.
- **2026-09-28 — The SettlingValue test (P2.8d, protected).** `apps/storybook/tests/settling-value.spec.ts` opens the "Slow updates and fast typing" story in left-to-right and right-to-left, types two quantities key by key, and proves that the value's box does not move by a pixel while pending and after settling (the dot appears in its reserved slot), and that the live region changes exactly once per settle (two settles, two announcements, nothing in between). It runs with the story tests: `test:stories` in `apps/storybook/package.json` names it next to `stories.spec.ts`, so CI runs it without a workflow change.

## Feedback

- **2026-09-28 — P2.5 split.** P2.5 is split into P2.5a (Toast), P2.5b (Alert, Banner, EmptyState, ErrorState) and P2.5c (Skeleton, ProgressBar, Stepper), each with its own pull request (BUILD-PLAN rule 6).
- **2026-09-28 — The previous Design System's feedback components (decided by the owner).** Carried over exactly; everything not listed is the Mantine 9 default with Liro tokens:
  1. **Toast** (the old `notice` API: success, info, warning, error, loading, update): bottom right (the other side in right-to-left), at most 4 visible; radius md, with a border; an 18px icon coloured by family — success positive (CheckCircle2), info primary (Info), warning caution (AlertTriangle), error destructive (CircleX); closes by itself after 3500ms (success), 4000ms (info), 6000ms (warning), never for an error; loading in the primary colour with a spinner, no close button, never closing by itself, and `update(id, …)` turns it into success, warning or error with that kind's icon, colour, closing and a close button; an optional title, a required message.
  2. **Alert and Banner:** Mantine Alert with radius md and the "light" variant (the tone's subtle background, text and icon in the tone's colour).
  3. **EmptyState** (the old one was built on Mantine's EmptyState): three variants with lucide icons at stroke width 1.5 — empty: Inbox; no results: SearchX; error: CloudAlert; the error state never shares an icon with the empty state (an empty list and a failed load need different reactions). The default title and description of each variant come from the provider's messages and can be replaced. An optional action: one button, neutral "default" weight, small (xs). `compact`: Mantine size sm with a 24px icon; normal: size md with a 32px icon. ErrorState adds the case-id slot and the "report a problem" action slot of the plan.
  4. **Stepper:** size sm, step icons 32px, the primary (blue) colour. **ProgressBar:** size sm, fully rounded ends (9999px), filling from the start side, so it flips in right-to-left. **Skeleton:** radius md.
- **2026-09-28 — Toast (P2.5a).** `notice.success | info | warning | error | loading(message, { title, id })` returns the toast's id; `notice.update(id, kind, message, { title })` replaces it with that kind's look and time; `notice.dismiss(id?)`. `<Toaster />` is placed once inside LiroProvider (not by the provider itself, so nested providers do not show every toast twice). On sonner (`toast.custom` with our own view, `unstyled`): position bottom-right, or bottom-left when the provider's direction is right-to-left; `visibleToasts` 4; `expand`, so toasts stand in a list as Mantine's, not a pile; the region's name is `messages['notice.region']` and the close button's `messages['notice.close']`; z-index the toast layer (A.4). Swiping a toast away is sonner's; the close button is the alternative that needs no dragging (WCAG 2.5.7), except on loading, which the application updates. **Role:** Mantine gives every notification `role="alert"`; sonner already announces new toasts in a polite live region, so errors and warnings keep `alert` and the others take `status` — `alert` everywhere would announce twice and interrupt the user for a success.
- **2026-09-28 — Values taken from Mantine defaults (P2.5a).** `@mantine/core` 9.6.2 Notification.css and Notification defaults: the overlay surface (Mantine white / dark-6), border 1px `border.default` (Mantine gray-3 / dark-4), shadow lg, radius md; padding 10px (spacing xs), 22px at the inline start; the icon in a 28px circle filled with the family's colour, the icon white (`text.onAccent`), 16px before the text; the loader 28px in the primary colour (oval: a 3.5px ring, 28 / 8, with one quarter open, `border.brand`); title 13px, weight 600, `text.primary`, 2px above the message; the message 13px, `text.primary`, or `text.secondary` under a title; the close button 28px with a 16px icon (CompactIconButton look), 10px after the text. `@mantine/notifications` 9.6.2: 440px wide (`containerWidth`), 16px from the corner (spacing md) and 16px apart, position `bottom-right` (the owner's too).
- **2026-09-28 — Alert and Banner (P2.5b).** `Alert` takes `tone` (info, success, warning, danger), an optional `title`, the message, an optional `icon` (default by tone, as the toasts: Info, CircleCheck, TriangleAlert, CircleX) and `onClose`, which makes it dismissible. Mantine Alert, light variant (owner): the tone's `bg`, a 1px transparent border, radius md, 16px padding; the 20px icon and the title in the tone's `fg`, 16px between icon and text; the title 13px bold; the message 13px in `text.primary` (Mantine's light variant writes the message in the text colour, only the title and icon in the alert's colour); 10px between title and message. Warnings and dangers are `role="alert"`, information and success `role="status"` (as the toasts; Mantine gives every alert `role="alert"`). **Departure:** Mantine's close button is 16px; ours is 24px with the same 16px icon and no background, in the tone's colour (the minimum target wins, as for the pill). `Banner` is the same look in one row across its container — icon, the title in bold before the message, `actions` at the end, the close button — for something about the whole page or application (offline, a trial ending); its placements in the shell are P5.3.
- **2026-09-28 — EmptyState and ErrorState (P2.5b).** `EmptyState` takes `variant` ('empty' default, 'no-results', 'error'), `title`, `description`, `icon` (each defaulting to the variant's), one `action` (`label`, `onClick`, optional `icon`) and `compact`. Mantine EmptyState, size md or sm, align center: a centred column, gaps 10px (8px compact); the icon (32px, 24px compact; the owner's sizes replace Mantine's indicator) at stroke width 1.5 in `text.secondary` (Mantine's dimmed); the title 16px (14px compact), weight 600, line height 1.3, `text.primary`; the description 13px, line height 1.55, `text.secondary`, at most 32rem; the action a Mantine 'xs' button (30px, 14px padding, 12px text) in the neutral "default" weight.
