Below 62em (`useBelowMd`, the settled viewport store of `usePhone` made into a small factory) the list; choosing an item shows its detail full width with "Back to list" (back intent) and "Next item" (next intent, secondary). **Rows (the old WorklistItem):** title sm semibold, subtitle xs `text.secondary`, the one deciding figure at the end (sm medium, tabular) with the status badge under it, optional inline actions (Approve / Reject) under the text; rows separated by `border.subtle`, padding sm by md (12px 16px); the chosen row `surface.selected` with the 3px `border.selected` bar at its start (the neutral selection, D17), `aria-current`; the row is a button (Enter or Space chooses), the inline actions stand outside it. The detail pane is optional; `empty` holds an EmptyState.
  - **Status and family colours on a selection (decided by the owner).** `contrast.ts` now also measures every tone's text straight on every surface, on `surface.selected` over every surface, every tone's badge (fg on bg) over a selection, and every family's text on a selection as a subtle and as a light button, in both themes (914 checks). Light passes everywhere. In dark, five pairs fell below 4.5:1 over the selection (#363636): danger fg 4.40 (badge 4.18), premium badge 4.31, the primary light button 4.19, the document light button 4.44, destructive 4.40 (light button 4.09). **On a selected row or card these take the next lighter shade — the one their hover already uses:** danger and destructive red 2, premium and document violet 2, primary blue 3 (`ON_SELECTED` in `tokens.ts`; lowest after the change 5.15). A selected element says so with `data-liro-surface="selected"` (DataTable rows, phone cards, WorklistPage rows), and `tokens.css` redeclares those variables inside it in the dark theme, so every utility that reads them follows. Recorded in Appendix A.2. With it, the chosen worklist row shows its inline actions again (a first version had hidden them).

StatusBadge's label now takes its direction from its own text (`TEXT_ISOLATE`, the P3.6 rule), and DateText's `time` element is `dir="auto"`, as NumberText's and MoneyText's `bdi`: a date of Latin digits has no strong character and is laid out left to right, and an Arabic date (Intl writes it with right-to-left marks) stays right to left. Table cells had hidden the date fault, because they are isolated themselves (P3.6). The right-to-left baselines of the badges and dates change.

- **2026-10-06 — Detail and record form pages (P4.4, decided by the owner).**
  1. **Key figures (`KeyFigures`, new, exported):** two to four values under the title; the label xs (12px) `text.secondary` above, the value 20px (xl) semibold with tabular digits, 2px apart; 32px (xl) between items; no frame, no card, no separators; colour only for a state (`tone`: danger, warning, success, info — "Overdue 3 days" in `status.danger.fg`); two columns on phones (`layout`). A description list.
  2. **Section bar (`SectionBar`, new, exported):** like the module tabs — a 40px row of links, start-aligned, under the page header, sticky under the AppShell's sticky top (`--liro-shell-top`); `surface.page` with a 1px `border.default` line below; the current section with the 2px blue line; a press scrolls to the section (smoothly unless the user reduces motion) and moves the focus there; the current one follows the scroll (`currentSection`, tested: the last section whose top has passed the bar). A `nav` landmark named `messages['page.sections']`, the current link `aria-current="location"`. Sections keep a scroll margin of the shell and the bar (`SECTION_SCROLL_MARGIN`), so nothing reached by the bar or the keyboard hides under them (WCAG 2.4.11). DetailPage shows it with `sectionBar` (default off); forms keep their tabs. **Two details found while testing:** the bar listens to every scroll on the page (on `document`, in the capture phase), because the scrolling element may be an application's container rather than the window (scroll events of an element do not bubble — in Storybook the window does not scroll); and a pressed section stays current until its scroll ends (`scrollend`, else 1 second), so the bar does not run through the sections it passes.
  3. **Side column:** 300px from lg (75em), the old form value, for both DetailPage and RecordFormPage (one rule); 24px (lg) from the content; below 75em it stands under the content, never hidden.

It is PageHeader's `back` (with `status` after the title and a `subtitle` line under it, aligned with the title); `BackButton` is exported.

Page padding 24px (16px on phones) and the content's maximum width, as ListPage. **Without an owner's value:** 16px between the header and the key figures and between sections, 12px between the back button, the title and the status. **DataTable `inCard` tightened (found in the baseline review):** in a card, the reserved loader slot now stands right above the table without a gap of its own — the card's header or the FilterBar already gives the room — so a flush SectionCard no longer showed about 42px of empty space above a table; the parts under the table keep 12px from it. The slot is still always reserved, so the table never moves.

- **2026-10-06 — Links keep their colour in every state (P4.4, the owner's bug report).** The header lockup showed the browser's visited purple. **Cause, found:** a local `@veljaos/tokens` build from before `text.logo` existed — the `text-logo` utility then has no rule, and the browser's own link colours apply (CI builds afresh, so its images were right; after a pull, run `pnpm build`, AGENTS.md). **Fix, as the owner asked:** every link-styled element now names its colour for each state — the lockup (`text-logo` with `visited:`, `hover:`, `active:`, `focus:`, never underlined), breadcrumb links (`text-link`), module tabs and section-bar links (`text-primary`), launchpad cards (`text-primary`), the back button (the neutral family's text) and the skip link. QuickPreview's "Open" is a button, not a link; the status pages (P4.7) will follow the same rule. **Limit:** these are utilities in Tailwind's layer, so an application's own unlayered `a:visited` rule would still win; the Core's styles must not colour links globally. **Story test:** "BrandLockup / Visited link" links to the page it is on (always visited); browsers hide a visited link's colour from scripts, so its play function checks that the stylesheet has the explicit `:visited` rule and that the link's colour is the logo's, and its visual baseline shows what the browser draws for the visited state.

- **2026-10-06 — Document page (P4.5, decided by the owner).** `DocumentPage` — the most important template — and its new pieces `LifecycleBar`, `DocumentTotals` and `SidePanels` (all exported).
  1. **Lifecycle bar (dots and lines):** above the header, one 32px row; each step a 16px dot and its name (13px), 24px 1px `border.default` lines between them, 8px on each side. Completed: a neutral dot (`surface.sunken`, `border.strong` ring) with a 10px check, the name `text.secondary` (and "Completed" for assistive technology). Current: a `brand.solid` dot, the name semibold in `text.brand`, `aria-current="step"`. Future: an empty `border.strong` ring, the name `text.tertiary`. Error (`error` on a step, e.g. "Rejected by SEF"): a `status.danger.solid` dot with a white X, the name semibold in `status.danger.fg`; its short reason in a tooltip on hover and focus (the name is a button, so the keyboard reaches it) and in its accessible name. Steps from the application; `lifecycleState` tested. **Phones:** one line, "Step 3 of 4: Sent to SEF" (`messages['lifecycle.step']`), with the current dot.
  2. **Side panels — both kinds of collapsing:** each panel's header is one button (the name sm semibold, the count xs `text.secondary`, a 14px ChevronDown at the end that turns when closed) that collapses the panel to its header row; and a subtle neutral "Hide panels" / "Show panels" button (PanelRight, mirrored in right-to-left) in the document header hides the whole 300px column, the document then taking the full width. Below 75em and on phones the panels stand under the document and the whole-column button is not shown. Both states are the application's (`panelsOpen` / `onPanelsOpenChange`, `panelsHidden` / `onPanelsHiddenChange`), so the Core can remember them. **Each panel has 16px (md) on all four sides**, its content aligned with the header's text, the same space above the first row and below the last (the owner's review). A closed panel's content is not mounted.
  3. **Totals:** an end-aligned block, 320px (the full width on phones), no card; rows from the application in any number (tax bases and VAT per rate, exempt, advance deducted …), label and amount 13px, 8px apart, the label `text.secondary`, the amount a SettlingValue with tabular digits; a row may start a group with a thin `border.subtle` line above; only the final row is large (16px semibold) under a 1px `border.strong` line. **Label and amount share a baseline** (the owner's review: "Tax base 20%" sat higher): the amount's text gives the baseline and SettlingValue's dot slot is centred; a long label wraps and its amount stays on the first line's baseline. Nothing is computed (D4).
  4. **Counterparty only** (the user's own company is in the AppShell header): a label from the application ("Customer" on sales, "Supplier" on purchases) in xs `text.secondary`, the name sm semibold, the tax number and the address xs `text.secondary`; then the key figures.
  - **The lines:** in one card, edge to edge, **without a title by default** (the owner's review: the column headers say what it is; `linesTitle` is optional), the table header at the card's top, the totals under them inside the same card. DataTable in a card (`inCard`) now reserves its refetch-loader slot only when the application passes `loading` or an export slot; otherwise there is nothing above the table. **Phone cards in a card** (found in the baseline review: ListPage and DocumentPage on phones showed about 42px of empty space above the first card): a DataTable `inCard` in the cards layout keeps its cards 16px inside the card's edges itself, so ListPage no longer pads its phone list and the application passes `inCard` in both layouts. **The tax number line** takes its direction from its content and keeps the page's start side (TEXT_DIRECTION, D15); with `dir="auto"` alone it moved to the left edge of a right-to-left page. Further sections (payment terms, notes) as SectionCards; the side column and its rule as DetailPage's.
  - **Messages:** `lifecycle.step`, `document.hidePanels`, `document.showPanels`, `document.panels`.

- **2026-10-06 — Report, dashboard and settings (P4.6, decided by the owner).** `ReportPage`, `DashboardPage`, `SettingsPage`, and the new `StatCard` and charts (`BarChart`, `LineChart`, `AreaChart`, `DonutChart`), all exported.
  1. **Chart colours, restrained:** one blue and greys. The main series is `chart.main` (`#0078D4`, blue 4 in dark), a comparison series `chart.comparison` (grey 6 / grey 5) and the rest `chart.other` (grey 8 / grey 3); a series (or a donut slice) takes a status tone only when it means a state ("Over 30 days" in danger). `palette="categorical"` is opt-in, never the default, for really distinct categories: five hues in a fixed order, never cycled — `chart.category1`–`5` = blue 6 / blue 5, orange 7 / orange 6, violet 8 / violet 5, green 7 / green 5, teal 5 / teal 5 — and such a chart always shows its legend. **Validated** with the dataviz palette validator (lightness band, chroma, colour-vision-deficiency separation of adjacent pairs, normal vision, contrast) against the raised surface in both themes: dark passes every check; light passes with one contrast warning, teal 5 at 2.8:1 against white. The warning is covered as the validator requires — the legend is always shown and every value is in the "Show as table" view — and `contrast.ts` records it as the one chart mark measured at 2.8 instead of 3:1 (WCAG 1.4.11; all other chart marks are measured at 3:1, now 934 checks). The tokens are in Appendix A.2 (`chart` group).
  - **Marks (the dataviz rules):** bars at most 24px with 4px rounded ends at the data, square at the baseline, 2px apart; 2px lines; an area a 10% wash; a 1px `border.subtle` hairline grid, no vertical lines; axis text 12px `text.tertiary`; no animation; no second value axis, ever. A donut has 2px separators in the surface colour and direct labels (the name and the share). Text never wears the series colour: a swatch beside it carries the identity. A legend for two or more series; one series is named by the title.
  - **On Recharts directly**, as shadcn/ui's Chart is; its wrapper (`ChartContainer`, its tooltip and legend) is not copied — the card, legend, tooltip and table are our own, on tokens.
  - **Each chart is a card** (radius lg, `border.default`, padding md) with its title (h3) and a line under it, and a 28px "Show as table" / "Show as chart" button at the end of its header: the same values as a table, so nothing needs hovering or seeing colour; the tooltip on hover adds nothing the table does not have. In right-to-left the category axis runs from the right and the value axis stands at the right.
  - **Drawn once the fonts are ready** (found in the visual tests: the value axis was 2px wider in one run than in the next): Recharts measures the axis labels once, as it draws, so a chart drawn before the web font arrived kept the fallback font's width. The plot keeps its height and is drawn when `document.fonts` is ready, and again when more fonts load; without a font API (a server, jsdom) it is drawn at once. The stories' `settle` waits for the fonts and two frames.
  - **Rule D4 for charts:** values arrive as decimal strings and every value a person reads (tooltip, table, donut labels) is formatted from the string through the provider's `format`. To place a mark the string becomes a JavaScript number — drawing is the one place where that is allowed, because nothing drawn is read back, added or shown; the axis ticks are the scale's round steps, formatted by `format.number`. A donut's shares come from the application, never computed.
  2. **StatCard:** a card (radius lg, `border.default`, padding md, no shadow, no decorative icon): the label xs `text.secondary`; the value 24px semibold, tabular; under it the change with a 14px arrow, green or red only when the application says it is good or bad (`sentiment`), otherwise `text.secondary`; the comparison ("vs September 2025") in xs `text.secondary`; an optional 32px grey sparkline (`text.tertiary`, 2px, no axes; `sparklinePoints`, tested). Loading: a 104px skeleton. **DashboardPage:** the title and the page's actions, the StatCards in a row (one column on phones, two from 36em, four from 62em, 16px apart), then the charts in two columns from 62em, 16px apart (a chart may span both).
  3. **SettingsPage:** sections as cards with a title (sm semibold `text.secondary`); each setting a row — the name (13px semibold) and its description (xs `text.secondary`) at the start, the control at the end, a `border.subtle` line between rows, padding 12px by 16px; on phones the control stands under the description. **No Save button:** a setting saves as it changes (the application's work); the row then shows "Saved" with a check in `status.success.fg` (`role="status"`), or the error in `status.danger.fg` with its icon (`role="alert"`). Several groups are page-level tabs (centred, D16), never a side navigation. **Without an owner's value:** the page's width is at most 960px (`max-w-240`) — rows wider than that put the control too far from its name.
  4. **ReportPage:** the title and the export at the end of the header; the parameters in one row in a card, bottom-aligned, "Run report" (primary, with the Play icon) at the end, stacked with a full-width button on phones; after a run they collapse into one line ("Period: 01.01.–30.09.2026. · Account: 2040 · Edit"), the result taking the room; "Edit" (a link-styled button, `aria-expanded`) opens them again. Controlled with `parametersOpen` / `onParametersOpenChange`, or open until the first run. The result under it: a DataTable in a flush card (`inCard`), a chart, or an EmptyState before the first run.
  - **`hideLabel` on every field** (FieldBaseProps): the label stays for assistive technology only, where the row around the field already names it (a setting row); description and error stay visible.
  - **Messages:** `chart.showTable`, `chart.showChart`, `report.run`, `report.edit`, `report.parameters`, `settings.saved`.

- **2026-10-06 — The design quality bar of Phase 4 (owner; written down here on 2026-10-07, P4.7).** It applies to every template and example screen, and every step report lists it with yes or no per template. **Forbidden** (they make a screen look generated): cards and shadows around everything — data that belongs in a table or a list goes in one; decorative icons next to every heading or item — icons only where they carry meaning (actions, status, navigation); UPPERCASE micro-labels, gradients, glassmorphism, colourful backgrounds, emoji; centred marketing-style layouts — business screens are start-aligned; filler text ("Here you can manage your invoices"), lorem ipsum, "John Doe", round fake numbers. **Required:** hierarchy through type size and weight, not colour or boxes; information density suited to accounting work (Microsoft Business Central and SAP Fiori, not generic dashboard templates); one alignment grid, spacing only from tokens; colour only where it means something (status, action, error), everything else neutral; realistic example data — Serbian company and person names, PIB numbers, amounts like 12.345,60, sensible dates, real statuses — with the interface text in English (the Core translates); short, specific texts ("Issue invoice", not "Click here to issue a new invoice").

- **2026-10-07 — Status pages and AuthShell (P4.7, decided by the owner).**
  1. **`StatusPage`** (new, exported) for seven kinds: `unauthenticated` (401), `planRequired` (402), `forbidden` (403), `notFound` (404), `error` (500), `maintenance`, `suspended` — the one place where centring is right. Full height (`min-h-dvh`) on `surface.raised`; a centred column at most 440px, 24px (lg) apart: the large lockup (24px), an 84 × 84px square (radius xl) filled with the tone's `solid` with a 36px icon (stroke 1.75) in `text.onAccent`, the text group, the buttons. **Tone and icon (owner, from the old system):** 401 warning LogIn, 402 warning CreditCard, 403 warning Lock, 404 neutral FileQuestionMark, 500 danger ServerCrash, maintenance warning Construction, suspended danger Ban — blue never fills a large surface (D17), and a session ending is not a failure. The icon is decorative (`aria-hidden`): the title says it. **The line above the title** (sm, bold, the brand face, `text.secondary`, never upper case) is the bare HTTP code by default ("404": no word "Error" — 401 and 403 are not errors, and a number needs no translation); maintenance and suspension have none; `eyebrow` replaces it and `null` removes it. **The title** is the page's only heading, so an h1, drawn in the h2 size (20px); the description sm `text.secondary`; for an error the case number ("Case number:", `messages['empty.caseId']`, monospace, selectable, left to right). Titles and descriptions default to `messages` (`status.<kind>Title`, `status.<kind>Description`), the application may pass its own. **Actions:** at most two, full width in a column at most 280px: the main one filled (primary family), the other in the default look (neutral, raised with a border); with `href` they are links (`linkComponent`) drawn as buttons with their colours named for every link state (P4.4: `enabled:` hovers do not apply to links). **Without an owner's value:** 8px between the stacked buttons and between the lines of the text group; 48px above and below the column, 16px at its sides. `statusLook` and `STATUS_KINDS` are exported for the Core's routing; tested.
  2. **`AuthShell`** (new, exported): the page `surface.sunken`; the text lockup (24px, with the product name also on phones) centred above a 420px card — `surface.raised`, a 1px `border.default` border, radius lg, padding xl (32px), the xs shadow (the owner: at most a subtle one); in the card the title (an h1 in the h2 size) and an optional description (4px under it), then the flow (`children`, 24px under the title); under the card, centred, the `footer` in xs `text.tertiary` (language, terms; the application's links name their own colours). **Phones:** the card is the page — `surface.raised`, no border or shadow, full width with 16px (md) side padding, the lockup above. No illustration, no side image.
  3. **SettingsPage start-aligned (owner):** the 960px column now stands at the page's start, aligned with the header like every other page, and so do its tabs: `Tabs` has a new `align` ('center' by default, page-level tabs as D16; 'start' where the tabs head a start-aligned column). AGENTS.md D16 names the exception.
  4. **Dashboard: no orphan cells (owner).** The "Receivables by age" donut no longer stands alone in its row: a horizontal bar chart "Top 5 customers by revenue" (Serbian companies, thousands of RSD) stands beside it — `BarChart` has a new `orientation` ('horizontal': categories down the start side, bars along the reading direction, rounded at the data end, the value axis below and reversed in right-to-left). The donut is drawn smaller in its card (inner 50%, outer 66% of the plot, was 58% / 78%), leaving room for the labels outside.
  - **Messages:** `status.*Title`, `status.*Description` for the seven kinds.
  - **Favicon:** the three small-size variants the owner asked for are in the P4.7 report; the chosen one goes into P4.7b (a protected-file pull request: Storybook's favicon URL gets `?v=N`).

- **2026-10-07 — The owner's small favicon (P4.7b, replaces "The tab favicon" of P4.0).** Neither the downscaled sphere of P4.0 nor the three pixel-grid variants shown in the P4.7 report kept the mark recognisable, so the owner drew the small mark from the logo's own rule — a centre dot and rings, the dots larger toward the bottom-left and smaller toward the top-right. His files are copied as they are (not redrawn, not optimised) into `@veljaos/tokens/brand/web/`: `favicon.svg` (viewBox −200 −200 400 400, #0078D4, #3EACEB under `prefers-color-scheme: dark`), `favicon.ico` (16, 32, 48), and the new `favicon-16.png` and `favicon-32.png`; `brand.test.ts` lists and measures them. The full sphere stays for 48px and up (`apple-touch-icon.png`, the manifest's icons). **Storybook:** `managerHead` writes the two links itself — `favicon.ico?v=2` (sizes 48x48) as the fallback, then `favicon.svg?v=2` (type image/svg+xml), which browsers that draw SVG take — so `main.ts` no longer names `favicon`. With an icon link in its head Storybook writes none of its own, and with two links it adds no status dot to the icon. **The version (`?v=2`)** makes a browser fetch a changed icon at once instead of its cached one; raise it whenever the icon changes. The Core should do the same in its page head.
- **2026-10-07 — Charts catalogue (P4.7a, the owner's rules).** The owner asked that the charts be built on shadcn/ui's Chart: the P4.6 charts used Recharts directly (as shadcn's Chart does) without its wrapper, and the owner chose to adopt it.
  1. **The primitive.** shadcn's `chart.tsx` (registry style `radix-vega`, fetched 2026-10-07; its registry names Recharts 3.8, the repository keeps 3.10.1) is copied into `primitives/chart.tsx` (not exported, D10) and adapted: a config entry's `color` is a Liro meaning set as `--color-<key>` on the container (the per-theme `<style>` block is gone; the tokens switch themes); the container has a height and draws once the fonts are ready (the P4.6 measuring fix); the plot's text is laid out left to right (`[direction:ltr]`; the charts mirror their axes); Recharts' colours are replaced by tokens (axis text text.tertiary, grid border.subtle, cursor surface.hover / border.strong, the focus ring on the focusable surface); the tooltip and the legend take ready text — never `toLocaleString` on a number (D4) — with a dot, line, dashed or no indicator and an optional total row; the legend is a list in the page's direction outside the plot, so its order follows the reading direction.
  2. **`@veljaos/ui/charts`, a subpath of its own** (tsup entry `charts`, `exports["./charts"]`): `AreaChart`, `BarChart`, `LineChart`, `PieChart`, `DonutChart`, `RadarChart`, `RadialChart`, `ChartSeriesToggle` and the helpers. **The root `@veljaos/ui` no longer exports the charts** (an alpha breaking change, stated in the changeset; no application used them, and the owner agreed). Only `dist/charts.js` names Recharts — `index.js` and the shared chunks do not — so an application without charts never loads it. The consumer-check app imports `BarChart` from the packed subpath (its sources are not a protected file; `check.mjs` is unchanged).
  3. **Variants as props** (every shadcn variant is a story with Serbian data): area `curve` (`monotone` by default, `linear`, `step`) and `stack` (`stacked`, `expanded` to 100% with percent ticks); bar `orientation`, `stack`, `labels` (`end`, `inside`), `activeCategory` (one bar blue, the rest grey), `signTones` (profit green, loss red, both named in the legend), `colorBy="category"` (a categorical hue per bar); line `curve`, `dots`, `labels`; pie `donut`, `labels` (`outside`, `inside`, `none` with the legend), `labelText`, `activeKey` (8px larger), `centre` (the application's total), `outerRing` (the same parts for another period around the first, each keeping its colour — shadcn's "stacked"); radar `dots`, `fill` (false: lines only), `grid`; radial `labels`, `grid`, `centre`, `stacked`, `max` (the goal is the full circle); every chart `legend`, `valueAxis`, `categoryAxis`, `grid`, `tooltip` (`indicator`, `label`, `total`, `defaultCategory`), `controls`, `loading`, `error`, `layout`. **Interactive** charts are the application's: `controls` holds its time-range SelectField and a `ChartSeriesToggle` (each series' name and the application's total; the neutral selection — surface.selected with a 2px border.selected line, never blue); the application filters the data.
  4. **Choices without an owner's value, reported:** the default curve is `monotone`, not shadcn's `natural` — `natural` swings past the values, so a line would show amounts that never occurred. Stacked areas are a 40% wash (a 10% wash made the layers unreadable), still flat. Labels on a coloured mark stand on a small raised tag in text.primary (bars' inside labels, the pie's label list, radial ring names): white on blue 4 measured 2.6:1 in the dark theme, so text never stands on the data colour. Plots: cartesian 240px (200 on phones), pie and radial 240px, radar 280px.
  5. **Bars are drawn with `shape`:** Recharts 3.10 deprecates `Cell`; the shape function also rounds a negative bar at its data end (the bottom), squares stacked parts between each other, and colours a bar by category, sign or highlight.
  6. **Numbers:** values stay decimal strings, numbers only to draw (D4). Axis ticks from 10,000 are written short with the Core's words (`chart.thousands` "12K", `chart.millions`, `chart.billions`; `shortTick`, tested without floating-point noise); a 100% axis in `chart.percent`. A null or unreadable value is a gap in a line or a missing bar, never 0; a chart with no readable value says `chart.noData`.
  7. **Keyboard:** Recharts' accessibility layer is on (P4.6 had it off): the plot is focusable (with a visible focus ring), the arrow keys move between categories and show the tooltip, Enter shows or hides it. Recharts reads a reversed axis, so in right-to-left the arrows still move with the screen (B.7). The plot is a `group` named by the title and described by the description (it was `img`, which may not contain a focusable element). axe passes on every chart story in the four modes (660 story and accessibility tests run locally).
  8. **Motion:** the marks enter in 300ms; none under `prefers-reduced-motion`, on a server, or in a browser driven by automation (`navigator.webdriver`: the story and visual tests), so pictures never depend on timing.
  9. **States:** loading draws a chart-shaped skeleton (columns, bars or a ring) in the plot's height, `aria-busy`, no spinner; an error shows its message (`chart.error` by default) with Retry (the refresh intent); empty says `chart.noData`; the plot keeps its height in every state. "Show as table" appears only when there is something to show.
  10. **Phones:** fewer ticks (3–4, and a wider gap), the header stacked (controls under the title), the legend under the plot; long labels: `orientation="horizontal"` (the docs page says when).
  11. **Pictures of tooltips:** the visual tests take full-page screenshots, which resize the page; Recharts redraws and drops a tooltip shown from the keyboard. The Tooltip stories therefore open with `tooltip.defaultCategory` (Recharts' `defaultIndex`), which survives the redraw; the keyboard is tested in "Line / Interactive".
  12. **"Charts / Choosing a chart"**, a docs page (`apps/storybook/src/choosing-a-chart.mdx`): trend → line or area; categories → bar, horizontal for long labels or more than 7 items; parts of a whole, five or fewer → donut, else bar; radar and radial only for a few scores or progress to a goal, never money over time; one number → StatCard.
  - **Messages:** `chart.noData`, `chart.error`, `chart.retry`, `chart.loading`, `chart.thousands`, `chart.millions`, `chart.billions`, `chart.percent`.
  - **AuthShell story placeholder (owner):** "name@company.rs" instead of a realistic address, which read as a filled value.
  - **Open, waiting for the owner:** the default alignment of `Tabs` (D16 names page-level Tabs, not only the module tabs — told to the owner before any change); a more clearly orange `status.warning.solid` in the light theme (orange 6, white icon 3.18:1; proposed, not changed).

- **2026-10-07 — The owner's P4.7a review (P4.7c).**
  1. **Warning solid (owner).** In the light theme `status.warning.solid` is orange 6 (#E56F01, was orange 7): orange 7 (hue 16°) read like danger's red 7 (hue 357°), so a session ending (401) looked like a failure (500). **Its rule:** the warning solid carries no text — icons only, a white icon at least 3:1 (3.18:1) — and it stands on a raised surface, never straight on the page background (orange 6 on the light page is 2.84:1). Enforced: `contrast.ts` measures the white icon on it and it on the raised surface in both themes (938 checks); `packages/ui/src/tone-usage.test.ts` fails when any `text-status-*-solid` class appears, and when the warning solid is used outside the places where it stands on a raised surface (the status page's square, the charts' marks in their card). Appendix A.2 updated.
  2. **Tabs at the start (owner; AGENTS.md D16 rewritten).** Page-level `Tabs` now stand at the start by default (`align` 'start'; 'center' stays for a list that heads centred content); every tab list inside page content is start-aligned. Only the AppShell's module tabs stay centred (P4.1); they are not the Tabs component. Changed stories: Components/Navigation (Tabs, English in RTL), the internal Tabs primitive's stories are unchanged (the primitive has no alignment), SettingsPage (already start, now by default).
  3. **Believable daily data.** The "Daily sales" stories had sawtooth ramps and Novi Sad at 0 every Sunday. Both stores now keep the same week (weekdays alike, Friday and Saturday stronger, Sunday short hours at about 60%), a few percent of day-to-day noise (a repeatable sequence, so pictures are stable), the first working days of a month stronger, an end-of-season sale week before the school year and the last Friday of September as peaks. The totals in the series toggle are added from the same data in whole paras (the stories play the application).
  4. **Equal tick intervals.** A dense category axis shows at most 8 ticks (4 on phones) at equal intervals ending on the last point (`equalTicks`, tested), never an uneven last step. Lines and areas leave 24px beside the plot on the side without the value axis, so the last label is not cut.
  5. **Curve by density (owner).** Lines and areas are straight segments (`linear`) up to 31 points, with a dot on each value up to 12 points; denser series are `monotone`; never `natural`. "Cash at month end" (the catalogue and the dashboard) is now straight with dots. `defaultCurve`, `LINEAR_MAX_POINTS`, `DOTS_MAX_POINTS`; areas take `dots` too. Written in "Choosing a chart".
  6. **Categorical palette without status hues (owner).** Blue, magenta, purple, teal, indigo — no red, orange or green, which read as danger, warning and success. Values outside the ramps (there is no magenta ramp): light #0078D4, #970E75, #7E6EF2, #179A8E, #334DB0; dark #1499E6, #B75B97, #8475F9, #059488, #5778DF. Found by a search over in-gamut OKLCH candidates per hue family, keeping only orders that pass the dataviz palette validator in both themes (lightness band, chroma floor, colour-blind separation of neighbours, the normal-vision floor) with at least 3:1 on the card: light worst neighbour ΔE 15.5 (colour-blind) / 23.3 (normal), lowest contrast 3.47:1; dark 12.2 / 16.9, 3.66:1. **The owner's teal #038387 fails the validator's chroma floor (0.094 < 0.10: it reads greyish next to blue), so the light teal is #179A8E.** The dark first hue is blue 5 (blue 4 is lighter than the validator's dark band). The light teal-5 exception of P4.6 (2.8:1) is gone: every chart colour is measured at 3:1. Story "Charts / Palette" shows each chart colour with its contrast measured on the card in the story's theme (its play function fails below 3:1).
  7. **Radial "plan by representative" (owner).** With `labels`, each ring's name and value stand at its start ("Jovana Marić **112%**") and every ring is the brand blue on the grey track — the names identify the rings, so no categorical colours. New `percent` writes the values with `format.percent`. The "Simple" story is now one ring (one person's plan).
  8. **Colour per bar** (`colorBy="category"`): the story stays; "Choosing a chart" says to use it only when the same colours link the bars to another chart on the page, otherwise one colour.
  9. **Percentages through the provider (owner).** New `format.percent(value, { decimals, sign })` in `LiroFormat`: the value is the percentage as a decimal string ("62.4"), formatted by the number scheme, never rounded; the sign, the percent sign and the space between them come from the locale's CLDR pattern through Intl (only the pattern — the number is never a JavaScript number): "62,4%" in sr-Latn, "62.4%" in en, "62,4 %" in de; `sign: 'always'` writes "+16,7%". Tested for sr-Latn, en and de. The 100% axis uses it; `messages['chart.percent']` is removed (a breaking change of an alpha type). The stories' shares and changes ("62,4 %", "+16,7 %" were typed by hand) now come from the same Serbian format (`percentText` in `story-frames`).

- **2026-10-07 — Example screens (P4.8, the owner's brief and additions).** Storybook "Examples" (`packages/ui/src/examples/examples.stories.tsx`) shows Liro as it will look: Kvadrat Gradnja d.o.o. on 6 October 2026, **one dataset** (`examples-story-data.ts`) and the screens **linked into one application**. Screens: sign in (AuthShell), home (Launchpad), invoice list (saved views, FilterBar, table, quick preview), invoice F-2026-0412 (lifecycle bar, customer, key figures, lines with three VAT treatments — 20%, 10% and an exempt returnable deposit — totals, advance deducted, side panels), overview (StatCards, charts and the largest open invoices), supplier invoices to approve (worklist with inline Approve / Reject that take the item off the list), an employee record (FormTabs in the form card, sections, the bottom bar once something changes) and a 404 page (any module the examples do not have, such as Banking). Each has a desktop and a phone story; the toolbar gives both themes and both directions.
  - **Navigation through the provider's `linkComponent`** (owner): a small router inside the stories passes its link to `LiroProvider` (the story `ExampleProvider` gained a `linkComponent` option); a link to "#/…" changes the example's route, as the Core's router will. The walk-through: sign in → home → Sales → preview F-2026-0412 → Open → back to the list; home → Overview, Purchasing (approvals), Employees; the lockup goes home; Sign out (user menu) goes to sign in. The story "Walk-through" clicks that path in its play function.
  - **The same figures everywhere (owner):** F-2026-0412 to Panonija Agro d.o.o., total 185.954,00 RSD, amount due 135.954,00 RSD after the advance A-2026-031, appears with these values in the list (and its preview), on its page and in the overview's "Largest open invoices"; the overview's overdue receivables (152.940,00 RSD, 2 invoices) are the overdue invoices of the list; cash and revenue match the charts. The stories add amounts in whole paras, playing the application.
  - **Only the public entry points:** the screens import `@veljaos/ui` (`../index`) and `@veljaos/ui/charts` (`../charts`), never internal modules, so they show what the Core can build. Their classes live in a `*.stories.tsx` file, so nothing of the examples reaches the published CSS.
  - Tested: every example's story test (the walk-through and the same-figures checks) and axe in the four modes, 136 checks locally.
  - **Found in the examples' review, fixed:** DetailPage and RecordFormPage put their side column beside the content from 75em by the viewport, even in the phone layout — in a phone frame inside a wide window the form shrank to one letter per line. The side column now always stands under the content when the page is laid out for phones (tested).

## Fixes from the owner's Storybook review (P4.9)

- **2026-10-08 — How P4.9 was done (owner's brief).** One pull request for all 28 items of the owner's review of Storybook. Each item was fixed as a class: the whole library (components, templates, examples, docs) was searched for the same pattern and every occurrence fixed, and each general rule is written down below. Where the brief left a choice open, the option most consistent with these decisions was taken and recorded in its entry. The plan additions (P5.6 provider sign-in buttons, P5.18 complex and corrective documents, P5.19 catalogs at scale, P5.20 registers and official forms, P5.21 common business processes) are in `BUILD-PLAN.md` as `todo`, not implemented.
- **2026-10-08 — The general rules of P4.9 (owner's review), in one place.**
  1. A number shown to a person goes through the provider's `format` — counts, percentages and amounts; messages that show a number receive it already formatted (`(count, text, …)`), the number kept for plural rules.
  2. A count is never shown without its noun ("5 tasks"), written by whoever knows what it counts.
  3. Money is never rounded on screen: every amount with its decimals (5.684.200,00 RSD).
  4. No cards inside a card: inside a card, repeated items are rows divided by lines; a container sets the space at its edges, its content never adds its own; a card ends with its last row.
  5. A placeholder never repeats its label ("All" for an empty choice filter).
  6. A due date is always shown as the date, its state as small tone text with words, never a second pill.
  7. A reference to another record is a link showing its kind, number and state.
  8. Values the system has decided are shown as values that can be changed, never as empty or pre-filled inputs.
  9. Records are one page, read and edited as a whole (view → Edit → fields, Cancel – Save in a sticky bar); no tabs or side column for a record of normal length.
  10. Button order everywhere: the confirming or main action last — Cancel – Save, Stay – Leave, Reject – Approve (mirrored in right-to-left); one dialog footer rule for every dialog.
  11. Lists and field grids take their columns from their own width, not the screen's; words are never broken inside; on phones the label stands above the value.
  12. A list that can hold thousands is searchable, sectioned and virtualised.
  13. One breadcrumb is not shown; the page title is not repeated where the header or a tab already names it.
  14. Menus for the user's own session are neutral (sign-out is not red).
  15. Dragging uses pointer events (no grey ghost): the item lifts with a shadow token, the others move aside with a motion token (none under reduced motion), a neutral placeholder marks the drop, and every drag has a button or key alternative.
  16. On phones a page's main action stands in the bottom bar within thumb reach; nothing overflows sideways at 360px; a scrolling tab row fades at the edge where more tabs are.
  17. No date followed by a comma in running text: write the word first ("Complete, 30.09.2026.") or split into two values.

- **2026-10-08 — Company switcher at scale (P4.9, the owner's review: an accountant may have 5,000 companies).** `CompanySwitcher` (`templates/company-switcher.tsx`, logic in `company-logic.ts`, tested) replaces the cmdk list of P4.1.
  1. **Data:** `ShellCompanies` gains `pinned` and `recent` (ids, the Core's data: the user's favourites and the recently used ones); `items` are all companies in the application's order for "All companies" (alphabetical is usual). `ShellCompany` gains `status` ({ label, tone }: "Suspended" danger, "Inactive" neutral — a StatusBadge after the name; the company can still be chosen, the Core decides what opens) and `note` (a short text the application writes with its own count and words, "5 tasks"). **`waiting` is removed** (an alpha breaking change) with `messages['shell.waiting']`: the bare "5" and "12" did not say what they counted, and a count needs its noun, which only the application knows.
  2. **Sections:** Pinned, Recent, All companies (`companySections`): each company in one section only, empty sections left out, headings only when there is more than one section (xs semibold text.secondary; for the eye only — `aria-hidden`, since a flat virtualised listbox cannot nest groups; every option carries `aria-setsize` and `aria-posinset`).
  3. **Search** above 7 companies (and always on a phone): name or description (the tax-number line), ignoring case and accents; names are folded once per list (`companyMatcher`), not on every key — 5,000 companies are searched in a few milliseconds (unit test under 200ms). Nothing found: "No company matches “…”." (`shell.noCompany`, `role="status"`). Placeholder "Name or tax number" (`shell.findCompanyHint`).
  4. **Keyboard:** the WAI-ARIA combobox pattern — the focus stays in the search field, `aria-activedescendant` names the highlighted option; ArrowDown/ArrowUp, PageDown/PageUp by ten, Home/End in the list (in the field they move the caret), Enter chooses (`companyKeyTarget`, tested). Without a search field the listbox itself takes the focus and the same keys.
  5. **Virtualised by its own small virtualiser, not TanStack Virtual:** rows have known heights (headings 28px, companies 36px, 48px with a description), so `rowOffsets`, `visibleRows` (binary search) and `scrollToShow` (tested) place only the rows in view and 240px around them — about 15 rows of 5,000. `useVirtualizer` would have needed a new `react-hooks/incompatible-library` exception in `eslint.config.mjs`, a protected file.
  6. **Desktop:** a 320px popover (was 260px: the status badge and the note need room), the list at most 400px high. **Phones:** the user menu has one entry "Switch company: <current>" (Building2), which opens a **full-screen sheet** (the Sheet primitive from the bottom, the full dynamic viewport height, safe-area insets) titled "Companies", the search field at the top taking the focus. The inline list of up to seven companies in the phone menu is gone: one place, one behaviour.
  7. **Command palette:** when `companies` is given, AppShell adds "Switch company…" (`shell.switchCompanyCommand`, an action) to the application's commands; it opens the popover (desktop) or the sheet (phone) once the palette has closed.
  - Story "Templates/AppShell / 5,000 companies" (generated, repeatable Serbian names and PIBs, `manyCompanies` in the story data): its play function checks that fewer than 60 options are in the page, searches and moves with the arrows.

- **2026-10-08 — Notifications (P4.9, the owner's review).** The bell keeps its red dot and no number (P4.1).
  1. **`NotificationsPanel`** (new, exported), what the bell opens: 360px (the screen less 32px on a phone); a header row — "Notifications" sm semibold, the unread count xs text.secondary ("3 unread", `notifications.unreadCount(count, text)`), and "Mark all as read" (a small subtle neutral button) while anything is unread; the recent notifications (the application chooses how many), each a link to its record through `linkComponent`: the unread marker, the title (13px, semibold while unread), the company and the time in xs text.tertiary; "View all" at the bottom, a link to the page. AppShell's popover around it has 8px by 4px padding.
  2. **The unread marker is neutral (D17):** an 8px `surface.inverse` dot and the word "Unread" for assistive technology — a notification is not an action, so no blue; the title's weight says it too.
  3. **`NotificationsPage`** (new, exported): the visible title (no module tab names it), "Notification settings" (a link drawn as a subtle neutral button) and "Mark all as read" in the header; one card (as ListPage): the All / Unread toggle (the saved-view look, the unread count after "Unread"), then a row with the company filter (MultiSelectField `summary`, searchable) and the type filter (the application's types), both "All" while empty; the list grouped by day — "Today", "Yesterday", then the date (`format.date`; not the long date, whose words would be in the tenant's language on an English screen) — each heading an h2. Each notification: the dot, the title as a link (opening calls `onOpen`, which marks it read), an optional second line, the company and the time, and a 28px button that marks it read or unread (Mail / MailOpen, named "Mark as read: <title>"). Empty: "No notifications"; filtered to nothing: "No notification matches these filters" with "Clear filters". Loading: five skeleton rows.
  4. **The page shows what it is given:** the application filters, as DataTable never filters; `filterNotifications`, `groupByDay`, `notificationDay` and `notificationFiltersActive` are exported for an application that filters on the device (tested).
  5. **Days and times:** a notification's `at` is an ISO instant in the tenant's offset ("2026-10-06T09:42:00+02:00"); its date part is its day, compared with the provider's `today`, and its clock part is shown. **New `format.time(isoInstant)`** in `LiroFormat`: the clock time as written in the instant, in the locale's form ("09:42", "9:42 AM"), never shifted to the device's zone (`dateTime` shows the device's) — so a day heading and the times under it always agree.
  - Examples: the bell's panel in every signed-in screen (Milica's six notifications, two unread, links into the example screens), "View all" opens the new "Examples / Notifications" screen (desktop and phone); the walk-through ends there and opens a notification, which goes to its record and leaves one unread. The read state is kept above the screens, as the Core keeps it.

- **2026-10-08 — Shell details (P4.9, the owner's review).**
  1. **Breadcrumbs only from two levels.** One crumb repeats the page's title; `Breadcrumbs` renders nothing for fewer than two items (tested), and AppShell shows neither the crumbs nor their divider.
  2. **The user menu is neutral.** Signing out loses nothing, so no entry of the user menu is drawn in the danger colours: AppShell's user menu ignores `MenuEntry.destructive` (DropdownMenu keeps it for real destructive actions such as Delete).
  3. **Scrolling module tabs fade at the edge** with more past it, toward the start and the end in reading order: a CSS mask on the row (`edgeFade`, tested) — `linear-gradient` in the mask with `transparent` and `currentcolor` (a mask uses only alpha), so the tabs fade into whatever surface is behind them with no colour value; the fade follows the scroll (scroll and resize listeners) and is absent when everything fits.
  - **General rules recorded:** a count is never shown without its noun — either the application writes the text ("5 tasks") or a message receives the number formatted; a list that may hold thousands of entries is searchable, sectioned (pinned, recent, all) and virtualised; a trail of one crumb is not shown; menus of the user's own session (account, sign out) are neutral.
- **2026-10-08 — Launchpad dragging is live (P4.9, the owner's review).** The editing mode no longer uses the browser's drag and drop (HTML5 `draggable`), which drew a grey ghost of the card and did not work with touch. Dragging now runs on pointer events: a mouse or pen drag starts after 4px; a touch after a 250ms press without moving more than 8px, so a swipe still scrolls the page (once a touch drag has started, `touchmove` is cancelled so the page does not scroll under it). The dragged card lifts — `shadow-lg`, `border.strong`, the raised layer, `cursor-grabbing` — and follows the pointer; the other cards slide to the places they would take in the slow duration (250ms) with the standard easing, which the motion tokens make 0ms under `prefers-reduced-motion`; a placeholder shows where the card lands: the slot's size, radius lg, a 2px dashed `border.strong` on `surface.sunken` — neutral, never blue (D17). The target is the slot whose centre is nearest to the dragged card's centre, measured on the laid-out grid when the drag starts (`dragTargetIndex`), so right-to-left needs no mirroring (B.7: the slots already run from the leading edge); the shown order is `moveModuleTo`, the other cards' shift `slotShift` (all tested, ltr and rtl). Releasing reports the new order through `onReorder`; Escape or a cancelled pointer puts everything back. The move-earlier / move-later buttons stay the keyboard's and the non-drag way (WCAG 2.5.7). Story "Templates / Launchpad / Dragging" drops Sales one place later and then holds Purchasing over the third place for the picture.
  - **General rule — dragging in the Design System:** pointer events, never HTML5 drag and drop (no browser ghost, touch works); the dragged thing lifts with a shadow token, what makes room moves with a motion token, the drop place is a neutral dashed placeholder, and every drag has a button or key alternative. The only other drag, DataTable's column resize (P3.2), already uses pointer events and draws no ghost.
- **2026-10-08 — Suspended: a company or an account (P4.9, the owner's review).** `StatusPage` with `kind="suspended"` takes `subject`: `{ kind: 'account' }` (the default) or `{ kind: 'company', name }`, the name from the application. The default texts follow it: "Account suspended" / "Your account is suspended. Contact your administrator." and "Company suspended" / "Access for Kvadrat Gradnja d.o.o. is suspended. Contact your administrator." (`messages['status.suspendedCompanyTitle']`, `messages['status.suspendedCompanyDescription'](company)`; the account description changed from "Access to this account is suspended."). Other kinds ignore `subject`. `SuspendedSubject` is exported. Stories "Suspended company" and "Suspended account".
  - **Messages:** `status.suspendedCompanyTitle`, `status.suspendedCompanyDescription`.
- **2026-10-08 — Lists, tables and counts (P4.9, the owner's review).**
  1. **Every number a component shows goes through the provider's `format` (general rule).** The list said "All 1.284" (saved views, through `format.number`) and "1,284 rows" (the count message wrote its own English grouping). **Rule:** a message that shows a number receives the number (for the application's plural rules) **and its text, already written by `format.number`** — `(count, text, …)`; the message never formats. Changed signatures (an alpha breaking change of `LiroMessages`): `table.count(count, text, exact)`, `field.selectedCount(count, text)`, `period.quarter(quarter, text, year)`, `due.overdue(days, text)`, `due.inDays(days, text)`, `bulk.selected(count, text)`, `bulk.selectAll(total, text)`, `bulk.confirmTitle(count, text)`, `grid.cell(column, line, text)`, `grid.removeLine(line, text)`, `shell.notifications(unread, text)`, `shell.waiting(count, text)`, `launchpad.hidden(count, text)`, `lifecycle.step(step, stepText, total, totalText, label)`. `formatCount(messages, format, count, exact?, threshold?)` takes the format (exported helper, also changed). The English defaults no longer import `formatDecimal`. Years are not counts: "September 2026" keeps its digits ungrouped. Tested: `formatCount` with sr-Latn gives "1.284 rows" and "More than 10.000 rows" (`data-table.test.tsx`); a replaced `table.count` receives "1.234" (`provider-p15.test.tsx`); DueDate writes "1.366 days overdue" in sr-Latn; ListPage's phone select shows "1.284".
  2. **DueDate: the date, always; the state as a small note, never a second pill.** A due column showed a badge ("3 days overdue") beside the status badge — two pills per row, and the date only in a tooltip. Now DueDate always writes the date (DateText), then, 8px after it on the same baseline (wrapping under it in a narrow column), a note in xs medium: "3 days overdue" in `status.danger.fg`, "Due today" and "Due in 2 days" in `status.warning.fg`, "Settled" in `text.secondary`; a later date has no note. The words carry the state, so nothing depends on colour. The tooltip (and its button) is gone. Applies everywhere DueDate is used: the invoice list, the dashboard's open invoices, the approval detail, QuickPreview, worklist stories. Story "DueDate, tooltip on focus" is replaced by "DueDate, narrow column" (its baselines are removed).
  3. **A placeholder never repeats the label (general rule).** The Status filter read "Status" above and "Status" inside. A choice filter's empty value now says "All" (`messages['filter.all']`, new) — select, yes/no and multiple choice; a text filter has no placeholder. Applies to every FilterBar.
  4. **Saved views at scale.** Desktop: the first five views are tabs (`visibleViews`, default `VISIBLE_VIEWS` = 5; a sixth alone is shown as a tab rather than a menu of one — `splitViews`, tested); the rest are under a "More ▾" tab-styled button (`messages['list.moreViews']`) opening a menu of radio items with their counts; when the current view is under it, the button shows that view's name and count with the active line, `aria-pressed`. A fixed number, not a measurement: it never changes with fonts or width (stable pictures; the row still scrolls sideways at very narrow widths). Phones: one select-like button "View: All 1.284 ▾" (`messages['list.view'](name)` plus the count) opening every view with its count and a check on the current one; a search field above it when there are more than 7 views (`VIEW_SEARCH_THRESHOLD`, as the companies; `messages['list.findView']`, "Nothing found" when empty). The "Save view" slot stays at the end of the row.
  5. **FilterBar on phones: search first, actions in one menu.** On phones the bar is a column: the search field first and full width, then Filters, Sort and, with the new `phoneMenu` (menu entries), one "⋯" button at the end (`messages['action.more']`) holding the list's actions (Export). With `phoneMenu`, `actions` (Columns, Export) is a desktop row and is not shown on phones: choosing columns means nothing on a list of rows without columns. Without `phoneMenu`, phones show `actions` under the search.
  6. **No cards inside a card (general rule, phones).** A DataTable `inCard` in the cards layout (ListPage, DocumentPage lines, ReportPage, the dashboard's table) drew bordered, rounded cards inside the card. In a card the rows are now one flat list: no border or radius per row, padding 12px by 16px, a 1px `border.subtle` line between rows (none above the first or below the last), the selected row `surface.selected` with the 3px `border.selected` start bar, the focus ring inset; the card totals a full-width sunken band with the `border.strong` line on top. A standalone DataTable on the page background keeps its cards. Stories: "DataTable / Phone: in a card, a flat list"; tested.
  7. **A card ends with its last row (general rule).** A DataTable `inCard` padded 12px under its last row even with nothing under it (ListPage "Visible title", ReportPage's result). The 12px now stands only under totals, a row-limit note, the count or the paging; otherwise the card's border follows the last row. Tested.
  - **Messages:** new `filter.all`, `list.moreViews`, `list.view` (a function of the view's name), `list.findView`; signatures above.
- **2026-10-08 — Documents and side panels (P4.9, the owner's review).**
  1. **One spacing rule for every side panel.** The header (padding md), an sm (12px) gap, the body padded md (16px) at the sides and the bottom: the header button is `px-4 pt-4` with `pb-3` while open (`pb-4` closed). The body's content adds no space of its own at its outer edges: inside a panel the first and last rows of a KeyValueList (`key-value-item`) and of the panel lists (`panel-row`) lose their outer padding, so every panel's first row stands 12px under its header and its last row 16px above the panel's edge, whatever the content (the owner's example: Delivery → "Delivered" stood lower than other panels' first rows). **Panel content uses the system's lists, never hand-made rows:** facts as a one-column KeyValueList (label start, value end on one baseline — a badge row and a text row align), documents as RelatedDocuments, comments and history as ActivityList. General rule: a container sets the space at its edges; its content never adds its own.
  2. **RelatedDocuments** (new, exported): every item is a link through the provider's `linkComponent` — the type (xs, text.secondary) above the number (sm medium, the link colour, left to right) — with the item's status (a StatusBadge from the application) at the end; rows 8px above and below with a border.subtle line between them. Nothing appears in the list that cannot be opened. General rule: a reference to another record is always a link to it, with its kind and state.
  3. **ActivityList** (new, exported) for comments and history: the author's name sm semibold with the time in xs text.tertiary on the same line, the text under it; entries newest first; the latest `limit` (2 by default) with "Show all 3" (`messages['panel.showAll'](count, countText)`, the count through `format.number`), then "Show fewer" (`panel.showFewer`). The time is written by the application (`format.dateTime` in the tenant's zone). P5.1's Message family replaces it for conversations; the panel list stays for the compact view.
  4. **The document header is slots; the Core decides what it shows** (documented in the DocumentPage docs): `counterparty` (the customer or supplier block), `keyFigures` (any two to four values — amount due, invoice total, due date — or none), and the new `details` (the document's own values in one wrapping row, 32px apart). A slot left out takes no room.
  5. **Drafts are as simple as possible.** The application passes no side panels (attachments only when there are some), two header actions (Preview, then Issue invoice — the main action last), the values the system filled in (number, issue date, due date) as `details`, and an EditableGrid with unit, tax category and amount per line; the totals follow the lines (computed by the Core). A draft never shows delivery or SEF state. **ChangeableValue** (new, exported): a value the system filled in shown as a value — the label xs text.secondary above, the value sm medium — with a 28px subtle pencil (the edit intent) named "Change <label>" (`messages['value.change']`); the pencil turns it into the application's field, which takes the focus and stays a field from then on (`editing` / `onEditingChange` make it controlled). Chosen over pre-filled fields: a field reads as "to be filled in", a value reads as "done, change if needed". General rule: what the system has already decided is shown as a value, never as an empty or pre-filled input.
  6. **Tax category with its rate in the VAT column** ("S 20%", "S 10%", "E"), and the totals' labels name the category ("Tax base S 20%", "VAT S 20%", "Exempt E (deposit)"), as the e-invoice system names them. The story data were checked: the DocumentPage stories' totals now include the invoice total (183.254,00 RSD) and amount due (133.254,00) of their four lines; the draft's totals are computed from its lines in whole paras (135.120,00 at S 20% → VAT 27.024,00; 8.500,00 at S 10% → 850,00; total 171.494,00) and change as the lines change. The example F-2026-0412 keeps 185.954,00 / 135.954,00.
  7. **"Add line" is a normal button:** the default neutral look (raised, border.default) at the normal 36px size and padding with the Plus icon — no longer the small tinted primary one. It is the only add-affordance of its kind in the library.
  8. **No cards inside a card on phones (documents).** EditableGrid has `inCard` (as DataTable): on desktop the grid keeps 16px from the card's edges; on phones its lines are a flat list with 1px border.subtle dividers, padded 16px at the sides, the totals a sticky bar with a line under it instead of a card. The read-only lines (DataTable `inCard`, cards layout) follow the same rule (DataTable change). General rule: inside a card, repeated items are rows divided by lines, never cards.
  9. **KeyFigures on phones, a clean two-column grid:** two equal columns 16px apart, 12px between rows, every value 16px (lg) semibold, so an amount with its currency fits its half of a 360px screen on one line (20px wrapped "135.954,00 RSD"). Desktop keeps 20px and 32px between items.
  10. **The document's main action on phones is in the shell's bottom bar** (within thumb reach): the invoice example's "Send reminder", the draft's "Issue invoice"; the header keeps the secondary actions (PDF, Preview).
  - **Messages:** `panel.showAll`, `panel.showFewer`, `value.change`.
- **2026-10-08 — Records: one page, read and edited as a whole (P4.9, the owner's review).** A record (an employee, a customer, an item) is a `DetailPage`, never a tabbed form with a side column. **View:** the header (back, name, status, subtitle, actions), the key figures (employee: net salary, leave left, contract ends), then the sections stacked — for the employee Personal, Employment, Leave, Payroll — each a SectionCard whose values are plain text in a KeyValueList (label and value in two columns), never disabled inputs; data that belongs to a part lives in that part's section (leave days and the manager in Leave). No side column: history and files become sections. **Edit (SAP Fiori):** one mode for the whole record. The application's one "Edit" header action (intent edit) sets `mode="edit"`; every section then shows its `edit` content (fields in a `FormGrid`, same order, same columns), a section with `editable: false` (from the application: the user may not change it, or it is kept elsewhere, e.g. Leave through leave requests) keeps its read-only content; the header and section actions go away; a bar sticky at the bottom (`FormBottomBar`, the form's bar: "Unsaved changes" when `dirty`, `editActions` — Cancel, then Save — at the end) stays visible while the page scrolls; the first field takes the focus; while `dirty` the back button asks first and closing the page asks the browser's question (one `useUnsavedChangesGuard`). No per-section Edit buttons. **Phones:** the same; Edit stands in the AppShell's bottom bar in view mode, and the page's own bar replaces it in edit mode. **RecordFormPage** is for a new record (its stories: "New employee", sections stacked, no tabs, no side column). New API: `DetailPage` `mode`, `editActions`, `dirty`; `DetailSection` `edit`, `editable`; `FormGrid` (exported). **Tabs on a record — only when** it is longer than about eight sections and its sections serve different people at different times (an item with its sales, purchasing and warehouse data): then the sections are grouped under start-aligned page Tabs, edit mode still covers the whole record, and a tab with an error says so (FormTabs' rule). Below that, `sectionBar` is enough.
- **2026-10-08 — Lists and grids answer to their own width (P4.9, the owner's review: "Wareho use", "complet e").** Cause, found: KeyValueList and FormSection chose their columns by the viewport (`sm:grid-cols-2`); in a phone-width pane (the approval detail in a phone frame, a narrow column) the list kept two columns, a value got less room than its longest word, and `overflow-wrap: break-word` broke the word in the middle. **Rule:** a component inside page content takes its columns from its own width (Tailwind container queries on a wrapper, `@container`), never from the viewport: KeyValueList two columns from 36rem (576px), three from 54rem (864px), one below; in a list narrower than 24rem (384px) — a phone — the label stands above its value, at the start (no more label-and-value squeezed side by side). FormSection's fields and the new FormGrid use the same breakpoints, so read and edit mode line up. `overflow-wrap: break-word` stays only as the last resort for a single word longer than the whole line (an e-mail address). Applications no longer need `columns={phone ? 1 : 2}`.
- **2026-10-08 — A grid item spans with `col-span-full`, never a fixed span (P4.9; Overview on a phone squeezed Revenue beside Cash).** Cause, found: the overview's table card had `md:col-span-2`; in the phone layout (one column, a phone frame in a wide window) a two-column span adds a second, implicit column, so the two charts stood side by side at 150px each. DashboardPage's phone grid is one column (`minmax(0,1fr)`), and its docs say: a chart or table spans both columns with `col-span-full`.
- **2026-10-08 — One dialog footer (P4.9, the owner's review: Stay / Leave unevenly spaced, not aligned with the text).** Every dialog, drawer and confirmation ends with `DialogFooter`: 16px under the body's last part, the buttons **8px apart, as in every other row of buttons** (ActionGroup, page header actions), their row ending on the body's 16px inset. ConfirmDialog (and its Delete, Irreversible and Reason presets, hence the unsaved-changes question) used its own row 12px apart while Dialog used 16px; now both use DialogFooter. ConfirmDialog's text (and the field of the irreversible and reason presets) starts where the title's text does — after the 18px icon and its 8px gap (26px) — instead of under the icon. FormWizard's Back / Next row is 8px apart too; AlertDialogFooter (internal) likewise. The old entry "buttons 12px apart" (P2.4b) is replaced by this.
- **2026-10-08 — Button order (P4.9, the owner; AGENTS.md D13 restated).** In every group the confirming or primary action is last: Cancel – Save, Stay – Leave, Back – Next, Reject – Approve; right-to-left mirrors the row, so the main action stays at the end. Stories that showed Save before Cancel were corrected (Button "Sizes").
- **2026-10-08 — Decisions on a worklist (P4.9, the owner's review).** Approve and Reject stand only in the detail pane, where the item is seen whole — never in every row. `WorklistItem.actions` is removed (an alpha breaking change); rows can instead carry checkboxes (`checked`, `onCheckedChange`, 16px box with a 24px target, named `messages['table.selectRow'](label)` with the new `WorklistItem.label`), and `bulkBar` (a BulkActionBar) stands above the list while any row is checked. On a phone the decisions go into the AppShell's bottom bar. The approval detail shows the invoice's lines (item, quantity, VAT, amount) and its attachment as a link. **After a decision** the next item opens at the same place and a toast confirms it; **Undo** only when the Core can take it back (an approval; a rejection has already gone to SEF), so an approval asks nothing first — an action that can be undone is done and offers Undo (ConfirmDialog's "When not").
- **2026-10-08 — ReasonConfirmDialog (P4.9, new, exported).** A confirmation that asks why — reject, return, cancel with a reason: the ConfirmDialog frame (tone, icon, buttons) with a required reason. With `reasons` (the Core's list) one must be chosen (RadioGroupField) and a "Details" text is optional; without a list the text is the reason and is required. The confirm button enables once a reason is given; `onConfirm({ reason, text })`. Messages `confirm.reason` ("Reason"), `confirm.reasonDetails` ("Details"); the application usually passes its own `reasonLabel` ("Reason for rejection").
- **2026-10-08 — A toast's action (P4.9).** `notice.*(message, { action: { label, onClick } })`: one small neutral button before the close button (Undo), from the application; pressing it runs the action and closes the toast. A toast with an action stays at least 8000ms (`NOTICE_ACTION_DURATION`, tested) so the action can be reached; sonner pauses it while the pointer or the focus is on it; an error still waits.
- **2026-10-08 — No date followed by a comma (P4.9, the owner's review: "30.09.2026., complete").** A Serbian date ends with a full stop, so a comma after it reads as a typo. Write the state first ("Complete, 30.09.2026.") or, better, two values ("Goods received: Complete", "Goods received on: 30.09.2026."). Searched the library, stories and docs: the approval details (example and WorklistPage story) were the only ones.
- **2026-10-08 — Money is never rounded, in stories too (P4.9, the owner's review: Overview KPIs).** Every RSD amount is written with its paras (5.684.200,00 RSD); `decimals={0}` was removed from the overview, StatCard, DashboardPage and radial/radar stories (their centre labels now "of 68.000.000,00 RSD"). Zero decimals stay only for currencies without a minor unit (JPY). The overview's subtitle names only the period ("September 2026"): the company is in the header; the home page's h1 ("Home") is for screen readers only.
- **2026-10-08 — A settings row names its control once (P4.9, the owner's review: "Default payment term" twice on a phone).** Cause, found: one row's SelectField was given its label without `hideLabel`. Fixed for the class: a new internal context `FieldLabelsHidden` makes every field label inside it screen-reader-only (a field's own `hideLabel` still wins); SettingsPage wraps each row's control in it, so an application cannot write the name twice (tested).
- **2026-10-08 — Charts' table view in right-to-left (P4.9, the owner's review: columns misplaced, headers not aligned).** Cause, found: the table's header cells had `text-end` together with `bidi-isolate` (`unicode-bidi: plaintext`) on the cell itself — the rule TEXT_ISOLATE's own comment forbids: plaintext resolves start and end against the cell's text, and a header of digits ("2026") is left to right, so in a right-to-left table it went to the left while its numbers stood at the right; the row headers had TEXT_DIRECTION the same way. Now the cells keep the page's direction and alignment and only the text inside is isolated (a span, `bdi` for values). The same table serves every family (area, bar, line, pie, radar, radial); each family has a story "Show as table" whose play opens it, so it has baselines in the four modes. No other component combined `text-start`/`text-end` with TEXT_ISOLATE on the same element (searched).

- **2026-10-08 — Follow-ups to the owner's review of #68 (P4.9d).**
  1. **A full-screen sheet fills its containing block, never the window's height.** `SheetContent` has a new side `full` (`inset-0`, full width and height); the phone company switcher uses it, titled "Switch company" (`messages['shell.switchCompanyTitle']`) with the close button, the search field pinned at the top and the list scrolling under it, the safe areas inside. **Cause, found:** the sheet was `h-dvh` anchored at the bottom: inside a story's phone frame (which contains fixed elements) it was taller than the frame, and focusing the search field scrolled the frame by the header's height, so the field was cut off and the page and its bottom bar showed below. Focus moves without scrolling (`preventScroll`), to the search field on open and back to the user menu's button on close (Escape, the close button or a choice) — the menu entry that opened it is gone by then. Tested in "Phone company switcher" (the sheet covers the whole frame, bottom bar included) and "Phone company switcher, Escape".
  2. **One inset in every dialog:** header, text and footer start 16px from the edge; the text starts at the header row's start (under the title icon), no longer indented to the title's text; the header's end padding is 16px like the body's (the 11px beside the close button is gone). Applies to Dialog, Drawer, every sheet and every confirmation (they share the dialog primitives and `ConfirmFrame`).
  3. **The safe action takes the focus (rule).** When a confirmation opens, the focus goes to Cancel / Stay — never to the confirming button or the close X — so a single Enter closes it and never deletes, rejects, voids or discards. `ConfirmFrame` (ConfirmDialog, DeleteConfirmDialog, IrreversibleConfirmDialog, ReasonConfirmDialog, the unsaved-changes guard) does it; the Radix AlertDialog primitive already focuses its Cancel. Tested by the story "ConfirmDialog / Initial focus on Cancel": Delete, Reject and Void each open with Cancel focused, Enter closes them, nothing is confirmed.
  4. **Toasts on phones stand above the bottom action bar** (rule): AppShell publishes its bottom bar's height as `--liro-shell-bottom` on the provider's root, and the Toaster stands 16px above it; `Toaster` has a `layout` ('phone': full width less 16px (md) at each side; by default from the viewport). The width is measured from an empty fixed line across the screen, because sonner sizes both its list and each toast with `--width` (a percentage would shrink twice). Tested in "Examples / Supplier invoice to approve, phone, approved".
  5. **A quantity and its unit are two values (rule).** The unit of measure is the Core's — a short symbol with its standard code (pc / H87, kWh, l, mo) — shown in its own Unit column or label; the Design System never joins a number to a word that would need a plural ("12 line"), because it has no plural rules of its own. Where the Core wants words, it sends the text already written for the quantity. The approvals lines got a Unit column; numbers the stories write into text ("5 to approve", "2 invoices") go through `format.number`.
  6. **The SettlingValue test on Windows.** The test samples the pending value for about a second (up to three on a loaded machine) and then waits at most 5 s for it to settle; with the story's 1.2 s answer the value had already settled on Windows before the test looked for the dot. The story now answers after 4.2 s (4.5 s after the last key): after the sampling, within the wait, on CI and on Windows (run four times in parallel). The test file is unchanged. A full local run of all story tests on Windows takes longer than the 25-minute global limit of the Playwright configuration, so its last tests end with "Test ended"; run a part (`-g`) or `--shard` locally.

## Liro patterns (Phase 5 part 1)

Phase 5 part 1 was built by seven groups in parallel (A: P5.1, P5.2; B: P5.3–P5.5; C: P5.6, P5.7 and part of P5.21; D1 and D2: P5.18 and LookupField; E: P5.19, P5.20; F: the rest of P5.21) and merged into one pull request. Their decisions follow, by step.

### P5.1 — History, comments and messages

- **2026-10-08 — The shadcn/ui message and questionnaire primitives (P5.1, owner: "Phase 5 builds on shadcn/ui").** shadcn/ui's registry items `bubble`, `marker`, `message`, `message-scroller` and `questionnaire` (style `radix-vega`, fetched 2026-10-08 from `https://ui.shadcn.com/r/styles/radix-vega/<name>.json`) are copied into `packages/ui/src/primitives/` and adapted as the P2.1 primitives: Liro meanings instead of shadcn colours, logical properties, no text of their own (required props), `cn` from `primitives/cn.ts`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, not exported (D10); internal stories "Internal/Primitives/Messages" and "Internal/Primitives/Questionnaire".
  1. **One new runtime dependency, `@shadcn/react` 0.3.1** (see "Versions"): Message Scroller and Questionnaire are thin styles over its unstyled `@shadcn/react/message-scroller` and `@shadcn/react/questionnaire`, which hold the behaviour (scroll anchoring, following the end, the questionnaire's fieldsets, number-key shortcuts and focus). Copying its compiled code instead was rejected (unreadable, untestable, not maintained with the registry). Bubble, Marker and Message are plain markup.
  2. **Bubble:** shadcn's seven variants are replaced by three neutral surfaces — `raised` (surface.raised with a border.default line: other people's messages), `sunken` (the user's own) and `ghost` (no box) — **no blue bubbles (D17)**. Radius lg, padding 8px 12px, 13px, text direction from the content. Not kept: `asChild` (bubbles are not buttons) and BubbleReactions (no reactions in Liro).
  3. **Marker:** xs text.secondary (shadcn's sm competed with the message); the separator's lines 1px border.subtle drawn as the pseudo-elements' top borders, 4px from the text with `me-1` / `ms-1`.
  4. **Message:** the 26px avatar slot (PersonAvatar 'sm') at the top of the row beside the name (shadcn put it at the bubble's bottom); header and footer xs text.secondary without inline padding; `align="end"` is `flex-row-reverse`, which follows the direction.
  5. **Message Scroller:** the region's name (`label`) and the button's text are required; the region focusable with the inset focus ring; the content a `log` (new messages announced politely); 12px between items (shadcn 32px); shadcn's `scrollbar-thin`, `scroll-fade-b` and `scrollbar-gutter-stable` utilities do not exist in Liro and are left out. The button is the neutral default button, small (30px), with an arrow and text, centred at the bottom; it fades and slides in the base duration, none under reduced motion.
  6. **Questionnaire:** a choice is a 44px row, border.default, radius md, padding 12px 16px, surface.hover under the pointer; **a chosen choice is the neutral selection (surface.selected with a border.selected line)**, while its radio or checkbox is the Liro control filled with brand.solid (checked controls stay blue, D17); the focus ring around the whole row; disabled without opacity; invalid with the danger border; the number key drawn as ShortcutHint's key. **Not kept:** the primitive's Previous / Next / Skip / Submit (English defaults, and they move in list order, not along a branching path), its Progress (a position, not answered questions) and its Input (the Design System's fields are used).
- **2026-10-08 — HistoryList (P5.1; the plan's "Timeline / HistoryList", one component).** The full history of a record: entries newest first, grouped by day like the notifications page — "Today", "Yesterday", then `format.date` — each heading an h3 by default (`headingLevel`), xs semibold text.secondary. An entry's `at` is an ISO instant in the tenant's offset; its date part is its day and its clock part is shown with `format.time` (or the application's `time`), so heading and time always agree.
