Each entry is a row with a 1px border.subtle line above it (none above a day's first): a 26px **actor marker** — a person's PersonAvatar 'sm' (nothing else marks a human), the Bot square for an agent (and AgentMark after its name), a neutral Settings2 icon for the system and a Plug for an integration, both on surface.sunken and named "System" / "Integration" (`role="img"`); then the name (sm semibold), the time (xs text.tertiary, tabular), the application's sentence (sm), each changed field as "label old → new" (label and old value text.secondary, the new value text.primary medium, both isolated (`bdi`) and exactly as given; the arrow mirrors in right-to-left; "Before:" / "After:" for assistive technology; an emptied field shows "—"), and "On behalf of <name>" (xs text.secondary). **Paging from the application:** `hasMore` shows "Show more" (neutral) under the list, `onLoadMore` asks for older entries, `loading` shows skeleton rows on the first page and a small spinner in place of the button after it. Empty: EmptyState compact ("No changes yet"). The day grouping is `components/day-groups.ts` (`groupByDayOf`, newest or oldest first; tested), shared with MessageList. Messages: `history.system`, `history.integration`, `history.onBehalfOf(name)`, `history.before`, `history.after`, `history.showMore`, `history.emptyTitle`, `history.emptyDescription`. The day headings use the neutral keys `day.today` / `day.yesterday` and one `dayHeading` (day-groups.ts), shared with MessageList and the notifications page (integration, 2026-10-09).
- **2026-10-08 — The Message family (P5.1).** For comments on a record and task conversations, on Bubble, Marker, Message and Message Scroller.
  1. **MessageBubble:** the author (PersonAvatar 'sm' and the name sm semibold; an agent's Bot square and AgentMark), the time (`format.time(at)` or the application's `time`), the text in a Bubble (`whitespace-pre-wrap`, so Shift+Enter's line breaks show), an optional `extra` inside the bubble under the text (a questionnaire, files). **Own and others' messages differ by side and surface, never colour:** others at the start on the raised surface with a border, the user's own at the end on surface.sunken without avatar and name ("You" for assistive technology). `status` 'sending' shows a small spinner with "Sending…"; 'failed' shows "Not sent" in status.danger.fg with an icon (`role="alert"`) and a Retry link button (`onRetry`).
  2. **MessageList:** messages **oldest first**, as a conversation reads, on Message Scroller (opens at the latest, follows the end while the reader is there, keeps the reader's place otherwise). A **day separator** (a Marker: Today, Yesterday, the date) starts each day. **Runs:** messages of one author within 5 minutes on the same day (`startsRun`, `RUN_MINUTES`, tested) show only their bubbles under one header, 4px apart (12px between runs); the author stays named for assistive technology. **"Jump to latest"** appears while the reader is away from the end; when messages arrived meanwhile it says "2 new messages" (the count through `format.number`). The user's own new message scrolls to the end. The list's height is its container's. Empty: EmptyState compact ("No messages yet"); loading: skeleton rows. `ThreadMessage.element` lets the application draw a whole message itself (an AgentQuestion).
  3. **MessageComposer:** a MentionCombobox (label for assistive technology only) and a toolbar: the application's `tools` at the start (attach), the hint "Enter sends, Shift+Enter starts a new line" (xs text.tertiary), Send at the end — the primary family in the light weight, so the page keeps its one filled button (D13). **Enter sends, Shift+Enter breaks the line**, Enter while the mention list is open chooses the person, an IME composition is never sent. `onSend({ text, mentions })` may return a promise: the text stays as sent, the hint becomes a spinner with "Sending…", and the text is cleared when it resolves (kept when it rejects). Disabled with a visible reason; `error`; `attachments` is a slot above the toolbar (for AttachmentList).
  4. **MessageThread:** the list (taking the room) and the composer under it, 12px apart.
  - **ActivityList stays:** the compact side-panel list of the latest entries; it is not a conversation. In the example the side panel "Latest activity" keeps ActivityList while the page's Comments and History sections use MessageThread and HistoryList.
  - Messages: `message.you`, `message.send`, `message.sending`, `message.sendHint`, `message.failed`, `message.retry`, `message.jumpToLatest`, `message.newMessages(count, text)`, `message.emptyTitle`, `message.emptyDescription`.
- **2026-10-08 — MentionCombobox (P5.1).** A multi-line text field in which "@" offers people: candidates from the application (all of them, filtered by name ignoring case and accents as ComboboxField, or its own search with `onSearch` after 300ms and `loading`). A mention starts at an "@" at the start or after a space (not inside an e-mail address) and reads up to 40 characters with at most two spaces (`mentionQueryAt`, tested). Choosing writes "@Name " as **one token: Backspace right after it removes the whole name** (`tokenBefore`); the mentions still in the text, with their ids, are reported on every change (`onMentionsChange`, `mentionsInText`), so the application never parses names. **WAI-ARIA:** the combobox pattern on a text area — the focus stays in the text, the shared ComboboxPopover list (a press never takes the focus), `aria-activedescendant`, `aria-autocomplete="list"`, `aria-controls` while open; **no `role="combobox"`, which a `<textarea>` may not carry (ARIA in HTML)**. Keys while open: ArrowDown / ArrowUp (wrapping), Home / End, Enter or Tab chooses, Escape closes until the next "@". Options: the 26px avatar (Bot square for an agent), the name and the application's line; the highlighted option the brand fill (D17). The text area grows with its content (`field-sizing: content`) from 60px to 160px. `MentionText` draws a message's text with its mentions semibold (`splitMentions`, longer names first; tested). Messages: `mention.list`.
- **2026-10-08 — Questionnaire (P5.1, owner 2026-10-07: a general step-by-step question component).** On the shadcn Questionnaire primitive (each question a fieldset named by its legend, real radio buttons and checkboxes, the number keys, the focus moving to the question whose turn it is). Questions as data (`QuestionDefinition`: id, title, description, type, options, required (default true), next, currency, decimals, placeholder, otherLabel, fieldLabel); the logic in `questionnaire-logic.ts` (tested).
  1. **Branching (declarative):** a question's `next` names the next question, `null` ends, left out the next in the list follows; an option's `next` wins over its question's; for several choices the first chosen option (in the options' order) that names one. The path is recomputed from the answers; a loop or an unknown id ends it.
  2. **Answer types:** single choice, multiple choice, date (DateField), number (NumberField), amount (MoneyField), short text (TextField), and "Other" with its own text on any choice (`other: true`; its field appears under the options, takes the focus and is required while chosen). Typed answers use the fields hidden-labelled (`fieldLabel ?? title`; the legend shows the question).
  3. **Back keeps every answer.** Answers on a branch no longer taken are kept in the component's state and come back when the user returns to that branch, but **only the answers of the questions on the current path are submitted** (`answersOnPath`).
  4. **Summary** (`summary`, default true): "Check your answers" (h2, `headingLevel`), every question on the path with its answer in a one-column KeyValueList (formatted by the provider; several choices joined with `text.join`; an optional question left empty "Not answered"), each with ChangeableValue's 28px pencil ("Change <question>"). After a change, Next returns to the summary — **unless the change opened questions on the path that have no answer: those are asked first.** The summary takes the focus when it opens. The final button is the application's (`submitLabel`, `submitIcon`), filled; `onSubmit` may return a promise, or `submitting`. Without a summary the last question's button submits (an agent's short question).
  5. **Progress:** "3 of 7 answered" — answered questions on the current path (so it can go down when a branch adds questions), both numbers through `format.number` — above a ProgressBar; `progress={false}` for a single question.
  6. **Keyboard:** Enter goes on (not on a button, which presses itself; a typed date or number is read by its own field on that Enter first); the number keys 1–9 choose options while the focus is not in a typing field (the key shown at the end of each option); radio buttons move with the arrows natively, checkboxes by the primitive.
  7. **Checks:** a required question without an answer ("Answer this question to go on."), an "Other" without its text ("Write your own answer to go on."), then `validate(question, answer, answers)` returns the application's message. The error shows after Next, under the choices (the fieldset `aria-invalid`) or under the field, and the focus goes to the answer.
  8. **Buttons:** Back (the back intent, light) at the start, hidden on the first question; the main action last: Next (filled), "Review answers" before the summary, the application's label without a summary.
  - **When to use which:** Questionnaire for a guided path whose next question depends on the answer; FormWizard (P3.5) for a long multi-step form whose fields are known; FormSection for an ordinary form.
  - Messages: `questionnaire.next`, `questionnaire.back`, `questionnaire.review`, `questionnaire.submit`, `questionnaire.progress(answered, answeredText, total, totalText)`, `questionnaire.progressLabel`, `questionnaire.summaryTitle`, `questionnaire.notAnswered`, `questionnaire.required`, `questionnaire.otherRequired`, and `text.join(items)` (a list of labels or names on one line; English ", ").
- **2026-10-08 — Example screens of P5.1 and P5.2.** "Examples / Invoice with history, comments and presence" (`/sales/invoices/F-2026-0410`, also reached from the invoice list's preview): F-2026-0410 to Medic Lab Niš d.o.o. as a DocumentPage — four lines whose amounts, bases, VAT and total are computed in `data-A.ts` in whole paras and checked against the dataset (186.420,35 RSD, 100.000,00 paid on 02.10.2026., 86.420,35 due; the module throws if they disagree); PresenceAvatars in the header; Comments as a MessageThread in which the Liro agent asks for the payment date with an AgentQuestion (answering adds the agent's reply and a history entry); History as a HistoryList (person, SEF, the bank import, the system, the agent on behalf of Milica; "Show more" loads the first entry); side panels Delivery, Related documents and Latest activity (ActivityList). "Examples / Employment contract questionnaire" (`/hr/contracts/new`): Stefan Nikolić's contract — employee, position (choice + Other), indefinite / fixed term (end date and reason), start date, office / remote / hybrid (office days), probation, full / part time (hours), gross salary, annual leave days; the application's checks (start and end after today, leave at least 20 days — illustrative); "Generate contract" goes to the signing screen of P5.21. Desktop and phone; the play functions check the figures, the agent's question, a mention, the history paging and the whole questionnaire path from the keyboard.

### P5.2 — Presence and agent marking

- **2026-10-08 — AgentMark and PresenceAvatars (P5.2).**
  1. **AgentMark:** one marker after every name that belongs to an agent: lucide's Bot at 14px in text.secondary, **neutral, never blue** (an agent is not an action), the word `agent.mark` ("Agent") for assistive technology and in a tooltip on hover; `showLabel` writes the word beside the icon (xs medium) where it must be read without hovering. An agent's avatar, wherever one is shown, is the Bot icon on the neutral 26px square, never initials. Where only text can carry it (an accessible name), the name becomes "Liro agent (agent)" (`agent.named`).
  2. **PresenceAvatars:** who else has the record open (the application's data; the component never polls): up to `max` avatars (default 3), **4px apart, not overlapping** — an overlap needs a ring in the colour of the surface behind the row, which the component cannot know — then "+N" (the count through `format.number`) on the same neutral square; "+1" is never shown (the last avatar takes its place). The row is one button (target 28px): its accessible name and tooltip name everyone ("Also here: Dragan Ilić, Liro agent (agent)", joined with `text.join`); pressing it opens a popover "Who is here" listing each person with the application's line ("Viewing", "Editing") and AgentMark. Nothing for an empty list.
  - Messages: `agent.mark`, `agent.named(name)`, `presence.here(names)`, `presence.more(count, text)`, `presence.list`.

### P5.3 — Connection, environment and session markers

- **2026-10-08 — AgentQuestion (P5.3's agent sentence).** An agent asking the user something it cannot decide: MessageBubble with the agent's name, AgentMark and time, its question as the text, and the way to answer inside the bubble (`children`: a Questionnaire — one question with `summary={false}` and `progress={false}` — or a short form); once answered, `answer` replaces the form with the decision as text, so the conversation keeps it. A group named "Question from <agent>" (`agent.question(name)`). It is content: the application opens it in a Popover (the shell's agent button), a Drawer, or a MessageThread (`ThreadMessage.element`). AppShell has an additive `agent` slot before the notifications bell for the application's agent button (integration, 2026-10-09; story "AppShell / Agent button").
- **2026-10-08 — Shell markers (P5.3).** `ImpersonationBar`, `OfflineIndicator`, `EnvironmentMarker` and `ConnectionState` (`components/shell-markers.tsx`, logic `shell-markers-logic.ts`, tested), and an additive `banners` slot on AppShell.
  1. **Two bars, not Banners.** The impersonation bar and the offline indicator are strips across the screen aligned with the header's content: 8px top and bottom, the header's side padding (16px with the safe-area insets on phones, 24px from 48em), 13px text, one line that wraps, a 1px line under them. A Banner is a message inside a page; these are part of the shell and stay as long as their state lasts, so they cost as little height as possible. Each starts with a 16px icon and a semibold lead, then the parts separated by a decorative "·".
  2. **ImpersonationBar** (not dismissible): the warning tone (`status.warning.bg`, a `status.warning.border` line under it, UserCog and the lead "Viewing as Milica Petrović" in `status.warning.fg`, the rest in text.primary), then the mode, "Reason: …" and the time left, and at the end "Exit" (a small neutral default button, LogOut mirrored in right-to-left). A `section` named "Session as another user". **Time left:** the application passes the end as an instant (`endsAt`); the bar shows the whole minutes left, **rounded up** (`minutesLeft`, tested: 22 min 30 s reads "23 minutes left", the last seconds "1 minute left", never "0"), the number through `format.number`, and "Time is up" after the end. It redraws exactly when the number changes (`untilNextMinute`, tested), so it is never a minute late. The time is not a live region (it would speak every minute); ending the session is the Core's. Above the header, sticky with it.
  3. **OfflineIndicator** (`offline`, `waiting`, `action`): while offline a strip under the header in the neutral tone with its border (WifiOff), "Offline" and what is waiting in the application's words with its own count, an optional action at the end ("Try now"). When the connection returns the strip goes without a trace; a live region always in the page says "Offline" when it goes and "Back online" once when it returns, cleared after 5 s, and nothing on a page that starts online. Neutral, not warning: being offline is a state of the device, nothing is lost (and the impersonation bar has the warning tone, so the two never look alike).
  4. **EnvironmentMarker** (`label`, `tone`): a bordered StatusBadge after the brand, on phones too, never shrinking or wrapping; read as "Environment: Sandbox". **Tone:** warning by default (the data are not real: take care); premium, neutral, success or danger may be chosen; `EnvironmentTone` excludes info, so it is never blue (D17). Production shows no marker.
  5. **ConnectionState** (`status`: local / sending / sent / failed, `at`, `onRetry`): where a draft is, beside its actions — 12px text with a 14px icon in a `role="status"`: HardDrive "Saved on this device (at 14:12)" (`at` through `format.time`, the tenant's clock), the small Spinner ring "Sending…", CloudCheck in status.success.fg "Sent at 14:13", CircleAlert with status.danger.fg "Not sent. It is kept on this device." with a small "Send again" button (outside the live region) when `onRetry` is given.
  6. **Banners in the shell (AppShell `banners`, additive).** Application-wide Banners (a trial ending, a maintenance window) stand at the top of `main`, under the header and the offline indicator: full width and square (no radius or side borders, a 1px border.default line under each, 12px by the header's side padding), and **they scroll away with the page**. **The order, top to bottom:** impersonation bar → header (with module tabs) → offline indicator → banners → page. The first three are sticky together: a session as another user outranks everything; being offline concerns every action on the screen; a trial ending does not. A dismissible banner is the application's state (`onClose`). A message about one page's content stays an Alert in the page. Tested (order in the markup, banners inside `main`) and shown in "Templates / AppShell / Shell markers" (desktop and phone).
  - Messages: `connection.online`, `connection.local(time)`, `connection.sending`, `connection.sent(time)`, `connection.failed`, `connection.retry`, `environment.prefix`, `impersonation.region`, `impersonation.viewingAs(name)`, `impersonation.reason(reason)`, `impersonation.minutesLeft(minutes, text)`, `impersonation.ended`, `impersonation.exit`.

### P5.4 — Delivery and progress status

- **2026-10-08 — StatusTimeline (P5.4).** A process's states in order (delivery to SEF: Prepared → Sent to SEF → Delivered → Accepted), the current one highlighted, when each was reached and what comes next. A vertical list: a 16px dot per state on a 1px border.default line, the name (13px) with its time (`at`, an instant in the tenant's offset, shown as its date and clock through `format.date` and `format.time` — the tenant's clock, never the device's (integration 2026-10-09: `format.dateTime` would have shown UTC on CI) — in a `time` element, 12px text.tertiary) on the same line, an optional `detail` line (12px text.secondary), 16px between states. **One look for states:** the state logic is LifecycleBar's (`lifecycleState` reused): reached states neutral with a check ("Completed" for screen readers), the current one brand.solid with its name semibold in text.brand and `aria-current="step"`, later ones an empty ring with the name in text.tertiary, a failed one status.danger.solid with an X and its reason under the name ("Failed" for screen readers). **The next step** (`next`: title, description, actions, tone) stands under the current state: an inset block — not a card: radius md, 12px padding, the tone's subtle background, no border — with the title 13px semibold in the tone's fg, the description and the application's actions 8px apart; a `group` named "Next step: <title>". Neutral by default, warning or danger when the user must act; never blue. The dot is LifecycleBar's own `StepDot` (exported from `lifecycle-bar.tsx`, internal; `onLine` fills a future ring with the raised surface over the joining line), not a copy (integration, 2026-10-09).
- **2026-10-08 — JobProgress (P5.4) generalises "Progress in a dialog" (P3.6)** instead of adding a second pattern: the same Spinner (total unknown) or ProgressBar with the count, now with Cancel and the final report; one component inline in a card or as a Dialog's content (logic `job-logic.ts`, tested). **Running:** the label (13px semibold), the ProgressBar, under it "312 of 1.284" (both counts through `format.number`) and the item being worked on at the end of that line, cut with "…"; Cancel (the cancel intent) at the end of the footer, "Cancelling…" and disabled while the job stops; the count never draws past the total (`clampedDone`). **Report:** a 16px icon and "Finished" / "Cancelled" — CircleCheck status.success.fg when nothing failed, TriangleAlert status.warning.fg when some items failed, CircleSlash text.secondary when cancelled (`jobOutcome`, tested) —, the outcome lines written by the application with its own counts and nouns, each in its tone's fg, the failed items as rows (item 13px medium, reason 12px text.secondary, border.subtle lines) at most 240px high and scrolling (focusable only while it scrolls), and the application's actions ("Download report", then "Retry failed"). **The footer** stands 16px under the content with its buttons 8px apart at the end — DialogFooter's rule — so it reads the same in a card and in a dialog. In a dialog the application makes the Dialog not dismissible while the job runs. **Live regions (a departure from "Progress in a dialog"):** the running count is not live — a job that advances several times a second would make a screen reader talk without end; the ProgressBar carries its value for whoever asks — and the report is: a status region always in the page announces "Finished: <label>" and the outcome lines once.
  - Messages: `timeline.failed`, `timeline.next`, `job.progress(done, doneText, total, totalText)`, `job.cancel`, `job.cancelling`, `job.finished`, `job.cancelled`, `job.failures`.

### P5.5 — Files and document frame

- **2026-10-08 — FileDropzone (P5.5).** The field frame (label, description, error, disabled with its reason; no read-only) around a zone — radius md, a 1px dashed border.control border on the raised surface (danger when invalid, the disabled surface when disabled), 16px padding — with a 20px Upload icon, the "Choose files" / "Choose a file" button (neutral default, FileUp) and "or drop them here"; under them, **before choosing**, the accepted types and the size limit as the application writes them ("PDF, XML, JPG or PNG · up to 10 MB": `acceptText`, `maxSizeText`), 12px text.secondary, the button's description. The labelled group is the field's. **Dropping is a shortcut, never the only way:** the button opens the file dialog; phones show no drop text. While files are dragged over it the zone takes the neutral selection (surface.selected, a solid border.selected border), never blue. **The dragging rule is untouched:** files from the operating system arrive only through the browser's native drop events — no dragged element of ours, no ghost. **Checks:** every chosen or dropped file is checked again (`checkFiles`: the type first — extension in any case, exact MIME type or a "type/*" family, a typeless file only by extension —, then the size, then the count against `maxFiles` counting `existing`; a rejected file never uses up the count), because a dropped file never passes the input's `accept` and the dialog can be switched to "All files". Accepted files go to `onFiles`, rejected ones to `onReject` and are named under the zone in status.danger.fg with the reason, in an always-present `role="alert"`, until the next choice. Sizes are bytes compared only; the user reads the application's text (D4). Logic `file-logic.ts`, tested.
- **2026-10-08 — AttachmentList (P5.5).** Rows, never cards (P4.9 rule 4), a 1px border.subtle line between them; on its own or in a side panel the rows are 8px above and below and carry `panel-row` (SidePanels' edge rule); `inCard` pads them 12px by 16px. Each row: the name 13px medium (`bdi`, `dir="auto"`, long names wrap), the size as the application's text (12px text.tertiary), an optional `flag` (a StatusBadge: "Not an archival format"), an optional `detail` line, the state line, then `extra(file)` — **the per-file control slot** ("Send with the e-invoice"). **States and their next step in words:** uploading — a ProgressBar named by the file and "Uploading, 45%. It is checked for viruses next." (`format.percent`); scanning — ScanSearch, "Checking for viruses. It can be opened in a moment."; available — no state line, the name is a link-coloured button that downloads; quarantined — ShieldAlert in status.danger.fg, "Blocked: a threat was found, so it cannot be opened. Upload a clean copy."; failed — CircleAlert, "Not uploaded." and the application's reason, with "Upload again" when `onRetry`. **Download** through `onDownload(file)` at the moment of the click (a stored link would expire); while its promise is pending the name is `aria-busy` with a 14px Spinner after it. **Remove** (a 28px Trash2, destructive family, subtle) only where `canRemove(file)` returns true and `onRemove` is given; asking first is the application's. `attachmentOffers` (tested) decides what a row offers. Loading: three skeleton rows; empty: "No attachments" or the application's `empty`.
- **2026-10-08 — DocumentFrame (P5.5; protocol in `document-frame-protocol.ts`, tested; documented for viewer authors in `docs/document-frame.md`).** A viewer from another origin in an iframe with `sandbox="allow-scripts"` (`allow-same-origin` only with `allowSameOrigin`), `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"`. A message is taken only when it comes from the frame's own window, from `allowedOrigin` **exactly**, and matches the protocol (`{ protocol: 'liro-document-frame', version: 1, type, … }`; viewer → host `loading`, `ready(page, pageCount, zoom)`, `page`, `zoom`, `error(message?)`; host → viewer `goToPage`, `setZoom`); anything else is ignored. Commands go to `allowedOrigin` only; "*" only for an opaque origin ("null"), which cannot be named — the commands carry only a page number or a zoom level. **The viewer owns the state:** the toolbar shows what the viewer reports, never a guess. **Toolbar** (raised surface, 8px by 12px, a line under it): previous and next page (28px compact buttons, mirrored in right-to-left), "Page 2 of 3" (numbers through `format.number`, a polite status), zoom out, the level (`format.percent`, read as "Zoom 125%"), zoom in; steps 50, 75, 100, 125, 150, 200, 300 (`ZOOM_STEPS`, `zoomStep`); disabled at the ends and until ready. **Loading:** a page-shaped skeleton over the frame, `aria-busy`. **Error:** the viewer's `error` (its text, at most 300 characters) or no `ready` within `timeout` (default 20 s, `FRAME_TIMEOUT`): an ErrorState "The document could not be shown." with the text or "The document viewer did not answer." and "Try again", which loads a new frame. The frame: radius md, border.default, the sunken surface; 480px high unless the application sizes it. The stories host a viewer written in the story (`srcdoc`); one proves that messages from another origin are ignored.
  - Messages: `file.choose(multiple)`, `file.drop(multiple)`, `file.maxSize(limit)`, `file.rejectedType(name, accepted)`, `file.rejectedSize(name, limit)`, `file.rejectedCount(name, max, text)`, `attachment.uploading(percent)`, `attachment.scanning`, `attachment.quarantined`, `attachment.failed`, `attachment.download(name)`, `attachment.remove(name)`, `attachment.retry(name)`, `attachment.retryLabel`, `attachment.none`, `frame.toolbar`, `frame.previousPage`, `frame.nextPage`, `frame.zoomIn`, `frame.zoomOut`, `frame.page(page, pageText, count, countText)`, `frame.zoom(text)`, `frame.loading`, `frame.error`, `frame.timeout`, `frame.retry`.

### P5.6 — Sign-in building blocks

- **2026-10-08 — Sign-in building blocks (P5.6, with the owner's addition).** The flows live in the Core; the Design System gives the pieces inside AuthShell (shadcn's login-03), all exported. No shadcn code was copied: the pieces follow the shape of the login-05 block, rebuilt on our Field, Button and AuthShell.
  1. **EmailFirstForm** (login-05's shape): the e-mail field (`type="email"`, `autocomplete="username"` so a password manager pairs it with the next step's password), a full-width primary **Continue** (ArrowRight, mirrored in right-to-left), then the "or" divider and the providers' buttons (`providersPosition` 'after' by default, or 'before'), then optional lines `signUp` (sm text.secondary) and `terms` (xs text.tertiary), centred. `onSubmit` receives the trimmed address; the form has `noValidate` — the Core checks the address and returns `error`. `loading` keeps Continue's size and name, hides its content behind the 20px oval loader (ConfirmDialog's look), `aria-busy`, a second press does nothing; `disabled` with `disabledReason` shows the reason under the field and makes every button unavailable. Label and button default to `signIn.email` / `signIn.continue`.
  2. **ProviderSignInButtons:** "Continue with Microsoft" / "Continue with Google" — providers and labels from the application (`id`, `label`, `mark`, `onClick`, `disabled`, `loading`). **Neutral default buttons** (raised, border.default), full width, 8px apart; **never the provider's colour as a fill**. The mark is 18px, 12px before the label (Microsoft's spacing), decorative (the label names the provider): `mark: 'microsoft' | 'google'` draws the provider's official mark (see "Provider sign-in marks" below); another provider's mark comes in as a file address. The pressed provider is busy (loader, `aria-busy`); the others are `aria-disabled` until the redirect. `divider` 'above' / 'below' draws the "or" divider (xs text.secondary between two 1px border.default lines).
  3. **The marks** are drawn as Microsoft's and Google's sign-in branding guidelines give them: Microsoft's four 9px squares 1px apart in a 21 × 21 box (#F25022, #7FBA00, #00A4EF, #FFB900); Google's standard-colour "G" in a 48 × 48 viewBox (#EA4335, #4285F4, #FBBC05, #34A853). Both providers allow the colour mark on a light and on a dark neutral button, so one drawing serves both themes. The labels follow the providers' wording ("Continue with …"); the font is ours (the providers' rules on the label font are for their own button images; an application that must match them exactly can pass its own button).
  4. **PasswordField:** paste always allowed (WCAG 3.3.8), `autocomplete` 'current-password' (default) or 'new-password', autocapitalize / autocorrect / spellcheck off, written left to right in every language (a password is a code), aligned to the field's start. The show/hide toggle is a 28px subtle compact button inside the field at its end, named "Show password" with `aria-pressed` (pressed = shown) and `aria-controls`; Eye / EyeOff; a pointer press keeps the caret in the field. While Caps Lock is on (the key events' modifier state) "Caps Lock is on" stands under the field in a polite status region and joins the field's description. States through Field: description, error, required, disabled with reason, read-only (no toggle).
  5. **CodeInput** (one-time codes): one `role="group"` named by the label, its boxes text inputs named "Character 2 of 6" (numbers through `format.number`), description, error (every box `aria-invalid`) and disabled reason as Field's. **Pasting the whole code works into any box:** a text with at least as many usable characters as boxes fills them all from the first (`fillCode`); a shorter text fills from the box pasted into; spaces and dashes are dropped. The first box has `autocomplete="one-time-code"`; numeric codes have `inputMode="numeric"` and `pattern="[0-9]*"`; `kind="alphanumeric"` takes letters in upper case. Keys: a character fills its box and moves on; Backspace empties the box, or the one before when it is empty, and moves there (`eraseCode`); Delete empties the box; the arrows, Home and End move (`codeKeyTarget`). **Direction (choice):** a code is written left to right in every language, as numbers, money and codes are (P2.3a, P3.6c), so the boxes keep that order in a right-to-left page (`dir="ltr"` on the group) and stand at the page's start; the arrows follow the boxes' own order. Boxes 40 × 44px, 8px apart (six fit a 360px phone), the field's look, centred md text, tabular digits. `onChange` gives the value with an empty box as a space; `onComplete(code)` when every box is filled; `name` submits the code as one hidden value; `autoFocus`.
  6. **RecoveryCodes:** shown once — a warning Alert above the codes; the codes in two columns from 36em (one on phones), monospace, left to right, `select-all`, on surface.sunken, a named list. Three small neutral buttons: **Copy** (one code per line through the Clipboard API; "Copied" or, when the browser refuses, what to do instead, in a status region), **Download** (a text file made in the browser — `recoveryCodesText(heading, codes)` — through a Blob and a temporary link; nothing is fetched; `fileName`, default "recovery-codes.txt"), **Print** (the codes alone, from a hidden frame removed afterwards). `onAction('copy' | 'download' | 'print')` tells the application which way was used (its security log). "I have saved these codes" (a checkbox) enables **Continue** (primary, full width, last). `loading`: skeleton lines, every action unavailable.
  7. **SessionList:** the devices signed in, rows divided by border.subtle lines (no padding at the list's outer edges, P4.9). Each: the device kind's 20px icon (Monitor, Smartphone, Tablet), the device as the Core names it (sm semibold), a neutral "This device" badge on the current one (`aria-current`), and in xs text.secondary the place, the address (left to right) and "Active now" or "Last active 06.10.2026. 08:15". Other devices have a small neutral **Sign out** (named "Sign out: Edge on Windows"), busy while the Core's promise runs; the current device has none (the user menu signs out). `extraAction` is a slot per row before Sign out for the application's "This wasn't me". "Sign out all other devices" under the list (`onRevokeOthers`). Only this device: "No other device is signed in." `readOnly` (an administrator's view): no actions. `loading`: three skeleton rows.
  - **New helper `instantText(format, instant)`** (exported): an ISO instant in the tenant's offset written as `format.date` of its day and `format.time` of its clock ("05.10.2026. 14:12"), so day and time always agree (the P4.9 notifications rule), never shifted to the device's zone like `format.dateTime`. Used by SessionList and SignerList.
  - Messages: `signIn.email`, `signIn.continue`, `signIn.or`, `password.show`, `password.capsLock`, `code.box`, `recovery.codes`, `recovery.shownOnce`, `recovery.copy`, `recovery.copied`, `recovery.copyFailed`, `recovery.download`, `recovery.print`, `recovery.saved`, `recovery.continue`, `session.thisDevice`, `session.activeNow`, `session.lastActive`, `session.revoke`, `session.revokeNamed`, `session.revokeOthers`, `session.noOthers`.

### P5.7 — Kanban board

- **2026-10-08 — KanbanBoard (P5.7).** With `moveCard`, `placeOf`, `kanbanKeyTarget`, `dropTarget`, `dragLayout` (exported, tested in both directions).
  1. **Columns** 280px wide, 16px apart, from the leading edge; the board scrolls sideways inside its own region (named by `label`). A column is an area on surface.sunken, radius lg, 8px padding — **not a card**: the cards are the only cards (P4.9 rule 4); its heading (sm semibold, h2 by default) and, at the end, the application's count with its noun ("3 tasks"; the board never counts, rule 2). An empty column says "No cards" (xs text.tertiary) and is still a drop target (at least 80px high).
  2. **Cards** (raised, border.default, radius md, 12px padding, 8px apart): the title (sm medium; a link through `linkComponent` when the card has `href`), at most two description lines, the record it is about as "Invoice F-2026-0411 · Overdue" (P4.9 rule 7; the link is **underlined** because it stands in a line of text — axe `link-in-text-block`, WCAG 1.4.1), then the due date (DueDate), the application's meta ("2 comments") and the assignee's avatar (named) at the end.
  3. **Dragging (the general rule, "Launchpad dragging is live"):** pointer events, never HTML5 drag and drop; a mouse drag starts after 4px, a touch after a 250ms press without moving more than 8px (a swipe still scrolls). The card lifts — shadow-lg, border.strong, the raised layer, `cursor-grabbing` — and follows the pointer; the other cards slide with the slow motion token (250ms; 0ms under reduced motion); a placeholder of the card's height, radius md, a 2px dashed border.strong on surface.sunken — neutral, never blue — marks where it lands. The place is measured on the laid-out board when the drag starts: the column under the card's centre (else the nearest), and in it the place after every other card whose centre is above the dragged card's centre (`dropTarget`); shifts and placeholder from `dragLayout`. Measured slots need no mirroring in right-to-left (B.7). Releasing reports the move; Escape or a cancelled pointer puts everything back.
  4. **Keyboard (WCAG 2.5.7: every drag has another way):** each card has a 28px handle (GripVertical) named "Move: <title>", described by the instructions. **Space or Enter picks the card up** (`aria-pressed`), ArrowUp / ArrowDown move it within the column, the arrow pointing **forward in the reading direction** moves it to the next column keeping its height in the list as far as possible (`kanbanKeyTarget`), Space or Enter drops it, Escape (or leaving with Tab) cancels. The handle keeps the focus across columns. Every step is announced in a polite status region ("Picked up …. To do, position 1 of 3.", "Dropped …", "Move cancelled. … is back in its place."), positions through `format.number`.
  5. **Menu** (MoreHorizontal, 28px, "Move to: <title>"): the other columns — **the card goes to the end of that column (it does not jump ahead of work already there)** —, then Move up / Move down (disabled at the ends). On phones it is the main way between columns.
  6. **Phones** (below 48em or `layout="phone"`): one column at a time, chosen in a SelectField whose options carry the counts; `column` / `onColumnChange` make it controlled; a card moved by the keyboard to another column takes the select with it.
  7. **`onMove({ cardId, from, to })`:** the index is counted without the card itself, as `moveCard` applies it; the board shows what it is given, so the application decides (and may refuse). `readOnly`: no handles, menus or dragging. `loading`: skeleton columns.
  - Messages: `kanban.emptyColumn`, `kanban.moveCard`, `kanban.cardMenu`, `kanban.moveTo`, `kanban.moveToColumn`, `kanban.moveUp`, `kanban.moveDown`, `kanban.instructions`, `kanban.lifted`, `kanban.moved`, `kanban.dropped`, `kanban.cancelled`, `kanban.column`.
- **2026-10-08 — Example screens of P5.6 and P5.7.** **Sign in** (`/sign-in`): AuthShell with EmailFirstForm ("Work e-mail"), Continue (the walk-through's button keeps its name), "or", Continue with Microsoft / Google, "No account? Ask your company's administrator"; new story "Sign in, providers". **Tasks** (`/tasks`): To do, In progress, Waiting and Done columns whose cards link to the other groups' screens (F-2026-0410, statement 188, the VAT return, the signing page); counts with nouns through `format.number`; the story moves a card with its menu and checks the counts.

### P5.18 — Complex documents

- **2026-10-08 — Document lines in EditableGrid (P5.18, additive).**
  1. **Line types** (`getRowType`, `components/line-types.ts`), by typography, never colour: a **section heading** and a **text line** are one field across the row (`lineText`: `{ columnId, value }`, changes reported through `onCellChange` with that column id), the heading semibold, the text line xs text.secondary; a **subtotal** has no field — its text end-aligned and semibold across the columns before the first value column, its amounts (from the application) in the display columns, a 1px border.strong rule above (`border-t-strong` on its cells — in a bordered grid `SUBTOTAL_RULE`'s `border-strong` would colour every side), no remove button (the application removes a section with its heading); **discount** and **deduction** are lines whose negative amounts come from the application. Phones keep the types by typography in the same flat list.
  2. **Keyboard across types** (`gridKeyAction`, additive `rowCells` and `preferredColumn`, tested): rows without cells (subtotals) are skipped by Enter, Shift+Enter, phones' "next" and the focus after Ctrl+Delete; a one-cell row takes the focus in its cell, and the column of the last full line comes back in the next full line. Ctrl+Enter and Enter on the last row add a normal line.
  3. **"Add line ▾"** (`addTypes`): the button adds a normal line; its chevron (SplitAction's look, neutral default weight, "More options: Add line") offers the rarer types the document allows — text line, section heading, discount, deduction. `onAddRow(index, type)` gets the type (additive second argument); a heading's subtotal is the application's to add with it. Without `addTypes` it stays the plain "Add line" of P4.9.
  4. **Lookup column** (`type: 'lookup'`): one LookupField per line instead of a "Type" column (`value`, `results(row)`, `onSearch(rowId, query)`, `loading`, `recent`, `kinds`, `allowOneOff`, `onSearchAll(rowId, query)`, `create`: `GridLookupCreate`). The chosen record's kind stands in the cell after the field as xs text.secondary ("Service", "Fixed asset", "One-off"); "+ Create …" opens LookupCreateDrawer at grid level and fills the line through `onCellChange`; the focus returns to the line.
  5. **Details per line** (`details`, `GridDetail` `{ text, internal? }`): under the row in xs text.secondary, before its messages, and pointed to by the row's fields (`aria-describedby`). **Internal values** (`internal: true`, e.g. a fixed asset's book value) are written after the "Internal" note (`grid.internal`, semibold). What reaches the customer's PDF is the Core's rule; the mark only says so on screen. A stock warning is an ordinary row message of tone warning.
  6. **Tax category and unit columns** (`type: 'taxCategory'` with `categories`, shown as the code with the rate through `format.percent` — "S 20%", `taxCategoryText`; `type: 'unit'` with `units` — "pc" shown, its UN/ECE code "H87" stored). Both are SelectFields in the cell; the unit is its own column (P4.9d).
  7. **One-off lines** where the application allows them (`allowOneOff`): the line then needs an account and a tax category — the application's validation, shown as row messages.
  - Messages: `grid.textCell`, `grid.headingCell`, `grid.addText`, `grid.addHeading`, `grid.addDiscount`, `grid.addDeduction`, `grid.internal`. ComboboxPopover takes an additive `className` (internal; LookupField's minimum width).
- **2026-10-08 — 300 lines stay responsive (P5.18, measured).** A grid of fields was slow at 300 lines (before: typing a character 1.8–3.0 s, inserting a line 9–14 s, the page about 20 s, partly the story's queries and axe). Three causes, three fixes, cells still always fields: (1) **an inserted line mounted every line after it again** — the table body was an array of per-line arrays keyed by position; it is now one flat keyed list (this also affected P3.4 grids); (2) **every cell rendered on every change** — each editable cell is a memoised `GridCellEditor` whose props are the values its column gives for the row (the column compared by what its field draws, not by identity); the line number in each field's name comes from a context read by a tiny `CellName`, so an inserted line renames the fields below without rendering them; callbacks reach the latest props through one stable object; (3) **mounting 1,800 fields (600 of them Radix selects, which render their items while closed) and running axe over them** — a grid of **100 lines or more** (`VIRTUALIZE_FROM`, or `virtualize`) draws only the lines in view and 600px around them (`gridWindow`, `editable-grid-window.ts`), **plus the focused line, one before and two after it** (so Enter, Tab and the row shortcuts find their target), with spacer rows; heights measured once drawn (37px estimated before), the view follows any scrolling ancestor and resizing (one capture listener, one animation frame), and assistive technology still hears the whole table (`aria-rowcount`, `aria-rowindex`; on phones `aria-setsize` / `aria-posinset`). **Measured** (built Storybook, Chromium of Playwright 1.63 on the owner's Windows machine, event to next painted frame, five times each; "4×" is Chrome's CPU throttling):
  - "EditableGrid / Specification, 300 positions" (312 rows, 6 fields each): typing 11–19ms (4×: 107–160ms); Enter to the next line 14–34ms (88–204ms); inserting a line 54–124ms (536–836ms, one 2.4 s outlier on the first insert); ready about 2.2 s after navigation.
  - "Examples / Interim situation, editing the specification" (324 rows in AppShell and DocumentPage): typing 20–31ms (111–178ms); Enter 14–29ms after a first 200ms (59–159ms after a first 977ms); inserting 53–88ms (220–481ms).
  - Found on the way: `format.money` creates an `Intl.NumberFormat` on every call (`currencyFirst`), about 9% of a re-render of 300 amounts.
- **2026-10-08 — The "+ Create …" panel is a Drawer (P5.18/P5.19, `LookupCreateDrawer`).** Chosen per D14: a **Drawer at the end** — a short edit with the document still visible. A popover inside a 28px grid cell would cover the lines around it and, being non-modal, would let the focus leave a half-filled record. Title "New <noun>", the fields name (prefilled with the typed text, focused and selected), unit of measure, price (MoneyField in `currency`) and tax category; Cancel, then Create (DialogFooter). The application saves: `onCreate(draft)` returns a promise of the new record, or null when it did not save (its `errors` under the fields); while it runs the panel cannot be closed (no close button, Escape and a press outside ignored, buttons disabled). On success `onCreated(option)`; the focus returns where the application says (`onCloseFocus`). Messages: `lookup.createTitle(kind)`, `lookup.name`, `lookup.unit`, `lookup.price`, `lookup.taxCategory`, `lookup.createButton`.
- **2026-10-08 — Complex documents: one block order (P5.18, the owner's brief).** DocumentPage renders its blocks in a fixed order, and a block without content is not rendered: `banner` (a cancelled document's marker) → the header (lifecycle, back, number, status, actions; counterparty, `details`, the new `currency` block, key figures) → the "Based on" `references` → the lines' card (the lines; the `specification` summary row; the totals) → `notes` → `attachments` → the other `sections` → side panels as before. Phones keep the same order. New, all optional: `banner`, `currency`, `references`, `specification` (slots), `notes` and `attachments` (`DocumentBlock`: title, content, actions, drawn as SectionCards), and `totals` takes every DocumentTotals prop. `lines` may be null: the lines' card is then left out unless a specification or totals stand in it.
- **2026-10-08 — DocumentReferences (P5.18).** One line of links between the header and the lines: "Based on: Proforma PR-2026-031 · Advances A-2026-038, A-2026-044 · Contract 12/2026". Groups (`label` as shown, `kind` in the singular) and items (`number`, `href`, `status`) from the application; every number a link through `linkComponent` (left to right, medium). **A reference's state in one line (P4.9 rule 7), decided:** the state stands after the number as small text (xs medium) in its tone's text colour — the words carry it, neutral is text.secondary — and is part of the link's accessible name ("Advance invoice A-2026-038, Paid"; the visible state `aria-hidden`). A badge per number would make the line a row of pills; a tooltip would hide the state from touch. Groups are separated by a decorative "·", numbers of one kind by `references.listSeparator`; the line wraps on phones; empty kinds are left out, and the block renders nothing when all are empty. `label` replaces the lead: "Cancels" / "Corrects" for the back-links. Links are at least 24px high (`min-h-6`) when the line wraps.
- **2026-10-08 — Read-only lines with types (P5.18, DataTable, additive).** DataTable takes `lineType(row)`, `lineText(row)` (default the first column's cell) and `lineKind(row)`. A `heading` is one cell across the row (bold); a `text` line one cell across the row, xs text.secondary; a `subtotal` puts its label end-aligned and semibold before the amounts and the amounts under them, with a 1px border.strong rule above (the row before it drops its own line). **Which columns are the amounts, decided:** the trailing run of end-aligned columns (`subtotalStart`, tested) — no new column flag; with no end-aligned column the last one carries the amount. `discount` and `deduction` are lines. Headings, text lines and subtotals have no checkbox, no menu, and cannot be pressed. `lineKind` ("Item", "Service") stands under the first cell as xs text.secondary — never a colour. Phones keep the types by typography; a line's kind becomes its card subtitle when the card has none.
- **2026-10-08 — DocumentTotals: recap, deductions, home currency, footnotes (P5.18, additive).** Order: `rows` → `recap` → `deductions` → the final row → `exchange` → `footnotes`. **Recap by tax category:** a small table under its caption, column headers from the application (no "VAT" in the component, D5), rows: category, base, rate (`format.percent`), tax (`format.number` with the money decimals, or `format.money` with `recap.currency`); a missing rate or tax is "—", never 0. With a recap the block is **384px** instead of 320px, so four amount columns fit (chosen without an owner's value). **Deductions:** one row per advance, a group with a border.subtle line above the first, the label a link when the row has `href` (`TotalsRow.href`, new), right before the amount due. **Foreign currency:** `exchange` after the final row: the home-currency rows and the rate line in xs text.secondary ("1 EUR = 117,1825 RSD, NBS middle rate on 05.10.2026.", the rate through `format.number`, never rounded). The lines stay in the document's currency. **Footnotes:** under the totals, `<sup>` marker and text, given once; the lines repeat the marker ("E¹"). Without recap, exchange and footnotes the block is exactly P4.5's single `dl`; with them a `role="group"` named by `label`. Nothing is computed.
- **2026-10-08 — Currency block and notes (P5.18).** `DocumentCurrency` (currency, home currency, rate, rate date) in DocumentPage's `currency` slot: three values in the header's style ("Currency EUR", "Exchange rate 1 EUR = 117,1825 RSD", "Rate date 05.10.2026."). `DocumentNotes`: template texts chosen from the application's list (`NoteTemplate`) and a free note. View: the texts in order as paragraphs (line breaks kept); edit: a MultiSelectField of the templates with their texts under it, then a TextAreaField for the note. Edit mode is the application's. `chosenNoteTexts` and `hasNotes` (tested): a template no longer in the list is left out; a document without notes passes no `notes` block.
- **2026-10-08 — Long specification: a summary row and a full-screen sheet (P5.18, DocumentSpecification).** In the lines' card, under the lines, one row: "Specification of works: 300 positions, 537.836,68 RSD" (count and amount through `format`) with "Open" (neutral, Maximize2). **Decided per D14: a full-screen sheet** (the Sheet's `full` side): a read-only view of a part of the document, so the document stays under it, Escape and the close button return to it with the focus on "Open", and it needs no route of its own — `/projects/IS-2026-007/specification` is the editing page (EditableGrid), and a second page for the same data would be two patterns. The sheet: title and description in a header, the body scrolling; the application puts a DataTable with `lineType` and `stickyHeader` in it, then DocumentTotals with the same recap. Controlled (`open`, `onOpenChange`) or not.
  - **300 positions: virtualised (measured).** 324 rows, 9 columns, "Examples / Interim situation with a specification", from the press on "Open" (or a 2,000px scroll jump) to the next painted frame, five times each. **Without virtualisation:** opening 820–987ms, 4× 6.7–10.6 s — too slow. **With DataTable `virtualize`** (44px rows, one line each, 8 rows of overscan, `aria-rowcount`): opening 50–109ms (4×: 0.9–1.7 s); scrolling 21–50ms (4×: 345–590ms). A longer position text ends with "…" and is whole in the row's accessible content. The examples use `maxHeight="62vh"`.
- **2026-10-08 — Corrective documents are DocumentPages (P5.18, decided: no template of its own).** A decrease or increase document is a DocumentPage with "Corrects: Invoice F-2026-0410" references, its lines as **Original / Change / New** columns and the totals of the change. `correctionColumns(spec)` makes the three DataTable columns of one corrected value (`<id>.original`, `<id>.change`, `<id>.new`); `ChangeText` writes a change with its sign, medium weight, never rounded, "—" when empty. **New in `format` (additive):** `number` and `money` take `sign: 'always'` — "+" before a value above zero (zero, negative values and text unchanged; tested). A template would only have renamed DocumentPage's slots.
- **2026-10-08 — Cancellation: one dialog, then a marker on the page (P5.18).** **IrreversibleConfirmDialog gains `reason`** (additive): the same dialog asks why — a required text, or a choice from `reason.reasons` with optional details, as ReasonConfirmDialog — and for the typed number; the confirm button enables once both are given, and `onConfirm(answer)` receives `{ reason, text }`. The reason comes first, the typed number last (the thinking, then the decision); Cancel keeps the focus (P4.9d). One pattern, never two dialogs in a row. Then the application shows the status "Cancelled" (neutral badge) and **CancellationBanner** in DocumentPage's `banner` slot: the Banner look, **neutral by default** (a cancelled document is a state, not an error; `tone="danger"` for a louder Core), the Ban icon, "Cancelled", "Cancelled by Milica Petrović on 06.10.2026. at 11:20. Reason: …" (`format.date` and `format.time`, the tenant's time) and the link to the cancellation document. The cancellation document links back with `DocumentReferences label="Cancels"`. **The PDF's marker is the Core's**; the Design System draws only the screen.
  - Messages: `references.basedOn`, `references.lead`, `references.listSeparator`, `references.link`, `document.currency`, `document.exchangeRate`, `document.rateDate`, `document.rate`, `document.rateLine`, `document.cancelledTitle`, `document.cancelledBy`, `document.cancelReason`, `notes.templates`, `notes.free`, `specification.summary`, `specification.open`, `correction.original`, `correction.change`, `correction.new`.
- **2026-10-08 — Example screens of P5.18.** Draft invoice F-2026-0419 with lines found by search ("Invoice draft, lines by search"; the service USL-102 created in the walk-through); interim situation IS-2026-007 with its specification (contract 4.395.694,80, previous 1.047.939,40, this period 537.836,68, cumulative 1.585.776,08 RSD) and its editing page; F-2026-0418 (attachments with "Send with the e-invoice"), F-2026-0415 (foreign currency), decrease KO-2026-0009 of F-2026-0410 (total after it 167.762,75), F-2026-0407 cancelled by ST-2026-0004. Desktop and phone.

### P5.19 — Catalogs at scale

- **2026-10-08 — LookupField (P5.19).** The one searching field for catalogues of tens of thousands of records (customers, items, services, fixed assets, accounts), in forms and in document lines. ComboboxField's WAI-ARIA combobox (the input keeps the focus, the active row through `aria-activedescendant`, Enter chooses, Escape closes, Alt+ArrowDown opens) with: **the application's search only** — `onSearch(query)` after `searchDelay` (default 300ms; never for empty text), `results`, `loading` (the last results stay under the loading message); **recent records first** while nothing is typed (`recent`); **results grouped by kind** in the order of `kinds` (`LookupKind`: `key`, `heading`, `label`), records of no known kind last without a heading; each record a name, an optional second line (`description`) and an end detail (`detail`: "240 pc in stock"); **"+ Create <kind> “<query>”"** entries after the results while something is typed (`create`, reported by `onCreate(kind, query)`); a **one-off entry** where allowed (`allowOneOff`; reported as `{ value: '', label: query, oneOff: true }`); **"Search all…"** last (`onSearchAll(query)`, which opens the application's LookupDialog). **Choices made:** the list opens on typing, ArrowDown, Alt+ArrowDown or a press on the field — never on Tab alone (a grid of lookup cells would open a list in every cell); while something is typed the **first record found is active**, so Enter takes it (a "Create …" entry is never made active by itself, so Enter cannot create by accident); ArrowDown / ArrowUp wrap, PageDown / PageUp move by ten, Home / End go to the ends while the list is open. **Virtualised** with the company switcher's small virtualiser (`rowOffsets`, `visibleRows`, `scrollToShow`, reused): headings 28px, records 36px or 48px with a second line, action rows 36px; every option carries `aria-setsize` / `aria-posinset`; the kind headings are for the eye (`aria-hidden`: a flat virtualised listbox cannot nest groups) and each record's accessible name ends with its kind (", Fixed asset"). The list is at most 320px high and at least 20rem wide (or the screen less 2rem). "Nothing found" and "Loading…" stand above the list, outside the listbox (`role="status"`). Read-only is text, disabled shows its reason, errors as every field. The logic (`lookupRows`, `lookupKeyTarget`, `lookupRowHeight`, `lookupKindLabel`, `choosableCount`) is unit-tested. Messages: `lookup.recent`, `lookup.create(kind, query)`, `lookup.oneOff(query)`, `lookup.oneOffKind`, `lookup.searchAll`.
- **2026-10-08 — LookupDialog ("Search all…", P5.19).** The whole catalogue in a large dialog opened from a LookupField's last entry or a "Search all…" button: `title` (also the table's name), `description`, `initialQuery` (what was typed in the field), `onSearch(query)` (after FilterBar's 300ms pause), FilterBar's `filters` (default 2 inline), DataTable's `columns` / `rows`, `onChoose(row)` (the dialog then closes), keyset paging `hasNext` / `onNext` / `hasPrevious` / `onPrevious` (the application keeps the cursor; CursorPagination under the table), `count` / `countIsExact`, `loading`, `layout`.
  1. **Composed, not new:** FilterBar above a DataTable; the Dialog at 960px (at most 90% of the width), the table scrolling inside 55% of the screen's height under a sticky header. **Phones:** the full-screen sheet of P4.9d, the results a flat list, the safe area at the bottom.
  2. **Keyboard (decided):** the search field takes the focus when the dialog opens; **ArrowDown** moves the focus to the first result; ArrowDown / ArrowUp move between results and stop at the ends (ArrowUp from the first returns to the search), PageDown / PageUp by ten, Home / End; **Enter or Space chooses**; **typing on a result** (a character or Backspace without Ctrl, Cmd or Alt) sends the focus back to the search field, which receives the character. **Chosen over the combobox pattern** because the results are a table with columns: a listbox cannot hold a table, and DataTable's rows are already focusable and pressable. The keys are `lookupDialogKeyTarget` and `isTypingKey` (tested); the hint "Arrow keys move through the results; Enter chooses." stands under the table and is the dialog's description.
  3. **Nothing found:** the search counts as a filter for DataTable's empty state ("No rows match" with "Clear filters", which empties the search and the filters).
  4. The key listener is a ref callback, not an effect: a dialog open on the first render in a nested provider gets its portal content a moment later. Opening another modal at the moment the dialog closes does not work (Radix returns the focus to the dialog's opener, which closes the new one): an application opens the next overlay after the dialog has closed.
  - Messages: `lookup.keyboardHint`.
- **2026-10-08 — ImportWizard (P5.19, import with column mapping and a validation preview).** One card with the Stepper as its first row, the step's content, and the buttons at the bottom — Cancel at the start (when `onCancel`), then Back and the step's main action last. Four steps (`IMPORT_STEPS`, `step` / `onStepChange` controlled; the application reads and checks the file):
  1. **File:** a FileDropzone for one file (integration, 2026-10-09): `acceptText` and `maxSizeText` shown **before** choosing, `maxSize` checked on the device, the button or a drop; `onFileChoose(file)`; `reading` shows a Spinner; `file` (name, "1.213 rows, 8 columns, 96 KB") or `fileError` (`role="alert"`). Next is disabled until a file is read.
  2. **Columns:** one row per catalogue **field** (required marked " *" for the eye, "(Required)" for assistive technology), the file's column that fills it (a clearable SelectField, "Not imported" while empty), and a **sample value**. Rows per field rather than per file column, so the required ones are marked where they are chosen. The application's suggestion is the starting `mapping`; `suggested` marks those fields. **A column fills one field only** (`assignColumn`, tested), so a value is never imported twice. Unused columns are listed (`unusedColumns`). While a required field has no column, Next is an UnavailableAction ("Unavailable: Choose a column for Tax number", `missingRequired`).
  3. **Check — the validation preview, nothing saved yet:** the application's counts (ready, errors, warnings, duplicates) each with its icon and noun ("1.198 rows ready", "12 with errors"); the `previewNotice` slot (a DuplicateWarning); "Only rows with problems" (the application filters; `isProblemRow` for one that filters on the device) and "Skip rows with errors"; the rows (DataTable, virtualised above 100 rows) with their **line in the file**, each value with **its problems under it** (icon and words in the tone's colour, errors first: `issuesOf`). **Import is unavailable while rows have errors and are not skipped** (`importBlocked`, tested); the button says how many rows go in ("Import 1.198 rows").
  4. **Import:** JobProgress while it runs ("312 of 1.198" through the format; integration, 2026-10-09), then `result` (the application's report).
  - Phones: the mapping and the preview are flat lists in the card. **No read-only or disabled state:** a wizard is an action; a user who may not import does not get the page (D3).
  - Messages: `import.stepFile`, `import.stepColumns`, `import.stepCheck`, `import.stepImport`, `import.field`, `import.column`, `import.sample`, `import.notImported`, `import.suggested`, `import.columnFor`, `import.unusedColumns`, `import.missingRequired`, `import.ready`, `import.withErrors`, `import.withWarnings`, `import.duplicates`, `import.problemsOnly`, `import.skipInvalid`, `import.fixOrSkip`, `import.line`, `import.problems`, `import.noProblems`, `import.run`, `import.progress`.
- **2026-10-08 — BulkEditDrawer (P5.19, edit several records at once).** BulkActionBar → Drawer → ConfirmDialog composed: the application's "Edit N records" bulk action opens a Drawer from the end (D14), titled "Edit 24 records". **Each field that can be set for all** (`fields`: id, label, the application's `editor`, `valueText`) is a CheckboxField with **"Leave unchanged" under it by default**; ticking it (`toggleChanging`) shows the editor, indented. Rows divided by border.subtle lines (no cards in the drawer). **"What will change"** (an h3 region): each ticked field and its new value as a one-column KeyValueList (`changedFields`), or "Choose at least one field to change."; **Apply** ("Apply to 24 records") stays disabled until a field is ticked. Apply asks **once**: ConfirmDialog "Change 24 records?" with the changes in one line and "Change"; `onApply` may return a promise (the confirmation shows it is working and cannot be closed), then the drawer closes. A field the user may not change is the application's editor disabled with its reason. Deactivating is not a field here: it is a row or bulk action. Messages: `bulkEdit.title`, `bulkEdit.unchanged`, `bulkEdit.summary`, `bulkEdit.nothing`, `bulkEdit.apply`, `bulkEdit.confirmTitle`, `bulkEdit.confirm`.
- **2026-10-08 — Inactive records are hidden, never deleted (P5.19).** No new component. A catalogue has saved views **Active / Inactive / All** (ListPage, with counts) — the default hides inactive records; a LookupDialog offers a "Show inactive" filter instead. An inactive record is marked with a **neutral "Inactive" StatusBadge after its name**, never by colour or strike-through alone. Rows have **Deactivate / Activate** (row menu, and the bulk bar with one confirmation and the count); **a catalogue offers no delete**.
- **2026-10-08 — DuplicateWarning (P5.19).** The Core found records like the one being created; the Design System never looks for duplicates. A **warning Alert** (`role="alert"`): the `title` ("This may be a duplicate") and the Core's `message`, then the `matches` as **RelatedDocuments** (links with kind, number or name and state; reused, not a second list), then the choices: **"Open existing"** (only with one match: a link drawn as the neutral default button) and **"Create anyway"** (caution family; `createAnywayLabel`, e.g. "Import anyway"), the main choice last. With `reasonRequired`, Create anyway opens a **ReasonConfirmDialog** first and `onCreateAnyway` receives the answer. Used in a record form and in ImportWizard's `previewNotice`. Messages: `duplicate.title`, `duplicate.existing`, `duplicate.openExisting`, `duplicate.createAnyway`, `duplicate.reasonTitle`.
- **2026-10-08 — Example screens of P5.19.** **Customers** (`/sales/customers`): the dataset's seven customers and **50,000 generated** ones, virtualised; views Active / Inactive / All with counts; search and City / Group filters; Panonija Agro d.o.o.'s open balance 383.763,12 RSD (F-2026-0412 + F-2026-0403, added in paras); an inactive customer; bulk edit of the payment term and group; Deactivate / Activate; "Search all…" opens the LookupDialog. **Customer import** (`/sales/customers/import`): the four steps with an old system's CSV (1.213 rows), the suggested mapping, a duplicate warning (the same PIB), rows with errors and a warning; the import runs to "1.198 customers imported". **Measured** (built Storybook, Chromium, the owner's Windows machine while six other groups ran builds, so pessimistic): 50,007 records — a 2,000px scroll jump 163–463ms (4×: 243–1,089ms); selecting a row 86–109ms (4×: 135–1,072ms); switching view (the application filters 50,007) 120–200ms (4×: 103–1,761ms); a search 145–178ms (4×: 693–789ms); fewer than 60 rows in the page at any time. A real application pages from the server; the example holds 50,000 records to show the list stays usable even so.

### P5.20 — Registers and official forms

- **2026-10-08 — RegisterPage (P5.20).** A chronological register for a period — VAT records, the work-injury register, the safety-training register. Layout as ListPage: a **visible** page header (title, `back`, `status`, `subtitle`, `actions` — Print, Export, New entry, the main one last), then **one card**: the period row (`period`: the application's PeriodField or MonthField; `tools` at its end), the locks, the table to the card's edges, count and paging.
  1. **Never deleted, corrected:** a **No.** column before the application's columns and a **Correction** column after them, from `entry(row)` (`number`, `corrects`, `correctedBy`, `locked`): the new entry reads "Corrects no. 4" (text.secondary), the corrected one carries a neutral badge "Corrected by no. 7". The application's row menu offers "Correct entry"; the page has no delete.
  2. **Locked periods:** each `locks` item (`period`, `reason`, `detail`) is a row with a 16px Lock and words — "January–June 2026 is locked", the reason, the detail in xs — on surface.sunken between lines at the top of the card (rows, not a card in the card); each locked entry has a 14px Lock before its number and "Locked" for assistive technology, and **its menu holds one unavailable item that says why** ("Locked period: entries cannot be changed", `registerMenu`, tested) — disabled with a visible reason (D3).
  3. **Thousands of entries:** `virtualize` (DataTable's 44px rows inside `maxHeight`, default `70dvh`); the "5,000 entries" stories check that fewer than 60 rows are drawn and that `aria-rowcount` is 5001. Measured: a 2,000px scroll jump 129–266ms; 4× 677–3,427ms (the two date columns go through `format.date` per cell).
  4. Phones: entries as a flat list in the card, their number and correction among the details.
  - Messages: `register.number`, `register.correction`, `register.corrects`, `register.correctedBy`, `register.locked`, `register.lockedEntry`, `register.periodLocked`.
- **2026-10-08 — StatutoryFormPage (P5.20).** An official form mirrored with its own section and field numbers ("3.2", "8a.1"); definitions, numbering, rules and texts are the Core's data, and the page computes nothing.
  1. **Status draft → checked → submitted:** P4.5's **LifecycleBar** above the header (reused, not a second stepper: the form's life is a document's), the state as a StatusBadge after the visible title.
  2. **Sections** are SectionCards (h2, flush) each holding a DataTable `inCard`: **No.** (tabular, left to right) | **Description** | the previous period (`previousLabel`, none without it) | this period (`currentLabel`), amounts end-aligned through `format.money`; a `total` field is semibold. Phones: each field a row of the flat list with both periods under its number and name.
  3. **Drill-down:** an amount with `sources` is a button in the link colour with a dotted underline, named "510.809,60 RSD, sources of 3.2" (the visible amount first, WCAG 2.5.3); it opens a **Drawer from the end** titled with the field's number and name: each source a row — a link with kind and number, its date, amount and state — then the field's value under a border.strong line. The documents' amounts are the Core's.
  4. **Manual overrides:** a field with `editor` has a 28px pencil "Change 8a.2" (as ChangeableValue) that turns the amount into the editor with Cancel and Save (`onSaveOverride(field)`, a promise keeps the editor until it settles). An overridden value (`override`: `computed`, `by`, `at`) shows a neutral **"Changed manually"** badge, "By Ivana Stojanović on 05.10.2026. 14:12", "Computed: 68.957,60 RSD", and **"Use computed value"** — the way back. The Core keeps both values.
  5. **Rule checks** (`rules`: text, fields, result passed / failed / warning, `detail`): under each field they concern, those that did not pass, failures first (`fieldRules`), with an icon and words in the tone's colour; and **a summary Alert** above the sections — danger with a failure, warning with a warning, success otherwise (`rulesTone`) — titled with the counts and their nouns ("1 check failed · 4 checks passed", `ruleCounts`), listing the open checks each with **"Go to 8e.6"**, which scrolls to the field and focuses it.
  6. `readOnly` (a submitted form): no pencils and no way back; the drill-down stays. Print and export are the application's actions; `notice` takes an Alert of its own. A guided filing can put a Questionnaire before this page. **No loading state:** the Core sends the form whole.
  - Messages: `statutory.number`, `statutory.description`, `statutory.showSources`, `statutory.sources`, `statutory.noSources`, `statutory.fieldValue`, `statutory.overridden`, `statutory.overriddenBy`, `statutory.computed`, `statutory.useComputed`, `statutory.saveOverride`, `statutory.checkFailed`, `statutory.checkWarning`, `statutory.checkPassed`, `statutory.checksFailed`, `statutory.checksWarnings`, `statutory.checksPassed`, `statutory.goToField`, `statutory.checks`.
- **2026-10-08 — Example screens of P5.20.** **VAT return** (`/accounting/vat-return/2026-09`): an illustrative PP PDV-like form for September 2026 — 3.2 tax base 510.809,60 RSD drilling down to six invoices of the dataset, 8a.2 overridden by Ivana Stojanović (4.557,60 instead of 68.957,60), so "8e.6 must equal 8a.2 + 8b.2" fails with "Difference: 64.400,00 RSD"; August 2026 beside it; status Checked; all sums in whole paras. **Work-injury register** (`/hr/safety/injury-register/2026`): nine entries of 2026, January–June locked, no. 7 corrects no. 4; and a stress route with 5,000 generated entries. The legal codes, field numbers and texts are illustrative.
- **2026-10-08 — General rules of P5.19 and P5.20.** A catalogue record is deactivated, never deleted, and an inactive one is marked by a word; a register entry is corrected by a new entry that refers to it, both marked; a locked period says so with a lock, words and its reason, and its entries say why they cannot be changed; a value the system computed and a person changed shows who, when and the computed value, with a way back; a rule check stands next to the field it concerns and in a summary with a way to the field; a validation preview comes before anything is saved, and its counts carry their nouns.

### P5.21 — Common business processes

- **2026-10-08 — Users and roles: PermissionMatrix, the rest composed (P5.21).** With `isAllowed`, `setPermission`, `matrixKeyTarget`, `matrixFocusTarget` (tested).
  1. **A table** (caption the application's `label`): the areas as row headers (name sm semibold, optional description xs text.secondary), the actions as column headers (xs semibold, centred), a checkbox per cell named "Edit: Sales invoices". The value is the application's (area id → action ids); `onChange(value, { area, action, allowed })` reports every change.
  2. **A cell the action does not apply to** shows a dash, "Not applicable" for assistive technology, and takes no focus. **A cell the Core does not allow this role** (`unavailable` returns the reason) keeps its checkbox, disabled but **focusable** (`aria-disabled`), the reason as its description and tooltip.
  3. **Built-in roles are read-only** (`readOnly`): "Allowed" (a check) or a dash ("Not allowed", "Not applicable") as text — never disabled checkboxes (P2.2: read-only is plain text).
  4. **Keyboard:** one checkbox in the tab order (roving); the arrows move from checkbox to checkbox, past cells that have none, the arrow pointing forward in the reading direction to the next column (B.7); Home / End to the row's first and last, Ctrl (Cmd) + Home / End to the matrix's first and last; Space changes the checkbox. **Choice:** a `table` with this keyboard model, not `role="grid"` — the repository's jsx-a11y rules forbid an interactive role on a table, and the table keeps its native headers for screen readers.
  5. **Phones:** each area is a fieldset with its actions as CheckboxFields; unavailable ones disabled with the reason under them; read-only ones as text.
  - **Roles, members and invitations are composed, not new components** (one pattern per problem): DataTables of members (avatar, name and e-mail, roles, status badge, last sign-in through `instantText`, row menu Change roles / Deactivate), roles (built-in or custom, a count of members with its noun) and invitations (e-mail, role, invited by, sent, expires, Pending (info) / Expired (warning), row menu Send again / Revoke); the selected role's PermissionMatrix in a SectionCard (built-in read-only with "Duplicate"; custom editable, Cancel – Save in the card's header while there are changes). "Invite user" opens a Drawer from the end (D14). The three lists are page-level Tabs (D16); the drawer stands at page level outside the Tabs.
  - Messages: `permissions.cell`, `permissions.allowed`, `permissions.notAllowed`, `permissions.notApplicable`, `permissions.area`.
- **2026-10-08 — SetupChecklist (P5.21).** With `setupProgress`, `resumeStep` (tested). **One card** (radius lg) so the highlighted step's band reaches its edges and the card ends with its last step: the title (h2, the h4 size), an optional description, "2 of 5 done" beside a ProgressBar (at most 240px). The steps as rows divided by border.subtle lines, 12px by 16px: a 24px marker — done: a check on surface.sunken in a border.strong ring (LifecycleBar's completed step, neutral); blocked: a Lock; open: its number —, the title (with its state for assistive technology), the description, a done step's note (who, when) and a blocked step's reason in words. **Resume:** the step to do now — the application's `current`, else the first step still to do (a blocked step waits for something else) — is highlighted the neutral way (surface.selected with the 3px border.selected bar at its start, D17), carries `aria-current="step"`, and its action is the main button (primary, ArrowRight); other open steps' actions are small neutral buttons; a blocked step has none; a done step may offer "Change". Actions are links (through `linkComponent`) or buttons. `loading`: skeleton rows. Messages: `setup.progress`, `setup.done`, `setup.next`, `setup.todo`, `setup.blocked`.
- **2026-10-08 — Signing: SignerList and SigningPage (P5.21).** The signing itself is done by the Core or Liro Bridge; the Design System shows who signs and reports the user's choice.
  1. **SignerList** (with `signingSummary`, `signerTurn`, `maySign`, tested): "1 of 3 signed" first (a polite status); the signers in order (an ordered list): the order number in a 24px border.strong ring, the avatar, the name ("(you)" after the current user's), the role, the state in words — "Waiting", "Signed 05.10.2026. 14:12" with a check in status.success.fg, "Declined 06.10.2026. 09:02" with an X in status.danger.fg and the reason — never colour alone; dates through `instantText`.
  2. **Whose turn:** in order (`sequential`, default) the first signer still waiting may sign, as long as nobody declined (a declined document stops); `sequential={false}`: any waiting signer. The current user's row, on their turn, has **Decline then Sign** (D13): Decline the destructive light button, Sign the primary button (PenLine), busy while the Core's promise runs. Decline opens a **ReasonConfirmDialog** (Cancel focused, P4.9d). Before their turn the row says whose turn comes first ("Stefan Nikolić signs first"). Someone who does not sign sees only the states.
  3. **SigningPage** (template) **is built on DetailPage** — one header, one frame, one side-column rule: the header (back, the document's number as h1, status, subtitle, actions), the **preview** first and edge to edge (`preview`: DocumentFrame), the **attachments** (an AttachmentList), further `sections`, and the **signers** (a SignerList) in a SectionCard in the 300px side column from 75em. **Phones:** the signers come first — the person came to sign or to see who has —, then the document and the attachments.
  - Messages: `signing.summary`, `signing.waiting`, `signing.signedAt`, `signing.declinedAt`, `signing.you`, `signing.sign`, `signing.decline`, `signing.declineTitle`, `signing.declineConfirm`, `signing.notYourTurn`, `signing.signers`, `signing.document`, `signing.attachments`.
