- **2026-10-08 — MatchingView (P5.21).** Reconciles two lists — bank statement lines and open items, a supplier's statement and the purchase ledger (logic `matching-logic.ts`, tested). **Generic and controlled:** the lists (`MatchingList`: title, items, `selected` / `onSelectedChange`, `search` / `onSearchChange`, `empty`), the suggestions (`MatchSuggestion`: left and right items, label, `confidence` in words, `note`), the matches, every amount, what is left after a partial match (`remaining`) and the `summary` come from the application; the view reports `onMatch(leftIds, rightIds)`, `onAcceptSuggestion`, `onDismissSuggestion`, `onUnmatch`. It computes nothing and never decides whether two items may be matched.
  1. **One card, top to bottom:** a bar (the `summary` — KeyFigures — at the start; "Match" at the end, the screen's main action, with its keys as ShortcutHints); "Suggested matches"; the two lists side by side with a 1px border.default line between them; "Matched". Repeated items are rows divided by border.subtle lines (P4.9 rule 4). Suggestions first because accepting them is most of the work.
  2. **Confidence in words, never colour** ("Exact: amount and reference", "Likely: amount and payer"; xs medium text.secondary). No badge, no tone: a suggestion is not a state. A partial suggestion carries the application's note ("Leaves 36.420,35 RSD open on F-2026-0410"); after the match the item stays in its list with "36.420,35 RSD left" under its amount.
  3. **A suggestion or match row:** the left items, ArrowLeftRight ("matched with" for assistive technology), the right items; side by side from 36rem of the row's own width (`@container`, P4.9 rule 11), stacked below; then the confidence or note and the actions: dismiss (CompactIconButton, cancel intent), "Match" (small neutral, Link2), or "Unmatch" (small, subtle, Unlink2), each named with its suggestion or match.
  4. **The lists** are multi-select listboxes; the focus stays on the list and its active item is `aria-activedescendant` (the company switcher's pattern). **Keys (decided):** ArrowDown / ArrowUp (no wrapping: a long list has an end), Home / End, PageDown / PageUp by ten, **Space** selects or deselects, **Enter** matches the selection of both lists, **Escape** clears both selections, **Tab** goes to the other list (each list one tab stop; its search field before it). The keys are shown in the bar on desktop and are each list's description.
  5. **Neutral selection (D17):** a selected item is surface.selected with the 3px border.selected bar at its start; its checkbox is drawn checked (blue); the active item has an inset focus outline only while the list has the keyboard focus.
  6. **Rows:** title sm medium and subtitle xs text.secondary at the start, the amount at the end (sm medium, tabular, never wrapping) with the remainder under it; 12px by 16px.
  7. **Large lists (P4.9 rule 12):** each list may have a search field (the application filters); from **100 items** (`MATCHING_VIRTUALIZE_FROM`) a list draws only the rows in view — fixed 64px rows, a 480px scroll area, the company switcher's virtualiser, `aria-setsize` / `aria-posinset`. The "Large list (5,000 items)" story draws fewer than 40 options and searches.
  8. **Phones** (below 48em): one list at a time, switched by a segmented toggle whose items carry the list's title and how many are selected; the selection is kept while switching; the bar sticks under the shell's top (`--liro-shell-top`), so "Match" stays in reach.
  9. **States:** `loading` (five skeleton rows per list), an empty list ("Nothing left to match", the no-results EmptyState while a search is typed, or `empty`), `readOnly` (a posted statement: readable and navigable, `aria-readonly`, no checkboxes or actions), `matchUnavailableReason` (Match as an UnavailableAction with the Core's reason). Without a selection on both sides Match is an UnavailableAction with "Select at least one line in each list" — D3, never a silent grey button.
  - **Which one:** MatchingView pairs two sets of records; WorklistPage processes one list item by item; ComboboxField / LookupField choose one record for a field.
  - Messages: `matching.match`, `matching.selectBoth`, `matching.suggestions`, `matching.acceptSuggestion`, `matching.dismissSuggestion`, `matching.matched`, `matching.unmatch`, `matching.unmatchItem`, `matching.matchedWith`, `matching.search`, `matching.empty`, `matching.select`, `matching.clear`, `matching.spaceKey`, `matching.enterKey`, `matching.escapeKey`, `matching.showList`.
- **2026-10-08 — Balanced entry and BalanceBar (P5.21).** A journal entry is an EditableGrid (account combobox, description, debit and credit columns) with **BalanceBar** in its `footer` — beside "Add line" on desktop, in the totals card sticky at the top on phones (P3.6), so the balance is always in view. BalanceBar takes `debit`, `credit`, `difference` (decimal strings; it adds nothing), `state` ('balanced' | 'unbalanced' | 'incomplete', **the Core's rule**, not a comparison in the component), `currency`, `decimals`, `pending` and `layout` ('row' | 'stacked'). The amounts are SettlingValues, as the grid's totals. **Balanced:** CircleCheck and "Balanced" in status.success.fg. **Not balanced:** the difference and "Not balanced" with CircleAlert in status.danger.fg — words and tone, never colour alone. **Amounts missing:** the difference "—", never 0 (Appendix B.4), "Amounts missing" with CircleHelp in text.secondary; an unreadable amount keeps the grid's row message. The state's words are a polite `role="status"`. **Posting is the application's:** while the entry does not balance, "Post" is an UnavailableAction with the Core's reason ("Debit and credit must be equal. The difference is 2.000,00 RSD."). 'row': the three values on one wrapping row, 24px apart, the state after them; 'stacked': label at the start and value at the end per line, the state under them. Messages: `balance.label`, `balance.debit`, `balance.credit`, `balance.difference`, `balance.balanced`, `balance.unbalanced`, `balance.incomplete`.
- **2026-10-08 — PeriodicRunPage (P5.21).** A template for a process run once per period — payroll, depreciation, a VAT period close (`countChecks` in `periodic-run-logic.ts`, tested). Everything is the application's data: `steps`, `active` (the Stepper's index; `steps.length` = all done), `checks` (`RunCheck`: label, `result` passed / warning / failed / notRun, detail, an optional action), `lock` (open / locked with who and when), `progress`, `preview`, `rerun`, `actions`, `keyFigures`, `children`.
  1. **Layout, top to bottom:** PageHeader (back, the run and period as h1, status, subtitle, the step's actions — Rerun before them, the main one last); the **lock line** (Lock or LockOpen, "Period locked" / "Period open", who and when); the key figures; the **Stepper** (P2.5, reused); the **running step** (a SectionCard with JobProgress: the bar, "23 of 46" and the item being worked on, `RunProgress.current`; integration 2026-10-09); **Checks** (a flush SectionCard, rows: the result's icon and word in its tone — Passed, Warning, Failed, Not run yet in text.tertiary with a hollow circle; name and detail; the counts as the description, attention first); **Preview before posting** (KeyFigures and a DataTable `inCard`); further sections. Page padding 24px (16px on phones), DetailPage's maximum width.
  2. **Steps on phones:** "Step 3 of 5: Review" (`lifecycle.step`, reused) instead of the Stepper, whose five labelled steps would wrap into five rows at 360px.
  3. **Rerun with a reason:** "Rerun" (neutral, RotateCcw) opens ReasonConfirmDialog (caution family, the Core's reasons, Cancel focused) and reports `{ reason, text }`; with `unavailableReason` (a locked period, a running calculation) it is an UnavailableAction. The page does not change its own state: the application moves the run back.
  4. **Posting blocked by a failed check** is the application's UnavailableAction with the Core's reason.
  - **Which one:** PeriodicRunPage for a batch over a period that a person checks before posting; FormWizard for a form filled in step by step; LifecycleBar for a document's life; Questionnaire for questions; JobProgress for one long job.
  - Messages: `run.checks`, `run.passed`, `run.warning`, `run.failed`, `run.notRun`, `run.passedCount(count, text)`, `run.warningCount(count, text)`, `run.failedCount(count, text)`, `run.preview`, `run.periodOpen`, `run.periodLocked`, `run.rerun`.
- **2026-10-08 — Example screens of P5.21.** **Users and roles** (`/settings/users`): eight members, five built-in roles and the custom Site manager with its editable PermissionMatrix (two cells unavailable with the Core's reasons), pending and expired invitations; the invite drawer. **First-run setup** (`/setup`): Stanić Elektro STR, 2 of 5 steps done, Import customers next (to `/sales/customers`), the bank blocked "Waiting for Banca Intesa to confirm the agreement."; on phones the main step in the bottom bar. **Contract awaiting signatures** (`/hr/contracts/RU-2026-017/signing`): RU-2026-017 for Stefan Nikolić (the questionnaire's answers), preview, two attachments, signers in order (one signed, two waiting); the current user Milica Petrović does not sign. **`/sign/RU-2026-017`**: what Stefan sees from the link in his e-mail; Sign makes it "2 of 3 signed". **Bank statement matching** (`/banking/statements/188`): 14 lines (12 payments, a fee and interest booked to accounts), open items, seven suggestions (one partial: 50.000,00 on F-2026-0410 leaves 17.762,75 open after its decrease; one payment to two invoices), a malformed reference matched by hand, one line left unmatched so "Post statement" is unavailable with "1 line is not matched yet." **Journal entry** (`/accounting/journal/NK-2026-0912`): supplier invoice UF-2026-1204 booked (144.720,00 RSD), and an unbalanced stage (`?stage=unbalanced`, difference 2.000,00, Post unavailable). **Payroll run** (`/hr/payroll/2026-09`): 46 employees computed per employee in paras, half-up, at Review with one warning; "rerun" goes back to Calculate. Rates are illustrative, not the law. Desktop and phone each.

### Integration (2026-10-09)

- **2026-10-09 — Provider sign-in marks.** The Microsoft and Google marks are drawn by `ProviderSignInButtons` from `packages/ui/src/components/provider-marks.tsx` (`mark: 'microsoft' | 'google'`), not shipped in `@veljaos/tokens` (tokens holds Liro's own look only). Their fixed brand colours are the one place outside packages/tokens with hex values: `liro/no-raw-colors` is off for that one file in eslint.config.mjs (a protected-file exception, named there). The marks are trademarks of Microsoft and Google, used only to identify the sign-in provider as their brand guidelines allow, unaltered, on neutral buttons (never the provider's colour as a fill). Liro's own logo still comes only through props (D18).
- **2026-10-09 — @shadcn/react is pre-1.0.** `@shadcn/react` 0.3.1 (published 2026-08-31) may change its API in a minor version: it is pinned exactly, re-checked on every update, and wrapped by our primitives, so only `primitives/message-scroller.tsx` and `primitives/questionnaire.tsx` would change (see "Versions").
- **2026-10-09 — Name clash.** Group E's `lookupKeyTarget` (LookupDialog's keys) is exported as `lookupDialogKeyTarget`; `lookupKeyTarget` is LookupField's.

- **2026-10-09 — One pattern per problem: which list of events.** **HistoryList** — the whole history of one record (who changed what, old → new), a section of its page. **ActivityList** — the latest two or three entries or comments, compact, in a side panel. **StatusTimeline** — the states of one process over minutes to days (a delivery to SEF, a payment order, an import) with what comes next, in a side panel or a section. **LifecycleBar** — the dots of a document's life above its header, one row. **MessageThread** — a conversation (comments, a task). **Stepper / FormWizard** — steps the user goes through now; **JobProgress** — one job running now. One record may show several (F-2026-0410: LifecycleBar above, StatusTimeline in the delivery panel, ActivityList in the side panel, HistoryList and MessageThread as sections), each for its own question.
- **2026-10-09 — One busy button.** ConfirmDialog's loading look (the content kept at opacity 0 under the 20px oval loader in the text colour, `aria-busy`, a second press ignored; `waiting` makes the other buttons of a group unavailable without the loader) is `LoadingButtonPrimitive` in `primitives/button.tsx`; ConfirmDialog, EmailFirstForm, ProviderSignInButtons and SignerList use it instead of their copies. A new busy button uses it too.
- **2026-10-09 — Neutral day keys.** "Today" and "Yesterday" are `day.today` / `day.yesterday` (were `notifications.today` / `.yesterday`), written by one `dayHeading` for HistoryList, MessageList and the notifications page. A breaking rename of two message keys, allowed before 1.0 (changeset).
- **2026-10-09 — Group pieces wired together.** AttachmentList replaces the temporary file lists (F-2026-0418 with its per-file "Send with the e-invoice" control in `extra`; both signing screens); DocumentFrame hosts the contract in the signing screens and the SigningPage stories (one story viewer, `document-frame-story-data.ts`); ImportWizard chooses its file with FileDropzone (one file, `maxSize` / `maxSizeText` added) and shows JobProgress while importing (`import.chooseFile` and `import.progressText` removed); PeriodicRunPage's running step is JobProgress (`RunProgress.text` became `current`); the draft invoice's "Search all…" opens LookupDialog over the whole catalogue; F-2026-0410's delivery panel is a StatusTimeline; the examples' `Shell` takes the current company (group C's copy removed) and an agent button; `LineType` is exported once and the examples use it; every story's decimal arithmetic goes through `amounts-story-data.ts`.
- **2026-10-09 — One dataset.** F-2026-0410 shows group D2's lines, the ones KO-2026-0009 corrects: 186.420,35 issued, 167.762,75 after the decrease (06.10.2026., a "Corrected by" reference and a history entry), 67.762,75 due after 100.000,00 paid — in the list, the customer's balance, the task and the agent's question. F-2026-0407 is Cancelled (ST-2026-0004) in the list, the VAT records and the customer's balance, so the overview's overdue receivables, computed from the list, leave it out: 58.440,00 RSD from 1 invoice (F-2026-0411), not 152.940,00 from 2. **The rule:** an invoice counts in a total, count or table only while it is open (`isOpen`: issued, not cancelled, something due); the overview's overdue sum and its largest open invoices, the bank matching's open items and the customers' balances all use it, and amounts are added in whole paras, never as JavaScript numbers. `dataset-checks.ts` runs when the examples load and throws when a cancelled invoice owes or counts anywhere, or when a decreased invoice's list total, page total, amount due or customer balance ignores its decrease; the Invoice list and Overview stories check the same on screen.
- **2026-10-09 — The walk-through.** Home: Banking opens bank statement 188, Accounting the journal entry, Payroll the September run, a Tasks module; the HR tabs Contracts, Payroll and Safety at work; the bell's statement notification (n6) opens statement 188; "Company settings" in the user menu opens users and roles; "New invoice" opens the draft; the search (Ctrl K) lists the new screens and two new actions. The "Walk-through" story goes on to statement 188 and the tasks.

- **2026-10-09 — One row window for long lists (owner; measured).** **The rule:** every virtualised list draws the rows in view and 600px above and below them (`WINDOW_OVERSCAN`), keeps the row that holds the focus drawn wherever it is with one row before and two after it (`FOCUS_ROWS_BEFORE`, `FOCUS_ROWS_AFTER`), puts spacer rows wherever drawn rows are not next to each other, and still tells its whole size (`aria-rowcount` / `aria-rowindex`; `aria-setsize` / `aria-posinset` on phone cards). The rules and `rowOffsets` / `visibleRows` / `scrollToShow` live in the neutral `components/virtual-rows.ts` (they left `templates/company-logic.ts`; never public). The company switcher, LookupField, MatchingView and EditableGrid's row window use them; DataTable keeps TanStack Virtual (the plan's choice for tables) with the same rules (`overscan` 14 rows of 44px, cards 6 of 104px; a `rangeExtractor` adds the focused row). **Cheaper rows:** DataTable's rows are memoised (`data-table-row.tsx`: a scroll renders only the rows entering the window, a selection only the row selected; cells call `column.cell(row)` directly; cell classes joined once per table; handlers and `getRowLabel` read through a ref — `getRowLabel` must be a function of the row only); a row's menu is built on its first press or focus (`LazyDropdownMenu`, internal; the trigger carries `aria-haspopup`, `aria-expanded` and `data-state` as before); the provider caches its Intl formatters per (locale, options) and the currency's side per (locale, currency), amounts staying decimal strings (D4, tested). **Measured** (the owner's laptop, Ryzen 7 5700U, otherwise idle; built Storybook, Chromium of Playwright 1.63, event to the next painted frame, medians of two interleaved runs, 4× CPU throttling): a 2,000px jump in the register's 5,000 entries 166–174ms before, 178–193ms after; the work-injury register example 184–192 → 206–219ms; Customers 50,007 jump 195–208 → 202–234ms, selecting a row 226–227 → 203–227ms, switching view 283–292 → 272–295ms; EditableGrid 300 positions typing 42–44 → 39–41ms (at 1× all about 30–60ms). A jump now draws about 40 rows instead of 28 in the same time (a row costs about a quarter less), so a fast scroll never shows blank rows and the focused row is never unmounted. **Group E's seconds (up to 3.4 s) were the machine's load** — six groups building and testing at once — not the lists. Selecting and switching view in the Customers example are bounded by the example (the whole screen renders on a selection; TanStack Table builds a row model of 50,007 rows on a view switch), the application's part: a real application pages from the server. Part of every figure is Storybook's highlight addon, which applications do not run. Phone cards share the window rules but are not memoised yet. TanStack Virtual for the custom lists follows in its own pull request.
- **2026-10-09 — TanStack Virtual for every list (P5.21a; measured).** The company switcher, LookupField, MatchingView and EditableGrid's row window leave the custom virtualiser (`rowOffsets`, `visibleRows`, `scrollToShow`, `gridWindow`, removed with their tests) for `useVirtualizer` of `@tanstack/react-virtual` 3.14.13, the plan's choice ("TanStack Table + TanStack Virtual") and already DataTable's: one virtualiser to know, update and fix. The window rules are unchanged and shared (`virtual-rows.ts`): `overscan` is 600px as rows (`overscanRows`), a `rangeExtractor` keeps the focused line or the active option with one row before and two after it (`keepFocusedRow`, tested; DataTable uses it too), spacers stand between drawn rows that are not neighbours (`spacersBetween`), and the ARIA is as before (`aria-setsize` / `aria-posinset` on the options, `aria-activedescendant` now always names a drawn option; `aria-rowcount` / `aria-rowindex` in the grid). **Per list:** the company switcher and LookupField give `estimateSize` their known heights (headings 28px, rows 36px, 48px with a second line; nothing is measured) and keys per list of rows, keep the active row in view with `scrollToIndex` (a new search scrolls to the top with `scrollToOffset`); LookupField's listbox is its own small component mounted with the open list, so a list opened anew starts at its top. MatchingView: 64px rows from 100 items, `scrollToIndex` for the keyboard. EditableGrid scrolls with the page, not in a box of its own: custom `observeElementRect` (the window) and `observeElementOffset` (how far the window's top is below the first line's top, read once a frame after any scrolling or resizing), a `scrollToFn` that does nothing, and no scroll correction on a resize (`shouldAdjustScrollPositionOnItemSizeChange` false — the browser's scroll anchoring already keeps the view, and TanStack's own correction would count the shift twice); heights are measured as before (a line and its notes, a card and its gap) and handed over with `resizeItem`, kept per line id and layout. The focused line is now drawn apart from the window with a spacer between, as in DataTable, instead of every line between them. The company switcher, LookupField and MatchingView drew 240px around the view before; they now draw the 600px of the rule. `eslint.config.mjs` names the five files that call `useVirtualizer` in its `react-hooks/incompatible-library` exception. **Measured** (the same laptop and method as above: built Storybook, Chromium of Playwright 1.63, from the event to the painted frame after the last DOM change, medians of 12 samples in two interleaved runs, before → after, ms): at 1× — company switcher (5,000) 2,000px jump 9 → 15, PageDown 10 → 11; LookupField (600 results) jump 10 → 18, PageDown 12 → 14; MatchingView (5,000) jump 10 → 15, End / Home 13 → 19; EditableGrid (300) 2,000px page jump 409 → 568, typing a digit 11 → 16. At 4× CPU throttling — 22 → 34, 23 → 24; 32 → 53, 34 → 41; 28 → 42, 30 → 44; grid jump 1,240 → 1,513, typing 39 → 37. Every list answers within a frame or two at 1×; the lists cost a few milliseconds more on a jump because they now draw 600px around the view instead of 240px (2½ times the rows, so a fast scroll shows no blank rows). The grid's page jump mounts about fifty lines of fields either way and stays bounded by them (the old window drew every line from the focused one to the view, so a jump from the top mounted fewer new lines and unmounted none); typing is unchanged at 4×.

## The owner's review of Phase 5 part 1 (P5.23)

- **2026-10-10 — How P5.23 was done (owner's brief).** Nine items of the owner's Storybook review of Phase 5 part 1, in one pull request. Each was fixed as a class — the whole library, its stories and the examples searched for the same fault, every occurrence fixed — and each general rule is written below. Three helpers worked on independent parts (the bank statement; the customers catalogue and multi-step flows; the register and correcting documents) and the full checks ran after their work was merged. Phase 5 part 2 is not started.
- **2026-10-10 — The bank statement is worked line by line (P5.23, the owner's review).** A statement line can close invoices, but it can also be a bank fee, salaries, a tax payment, a transfer between own accounts, an advance or a loan repayment; "match everything on one page" (MatchingView) treated every line as matching and put two lists on one screen. **The statement is the page:** number, statement date, bank and account; opening balance, money in, money out and closing balance as KeyFigures; whether opening + in − out = closing checks out, as an icon and words (the Core's check, computed in whole paras); the bank's file (PDF/XML) in DocumentFrame inside a wide Dialog; the status (Imported, In progress, Posted); the progress ("3 of 10 lines done" with a ProgressBar). **The lines are a WorklistPage** — the same "decide items one by one" as the supplier invoices to approve, so the application has one way to do it. A row: the counterparty, the line number and the reference or purpose, the signed amount (`format.money` with `sign: 'always'`), the state in words, and for a done line how ("Closes F-2026-0412", "Bank fee: 5530 Bank charges"). **The line in focus** shows everything the bank sent — counterparty and signed amount in the heading; counterparty account, value date, payment code with its meaning, model and reference number, purpose — then **"What is this payment?"**, a RadioGroupField preselected by the Core's best suggestion, its reason as the field's description:
  1. **Close open items:** a CandidateList of the Core's ranked items (invoices, supplier invoices, credit notes: number, kind, partner, due date, open amount, the reason — "Exact: amount and reference", "Same customer, amount differs by 0,40 RSD"), one or several chosen; "Search all open items…" opens LookupDialog; the result before confirming ("Closes F-2026-0412 in full.", "Pays 50.000,00 RSD of F-2026-0410; 17.762,75 RSD stays open."); a difference is left open or written off (only up to the Core's limit, otherwise disabled with that reason); money over the items is recorded as an advance or written off.
  2. **Post to an account:** a Type select — Bank fee, Salaries, Tax or contribution payment, Transfer between own accounts, Loan repayment, Advance received / paid by direction, Other — fills in the account from the Core's settings; account and description as ChangeableValues (P4.9 rule 8), an optional cost centre.
  3. **Split over several accounts:** an EditableGrid of parts (account, description, cost centre, amount) with the journal entry's BalanceBar, the bank side against the parts' sum.
     Then Leave for later, and "Confirm and next" (the one primary). The next undecided line opens and a toast confirms with Undo; every decision can be taken back, and a done line reopened ("Change"), until the statement is posted. **Line states (decided):** Suggestion ready info; To do warning (needs a decision); Left for later neutral (a deliberate deferral); Done success — both To do and Left for later block posting, said in words. **Post statement** is an UnavailableAction with the reason ("7 lines still need a decision, and 1 line is left for later.") while any line is not done; a secondary button while lines remain, the primary once all are done (D13); it asks with a ConfirmDialog (Cancel focused) showing the summary (lines closing items, lines posted to accounts, money in, money out) and the journal entry's preview — a read-only DataTable (account, description, debit, credit) with BalanceBar, the journal entry's look. **Phones:** the summary, then the list; a tapped line is the whole screen (no list beside or under it) with Back to list, "7 of 10" and Previous / Next in its header, candidates stacked, Leave for later / Confirm and next in AppShell's sticky bottom bar; on the list the bottom bar holds Post statement. Example data (illustrative codes and accounts): ten lines — exact matches on F-2026-0412 and supplier invoice UF-2026-1204, a partial payment on F-2026-0410, a bank fee, a salary batch, taxes and contributions split over three accounts, a 0,40 RSD difference written off, a transfer to the own Raiffeisen account, an advance still to do, an unknown payer left for later; 5.284.550,17 + 291.566,40 − 4.180.462,00 = 1.395.654,57 RSD (`data-bank.ts` throws otherwise).
  - **Rule:** every flow where a person decides items one at a time (approvals, statement lines, future ones) is a WorklistPage: the list at the side, one item in focus, Previous / Next and the decisions (main last) in one bar, then the next item and a toast with Undo where the Core can take it back.
- **2026-10-10 — MatchingView removed (P5.23).** No screen needs two lists matched against each other any more: the bank statement is decided line by line, and a supplier's statement against the purchase ledger is the same question per line ("which open items does this line close?"). MatchingView, `matching-logic.ts`, `matching-story-data.ts`, its stories, tests, the 17 `matching.*` messages, its 48 baselines and its line in `eslint.config.mjs`'s `react-hooks/incompatible-library` exception are removed (alpha breaking). BalanceBar's tests moved to `balance-bar.test.tsx`; the stories' amount helpers are in `amounts-story-data.ts` (`sumUnits`). The entry "MatchingView (P5.21)" stays as history. **Which one, now:** WorklistPage decides items one by one; CandidateList chooses among proposed records within one decision; ComboboxField / LookupField choose one record for a field.
- **2026-10-10 — CandidateList (P5.23, new).** The records the application proposes for one decision, best first, each with its reason in words: the open items a payment may close, the record a new one may duplicate, the person a task may go to. Several (checkboxes, default) or one (`multiple={false}`: a radio group). A row: the control at the start; title sm medium, subtitle xs text.secondary, reason xs medium text.secondary; the figure at the end (sm medium, tabular, never wrapping); 12px rows divided by border.subtle lines (P4.9 rule 4). A chosen row is surface.selected with the 3px border.selected bar (D17); only the control is blue; the whole row is the control's label. Order, reasons, figures and preselection are the application's; the list reports the chosen ids in the candidates' order (`chooseCandidate`, tested) and computes nothing. Empty: `candidates.empty` ("Nothing to suggest") or `empty`.
- **2026-10-10 — WorklistPage: one bar for deciding (P5.23).** New: `back`, `status`, `subtitle` (a record's queue), `summary` (between the title and the list; on phones only above the list), `detailActions` (the item's decisions, main last), `onPrevious`. From md a bar under the scrolling detail (a border.default line on top, 12px by 24px) holds the position ("3 of 11", `worklist.position(index, indexText, total, totalText)`, the numbers through `format.number`), Previous item and Next item (arrows mirrored in right-to-left), then `detailActions`; it is always in view, and publishes its height as `--liro-shell-bottom` so toasts stand above it. Below md the detail is the whole screen: Back to list, the position and Previous / Next (36px icon buttons) in its header; the decisions in AppShell's bottom bar. The application chooses the previous and next item. The approvals moved Reject / Approve into this bar. `worklistPosition` exported (tested). Messages `worklist.previous`, `worklist.position`.
- **2026-10-10 — Wide dialogs and a confirmation's preview (P5.23).** `Dialog` and `ConfirmDialog` take `size: 'wide'` (720px, at most 90% of the screen) for a read-only view that needs room (a document in the viewer, a table). `ConfirmDialog` takes `preview` — what the action will do, under its message (a summary, a journal entry) — so a hard-to-reverse action shows its outcome in the dialog that asks; Cancel still takes the focus.
- **2026-10-10 — A draft's header is the invoice draft's (P5.23, the owner's review of NK-2026-0912: the Date "fell out", a pencil beside two plain values).** **Rule:** every value in a draft document's header is a `ChangeableValue` on one wrapping row — a value the user may change has its field (the Date a DateField, the Journal a SelectField, the number a TextField), a value the user cannot change here (the "Based on" record link, a contract, a site) is a `ChangeableValue` **without `field`**: the same label, the same 28px value row, no pencil, so the values stand on one baseline whether they can be changed or not. `ChangeableValue.field` is now optional (additive). Hand-made header facts are gone (the journal entry's `Fact`, the specification editor's `Detail`). The "Based on" link carries its state as small tone text after the number and in its accessible name ("Supplier invoice UF-2026-1204, Approved"), as DocumentReferences do. Checked: the invoice draft (example and template story), the journal entry, the specification editor — the three drafts of the library. The "Journal entry" story checks that the three values share one top and that only Date and Journal have a pencil.
- **2026-10-10 — A card that holds the page's only table has no title (P5.23, the owner: "Lines" above the journal entry's lines).** The table's column headers say what it is and the page's title names the document; a title only adds a row and 16px. Removed: the journal entry's "Lines", the specification editor's "Specification of works" (its h1 already says "specification of works"). DocumentPage's `linesTitle` stays for a page with more than one table; its documentation says so. Not this rule: a dashboard's cards (each a different view among charts) and the sections of a statutory form (several tables) keep their titles.
- **2026-10-10 — One gap between in-page tabs and their content; where tabs stand (P5.23, the owner's review of Users and roles: no space between the tabs and the table, desktop and phone).** **Cause:** `Tabs` put its panel straight under the tab line; each screen added its own space or none (SettingsPage added 16px, Users and roles nothing). **Rule:** the gap is `Tabs`' own — spacing md, 16px (`TAB_PANEL_GAP`, on every panel, tested); a panel's content adds no space above itself (SettingsPage's own 16px removed; the stories' panels lost their padding). **Where tabs stand:** tabs that switch between **different content** (Members / Roles / Invitations; the groups of a SettingsPage) are `Tabs` and stand **above** the content with that gap; views that **filter one list** (Active / Inactive / All; All / Unread) are the list card's saved views and stand **inside** the card (ListPage, NotificationsPage). Searched: the only `Tabs` screens are Users and roles and SettingsPage; every filtering view already stands in its card. FormTabs is the form card's first row (P3.6) and keeps its own spacing.
- **2026-10-10 — An alert with one line is one line high (P5.23, the owner's review of the VAT return: "5 checks passed" far too tall).** **Cause, found:** StatutoryFormPage passed `{open.length > 0 && …}` as the message; with nothing open that is `false`, which the Alert took for a message — an empty block and its 10px gap under the title (62px). **Rule:** an Alert with only a title — no message, or one that renders nothing (`false`, null, '', exported `hasMessage`, tested) — is **compact**: padding 10px by 16px (`ONE_LINE`), the icon centred on the title's line, 42px high. A Banner is a one-row strip and uses the same padding (was 16px). Every Alert and Banner was checked: the VAT return and StatutoryFormPage stories (compact when every check passes), RecoveryCodes, DuplicateWarning, the import's result (a title and a message: unchanged), CancellationBanner and AppShell's banners (one-line padding). The shell markers are strips with their own 8px padding (P5.3) and are unchanged.
- **2026-10-10 — Cancelled is danger, everywhere (P5.23, the owner).** The status-to-tone map is the application's data (BUILD-PLAN A.7: "cancelled danger"); the examples had "Cancelled" neutral in their map and on some screens. **One map for every example screen** (`TONES`, `toneOf`, `statusBadge` in `example-shell.tsx`): every screen and story of the examples takes a status's tone from it — the list, the quick preview, the page header, related documents and references, the phone cards — instead of writing a tone beside the label. **CancellationBanner** is danger by default (the badge's tone; `tone="neutral"` only where an application's map says so), announced politely (`role="status"`, Banner's new optional `role`: a state shown when the page opens is not an interruption, even in the danger tone), and says the word once: the title "Cancelled", then "by Milica Petrović on 06.10.2026. at 11:20. Reason: …", then the link to the cancellation document (`document.cancelledBy` now writes the part after the title — English default changed, signature unchanged). **Contrast:** new checks in `contrast.ts` — text.primary on every tone's bg, and text.link on every tone's bg over the page, raised, sunken and header surfaces — all pass in both themes; over a dark overlay a link on the neutral tone measures 4.43:1, so a neutral alert inside a dialog or drawer carries no link (a button instead). **The map (application data, examples only):** neutral — Draft, Imported, Inactive, Deactivated; info — Issued, Sent, Sent to SEF, Query sent, In review, Calculating, Checked; success — Delivered, Accepted, Paid, Booked, Posted, Completed, Approved, Signed by you, Submitted, Active; warning — Partially paid, To approve, Awaiting signatures, Awaiting your signature, In progress, Pending, Expired, Locked; danger — Overdue, Cancelled, Rejected, Suspended. Changed by this review: Cancelled neutral → danger; Completed and Accepted neutral → success (a finished step is a good outcome); In review warning → info and Pending info → warning (as A.7).
- **2026-10-10 — The bulk bar is a row of its own (P5.23, the owner's review: glued between the filter row and the table header, items too close).** Of the two options the owner gave — its own row, or replacing the filter row while rows are selected — **its own row** was chosen: it works the same wherever the bar stands (a DataTable on its own, a ListPage card, a WorklistPage list), search and filters stay visible and changeable while rows are selected, and replacing the filter row would need ListPage to know the selection DataTable owns. BulkActionBar now has the filter row's height and padding (at least 60px: 16px at the sides, 10px above and below a 36px control line), 12px between items, a thin vertical line (border.strong) between "3 selected" and "Select all 47.832", standard 36px buttons (the clear button an IconButton, "Select all" and the actions standard ActionButtons 8px apart), and the list keeps 12px above and below it (16px from a card's edges). `UnavailableAction.small` and the internal `OverflowRow.small` are removed (the bar was their only user; alpha breaking). **General rule:** a bar that appears inside a list is a row with the list's own rhythm — the height and padding of the row beside it, standard gaps and controls, the standard space to its neighbours.
- **2026-10-10 — Catalogue lists sort by every column and filter by several values (P5.23).** A catalogue (customers, suppliers, items, services, fixed assets, accounts) is searched and compared: every column is `sortable` and every choice filter a `multiSelect` (customers in Kać or Niš) by default. The application sorts, as for any controlled DataTable: text by the language's collation (`Intl.Collator`, numeric), amounts exactly as decimal strings read in whole units, never JavaScript numbers (D4). On phones FilterBar's "Sort" menu lists the same columns. LookupDialog gained `sort` / `onSortChange`. Applied to the Customers list and its "Search all…" lookup, the invoice draft's items/services/assets lookup (with a "Kind" filter) and the LookupDialog stories; no supplier, item, service or fixed-asset list page exists in the examples. A document list (invoices) is not a catalogue and keeps its own sortable columns. Documented in the ListPage description ("Catalogues").
- **2026-10-10 — A marker is not repeated where the view already says it (P5.23).** "Inactive" after a customer's name shows only where the list mixes active and inactive records — the All view, and a LookupDialog with "Show inactive". On the Inactive view the tab says it once (table and phone cards alike). **General rule:** a row marker every row of the current view would carry is left out.
- **2026-10-10 — Multi-step flows on phones (P5.23, the owner's review of ImportWizard: a stepper in two rows with broken lines, the summary wrapping unevenly, three stacked buttons).** At phone width (below 48em, or `layout="phone"`):
  1. **The Stepper is one line:** "Step 3 of 4 · Check" (13px semibold, the current step's description under it) over a 5px ProgressBar filled to the current step (hidden from assistive technology: the line says it). `Stepper.layout` (default by the viewport); on phones the line is not a control. New message `stepper.step` ("Step 3 of 4"); exported `compactStep`.
  2. **One footer row, sticky at the bottom while the flow is in view:** Back, then the main action last, above AppShell's bottom bar (`--liro-shell-bottom`) and clear of the home indicator; the buttons never wrap onto rows of their own (a long label wraps inside its button, P2.7d).
  3. **An unavailable main action's reason** is a line of its own under the row, describing the button (`UnavailableAction.reasonId`), never squeezed beside it (it may wrap to two lines at 360px; it is never cut).
  4. **Cancel becomes the header's close button** (X, 28px, "Cancel the import"); once a file is chosen leaving asks first ("Leave the import?", Stay – Leave), on phones and desktop.
  5. **ImportWizard's counts are a list**, one per line; its checkboxes stacked.
     Applied to ImportWizard (File, Columns, Check, Checking, Importing, Done, File error — a phone story each), FormWizard (`layout`) and PeriodicRunPage (its own "Step 3 of 5: Review" line replaced by the compact Stepper). Questionnaire already shows "3 of 7 answered" with a bar; its footer now never stacks, and is not sticky (it lives in bubbles and dialogs). LifecycleBar is not a flow and keeps its phone line. The internal `WizardFooter` holds the rule. Messages: `stepper.step`, `import.cancel`, `import.leaveTitle`, `import.leaveMessage` (the leave dialog reuses `form.leave` / `form.stay`). **General rule:** a multi-step flow on a phone says where the user is in one line with a bar, keeps Back and the main action in one sticky row (main last), puts an unavailable action's reason under that row, and moves Cancel to the header's close button, asking first when work would be lost.
- **2026-10-10 — One register pattern (P5.23, the owner's review: the space under "January–June 2026 is locked" differed between the template and the Examples, with a white strip in the template).** **Cause, found:** the template story passed `loading={false}`, the example none; a defined `loading` reserves DataTable's 14px refetch-loader slot in a card, which stood as a white strip between the grey locks band and the table header. **The page owns every space in its card:** RegisterPage passes DataTable's new `loaderSlot={false}` and draws the refetch loader (`RefetchLoader`, internal, shared with DataTable) at the end of its first row (period and tools); the locks band meets the table header whatever the application passes — checked in the template's and the examples' stories (`expectLocksMeetTable`). **Corrections both ways are the same link:** "Corrects no. 4" and "Corrected by no. 9" in the link colour, medium, 24px high, no badge (the grey chip is gone); pressing one scrolls to the other entry and focuses its number (an anchor with `tabIndex=-1` and a visible ring). A virtualised register draws the row first through DataTable's new handle (`ref`: `DataTableHandle.revealRow(id)`, TanStack Virtual's `scrollToIndex`, centred) — tested from no. 12 to no. 4990 of 5,000 in the template and the example. An entry not among the rows goes to the new `onGoToEntry(number)`; without it the reference is plain text. **General rule:** a container that draws a band right above a table in its card turns the table's loader slot off and shows the refetch in a row of its own (ImportWizard's check step, the only other such place, does the same).
- **2026-10-10 — A correcting document shows its source in the header (P5.23, the owner's review of KO-2026-0009: "Corrects: Invoice F-2026-0410 Partially paid" small and easy to miss).** The source is the most important fact of a decrease, increase, cancellation document, credit note or return, so it stands **beside the counterparty**, at the same level: DocumentPage's new `source` slot with the new **DocumentSource** — the application's label ("Corrects", "Cancels"), the source as a link with kind and number (sm semibold, link colour), its state (StatusBadge, as RelatedDocuments), and its date and total under it in xs text.secondary ("Issued 25.09.2026. · Total 186.420,35 RSD", `document.sourceDate`, `document.sourceTotal`, through `format`); several sources stand one under the other; on phones the two blocks stack. **Said once:** the "Corrects:", "Cancels:" and "Corrected by:" DocumentReferences lines are removed (DocumentReferences keeps the "Based on" line); the decrease's "Original total" key figure goes, since the source shows the total as issued. **Both ways:** the source lists its correcting documents in its Related documents panel (F-2026-0410: Decrease KO-2026-0009 first; F-2026-0407: ST-2026-0004), and where the correction changes its total the totals row names it as a link (`TotalsRow.href`). Applied to KO-2026-0009 and ST-2026-0004 and a new template story "Corrective document"; no credit-note or return screen exists yet. Replaces the back-link part of "DocumentReferences (P5.18)" and "Corrective documents are DocumentPages (P5.18)".
- **2026-10-10 — Room for the description in wide document tables (P5.23, the owner: item names in four lines).** **Cause, found:** KO-2026-0009's ten columns left Item 124px at 1440px, and "Gypsum boards 12,5 mm, 1200 × 2000 mm" took four lines; every other column had a 96px floor. **Rule:** a document table's description column keeps `DESCRIPTION_MIN_WIDTH` (240px, exported), so a name wraps to at most two lines; the correction columns (`correctionColumns`) and short codes (unit, tax category) may be as narrow as 64px (their headers wrap); amounts and codes never wrap; a table wider than its card scrolls sideways. `DataTableColumn.minWidth` now holds without resizing too (the column's width with its padding). Measured at 1440px over every Examples, DocumentPage, Documents, Statutory, Periodic-run and Register story: only the decrease exceeded two lines; now its Item is 240px, two lines, no sideways scroll (`expectAtMostTwoLines` in its play). The minimum is set on every read-only document lines table (F-2026-0410, the invoice and approval lines, D2's lines, the specification, the DocumentPage, DataTable and Corrections stories). Messages: `document.sourceDate`, `document.sourceTotal`.

- **2026-10-10 — Found while reviewing the new baselines (P5.23).**
  1. **A confirmation keeps its title and buttons in view.** The "Post statement" confirmation (summary and journal-entry preview) is taller than a 720px screen; the whole dialog scrolled, and with the focus on Cancel its title was out of view. Now only ConfirmDialog's body scrolls (`confirm-scroll`, a focusable region named by the title when there is a preview); the title row and the buttons stay. Dialog and Drawer already keep their title row sticky.
  2. **A story whose text depends on today has a fixed today.** The KanbanBoard Arabic and Japanese stories read the device's date, so "2 days overdue" became "3 days overdue" a day later and their pictures changed with no change in the code. `StoryProvider` takes `today`; the two stories pass 2026-10-09 (their old pictures stand). The other stories with dates relative to today use ExampleProvider's fixed date; no other picture changed between two days.
  3. **"3 of 10" in right-to-left (found by CI).** The worklist's position and the statement's "2 of 10 lines done" read "of 10 3" in a right-to-left page: a text that starts with a number and runs in English needs its direction from its content (`TEXT_DIRECTION`, D15). Both take it now; the compact Stepper and the other "N of M" texts already did. The partial-payment story ends at one scroll position, so its picture is the same on every run.

## Packaging

- **2026-09-24 — Consumers install tarballs.** `pnpm consumer-check` packs the three packages with `pnpm pack`, copies `apps/consumer-check` to a temporary folder outside the repository, installs the tarballs there and builds, typechecks and checks the app. `apps/consumer-check` is excluded from the workspace and from the root ESLint run, because its dependencies exist only in that temporary install (Appendix B.10).
- **2026-09-24 — What consumer-check proves.** The button renders (server-side render in Node); every CSS and JSON export resolves; the Vite bundle contains the token variables and the button utilities; the app typechecks against the packed `.d.ts` with `skipLibCheck: false`; `@veljaos/eslint-config` loads and reports Radix and internal `@veljaos/ui/*` imports. Rendering in a real browser comes with Playwright in P0.4.
- **2026-09-24 — Build outputs are declared.** Each build writes only to its package's `dist/`, and each build config states its outputs in a comment (Appendix B.10). `files: ["dist"]` in every package; no package is private.
- **2026-09-24 — No preflight in styles.css.** `@veljaos/ui/styles.css` contains the Tailwind theme and the utilities the components use, not Tailwind's reset: the reset belongs to the application.
- **2026-09-24 — @veljaos/tokens is a peer of @veljaos/ui.** The components' CSS reads the `--liro-*` variables, which the application loads once from `@veljaos/tokens/tokens.css`. pnpm warns that this peer is unmet in consumer-check, because it does not count a `file:` tarball as satisfying a version range; the peer is linked all the same.
- **2026-09-24 — Install scripts stay off.** `allowBuilds` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` denies the install scripts of esbuild and @parcel/watcher; both load prebuilt binaries from optional platform packages.
- **2026-09-24 — Versions start at 0.1.0-alpha.0**, the version the end of Phase 0 publishes; each later phase publishes the next `0.1.0-alpha.N`, and P6.5 publishes `1.0.0`. No `@veljaos/*` package was ever published, so there is nothing to collide with. (Corrected on 2026-09-24: the plan first said 2.0.0, assuming an earlier 1.0.0 existed.)
- **2026-09-24 — Proprietary license.** Every package has `"license": "UNLICENSED"`; the root `LICENSE` states the terms. Each build copies `LICENSE` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` into the package's `dist/` (`scripts/copy-legal.mjs`), and consumer-check verifies both are in every installed package.
- **2026-09-24 — Third-party notices.** `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` lists code copied into the repository, code included in the packages, runtime and peer dependencies, and what the Storybook build bundles. It is updated in the same pull request that adds any of these.
- **2026-09-25 — Package scope `@veljaos`.** The packages are `@veljaos/tokens`, `@veljaos/ui` and `@veljaos/eslint-config` (they were `@liro/*` until P0.5). GitHub Packages accepts only the scope of the account that owns the repository, and the GitHub name `liro` belongs to an unrelated person. Decided by the owner on 2026-09-25. Moving the repository to an organization later means renaming the scope again. Component and token names keep Liro (`LiroProvider`, `--liro-*`).
- **2026-09-25 — Notices checked against their sources.** The Tailwind CSS, React and Storybook notices match the upstream texts exactly (Storybook: `LICENSE` at tag v10.6.0). The Storybook build also bundles React DOM and scheduler (MIT, React's notice), axe-core 4.13.0 (MPL-2.0, with its third-party notices) and the Nunito Sans font (OFL-1.1); those notices were added. The `storybook` package pre-bundles its own dependencies without separate notices; they are not listed.

## Publishing

- **2026-09-25 — Tag-triggered publishing.** `.github/workflows/publish.yml` runs when a tag `v<version>` is pushed. It checks that the tagged commit is on `main` (so it passed the required checks) and that the tag equals the version of every package; runs unit tests, build and consumer-check; publishes with `pnpm -r publish` to `https://npm.pkg.github.com`; and confirms each version with `pnpm view`. Each package has `publishConfig.registry` and a `repository` field, which links the package to this repository.
- **2026-09-25 — No personal token.** The workflow authenticates with its own `GITHUB_TOKEN`, with `packages: write` for that job only. The token is referenced as `${NODE_AUTH_TOKEN}` in `~/.npmrc` and never written to disk.
- **2026-09-25 — Dist-tags.** A prerelease (`0.1.0-alpha.0`) is published under its identifier (`alpha`), a release under `latest`. `@veljaos/ui` pins its peer `@veljaos/tokens` to the exact same version (`workspace:*`), because both are released together.
- **2026-09-25 — Package visibility follows the repository.** The repository is public, so the packages are public too; installing from GitHub Packages still needs a token with `read:packages`. The code is proprietary regardless (`LICENSE`).
- **2026-09-25 — Version bumps were manual until the end of Phase 1,** where Changesets arrived with the first bump (`0.1.0-alpha.1`). A release: bump the versions in a step's pull request, merge, then tag the merge commit on `main` with `v<version>` and push the tag. Pushing a tag is not in the agent's allowed commands; the owner approves each one.
- **2026-09-25 — Storybook is not hosted.** No GitHub Pages or other hosting: it is viewed locally with `pnpm build` then `pnpm storybook`. CI keeps each pull request's static build as the artifact `storybook-static` (7 days); the publish workflow keeps the build of each version as `storybook-<version>` (90 days). The build carries `LICENSE` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`.
- **2026-09-25 — Changesets from 0.1.0-alpha.1.** `.changeset/config.json` releases `@veljaos/tokens`, `@veljaos/ui` and `@veljaos/eslint-config` as one `fixed` group, so they always share a version (the tag must equal every package version). The repository is in prerelease mode `alpha` (`.changeset/pre.json`) until 1.0.0: a patch changeset moves `0.1.0-alpha.0` to `0.1.0-alpha.1`. Changelogs use `@changesets/changelog-git`. Changesets only bumps and writes changelogs; publishing stays with `.github/workflows/publish.yml` on a tag. The config was written by hand: `changeset init` is interactive.
- **2026-09-29 — Release 0.1.0-alpha.2 (end of Phase 2).** `pnpm changeset version` in a release pull request moved the three packages to `0.1.0-alpha.2` from the Phase 2 changesets (P2.1–P2.8 and P2.7d); `@veljaos/eslint-config` changes only its version, which is a protected file, so the owner merges the release pull request. The remaining Phase 2 steps (P2.6b–P2.8d) reached `main` in one combined pull request (#35) at the owner's request, to save CI time; each step kept its changeset and status-table row.
- **2026-10-01 — Release 0.1.0-alpha.3 (end of Phase 3).** `pnpm changeset version` in a release pull request moved the three packages to `0.1.0-alpha.3` from the Phase 3 changesets (P3.0a, P3.1, P3.2, P3.2a, P3.3, P3.4, P3.5), which move to `.changeset/pre/`. `@veljaos/eslint-config` changes only its version, which is a protected file, so the owner merges the release pull request. New in this version: the `@veljaos/ui/form` subpath with React Hook Form 7.89.0 as an optional peer, and JetBrains Mono in `@veljaos/tokens`.
- **2026-10-07 — Release 0.1.0-alpha.4 (end of Phase 4).** `pnpm changeset version` in a release pull request moved the three packages to `0.1.0-alpha.4` from the changesets since alpha.3 — the P3.6–P3.7 polish and brand steps and Phase 4 (P4.0–P4.8, with P4.7a–P4.7c) — which move to `.changeset/pre/`. `@veljaos/eslint-config` changes only its version, a protected file, so the owner merges the release pull request. New in this version: the screen templates, `@veljaos/ui/charts` (the charts moved out of the root, an alpha breaking change), `format.percent`, the brand files and the owner's favicon.

## Agent permissions

- **2026-09-24 — `.claude/settings.json` is protected** (BUILD-PLAN rule 10). It allows pushing `bp/*` branches and only these `gh pr` commands: `create`, `view`, `checks`, `diff`, `list`, `edit` with `--body`/`--body-file`, and `merge <number> --auto --squash`. It denies pushing to `main`, force pushes, `gh pr close`, `gh pr review`, merges with `--merge`/`--rebase`/`--disable-auto`, anything with `--admin`, and `gh api`. Permissions are never widened without the owner's approval. Personal settings go in `.claude/settings.local.json`, which is git-ignored.
- **2026-09-24 — Auto-merge.** After opening a pull request the agent runs `gh pr merge <number> --auto --squash`; GitHub merges only when CI passes. A pull request that changes a protected file gets no auto-merge: the owner merges it.

## Storybook

- **2026-09-24 — Stories use the source.** Storybook aliases `@veljaos/ui` to `packages/ui/src`, so the provider in the global decorator and the components in the stories are the same module. Tokens come from the built `@veljaos/tokens`: run `pnpm build` before `pnpm storybook`.
- **2026-09-24 — Stories stay out of the package CSS.** `packages/ui/src/styles.css` excludes `*.stories.tsx` and tests from its sources; Storybook adds them back in its own `preview.css`.
- **2026-09-24 — Toolbar globals** go through `LiroProvider`: theme, direction (from locale, or forced), format locale (`en`, `sr-Latn-RS`, `ar`, `ja`), number scheme (from locale, or one of five), money decimals (0, 2, 4, 6). The viewport is Storybook's own: phone 390×844, tablet 820×1180, desktop 1440×900. Storybook turns a numeric URL global into a number, so the decorator compares values as strings.
- **2026-09-24 — Accessibility.** The a11y addon runs only the WCAG 2.0/2.1/2.2 A and AA rules, with `test: "error"`, so a violation fails the story tests of P0.4.
- **2026-09-25 — Foundations pages live in the Storybook app.** `apps/storybook/src/**/*.stories.tsx` holds pages that belong to no component, starting with "Foundations/Tokens". It imports `packages/tokens/src/tokens.ts` directly, so it shows exactly what the package publishes.

## Provider

- **2026-09-24 — First LiroProvider subset (P0.3).** `locale`, `direction` (default from the locale's likely script, via `Intl.Locale.maximize()`), `colorScheme` (`system` follows `prefers-color-scheme`), and `format` with `number`, `money`, `numberScheme` (default read from Intl) and `moneyDecimals` (default 2). P1.5 added messages, parsing, dates, `today`, `weekStartsOn` and `linkComponent`.
- **2026-09-24 — The provider sets `dir`, `lang` and `data-liro-theme` on a `display: contents` wrapper.** It adds no box to the layout, and all three still inherit. Portals (overlays, P2.4) will need their own container inside the provider.
- **2026-09-24 — Formatting works on the decimal string.** Zeros are added up to `decimals`; digits beyond it are never cut or rounded; a string that is not a decimal is shown unchanged. Group separators: `.`, `,`, no-break space, or right single quotation mark. The currency position comes from Intl for the locale; amount and currency are joined by a no-break space.
- **2026-09-24 — Numbers in right-to-left text need isolation.** Without it, the bidi algorithm moved the minus sign to the end of "-42,00" and put the currency on the wrong side. The Storybook sample wraps values in `<bdi>`. `NumberText` and `MoneyText` (P2.8) must do the same.
- **2026-09-25 — LiroProvider complete (P1.5).** All props of BUILD-PLAN section 5: `locale`, `direction`, `messages`, `format`, `today`, `weekStartsOn`, `colorScheme`, `linkComponent`. `useLiro()` returns the resolved values: the full `messages` and `format`, `today`, `weekStartsOn` and `linkComponent` (default `'a'`). Outside a provider it returns the English defaults, built once, with `today` following the device's date.
- **2026-09-25 — Messages.** `LiroMessages` (`provider/messages.ts`) holds exactly the keys the plan lists, each required, so `messages.en.ts` fails to compile when one is missing; a test proves it with `@ts-expect-error`. Components add their keys in the step that adds the component. `table.count` groups the count with the Design System's own formatter (`1,234 rows`), not with Intl.
- **2026-09-25 — parseNumber.** Implements Appendix B.3 on the text, never through a JavaScript number: both separators present → the last is decimal; one kind once → decimal, repeated → grouping; the screen's scheme only breaks the tie of an exactly grouped integer in its own grouping (`240.000` on dot-comma). Spaces (space, no-break, thin, narrow no-break) and both apostrophes always group. One kind of grouping per number, in groups of three or Indian 2-2-3; anything else is `null` (B.4) — for example `1,2,3` or `12 34`. Signs `-`, `+` and U+2212 are read; `-0` becomes `0`; digits are kept as typed (`1.50` stays `1.50`). A test prints every scheme's output and reads it back.
- **2026-09-25 — Dates are Gregorian with Latin digits.** `date` writes the locale's numeric date (day, month, year with two-digit day and month) with `calendar: 'gregory'` and `numberingSystem: 'latn'`: values are ISO dates, numbers are written with Latin digits everywhere, and `parseDate` must read back what `date` writes (for Arabic, Intl's default would be Arabic-Indic digits). `dateTime` writes the instant in the device's time zone. `date` on a value that is not a real date returns it unchanged, like `number`.
- **2026-09-25 — parseDate follows the locale's order.** Digits only (`010326`, `01032026`) and three numbers separated by `.`, `/`, `-` or spaces are read in the order of day, month and year of the locale's own numeric date, so `01/03/2026` is 1 March in `sr-Latn-RS` and 3 January in `en` (US order). ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` is read everywhere. A two-digit year is in the 2000s. Bidirectional marks that Intl writes into right-to-left dates are ignored. Impossible dates are `null`.
- **2026-09-25 — Serbian without a script is Latin.** Every Intl call goes through `intlLocale()`, which turns `sr` and `sr-RS` into `sr-Latn` and `sr-Latn-RS` (Appendix B.1); `sr-Cyrl-*` stays Cyrillic.
- **2026-09-25 — weekStartsOn from Intl.** `Intl.Locale#getWeekInfo()` (or the older `weekInfo` getter) gives the first day: Sunday for `en` and `ja`, Monday for `sr-Latn-RS`, Saturday for `ar`. Where the engine has neither, Monday. Month names are `month` 1–12, weekday names `weekday` 0 (Sunday)–6.
- **2026-09-25 — today.** Default: the device's local date, read once when the provider mounts. The application passes the tenant's date. The LiroProvider story fixes `today` in a nested provider, so screenshots do not change with the date.
- **2026-09-25 — Radix direction.** The provider wraps its children in Radix's `Direction.Provider` with the resolved direction, next to `dir` on its wrapper.

## CI

- **2026-09-24 — Two jobs, one pipeline.** Job `Install, lint, typecheck` (name kept from P0.1): lint → typecheck → unit tests → formatting → build → consumer check → Storybook build, then uploads `storybook-static`. Job `Story tests, accessibility, visual` runs in the pinned Playwright image on that exact build: story tests → accessibility → visual. Accessibility and visual run even when an earlier step fails, so one run shows every problem.
- **2026-09-24 — Story tests run on Playwright, not on the Storybook test runner or the Vitest addon.** `@storybook/addon-vitest` 10.6 supports Vitest 3–4 only (we use 5), and runs against the source instead of the built Storybook. `@storybook/test-runner` brings Jest as a second test framework and its own Playwright version. Instead, `apps/storybook/tests/support.ts` listens on Storybook's own channel and reads the `storyFinished` status Storybook computes (exceptions, play function, failed reports such as the accessibility addon's), plus `storyErrored`, `storyThrewException`, `playFunctionThrewException`, `storyMissing` and page errors.
- **2026-09-24 — Every check runs in four modes:** light/dark × ltr/rtl, set through Storybook globals in the URL. Story tests and accessibility include docs pages; visual compares stories only (docs pages are prose).
- **2026-09-24 — One list of accessibility tags.** `apps/storybook/.storybook/a11y.ts` holds the WCAG 2.2 A/AA tags for both the Storybook addon and the Playwright axe run. No rule is disabled and nothing is excluded.
- **2026-09-24 — Visual baselines** live in `apps/storybook/tests/__screenshots__/`, without a platform suffix, and are generated only in the pinned image: viewport 1280×720, device scale 1, full page, animations off, `maxDiffPixels: 0`. When a story has no baseline or a difference is intended, the failing run uploads the `playwright-results` artifact; the new images are reviewed and committed from it. The first baselines came from this route.
- **2026-09-24 — The server is started by the job.** Playwright's `webServer` runs `vite preview` on `storybook-static` with `reuseExistingServer: false` (Appendix B.9: a crashed, reused dev server once failed 166 of 198 tests).
- **2026-09-24 — Protected files (rule 10).** `scripts/protected-files.mjs` holds the list: `.claude/settings.json`; ESLint configuration and `packages/eslint-config`; every `tsconfig*.json`; `.github/`; the check scripts (protected-files, consumer-check); Playwright configuration, `apps/storybook/.storybook/` (Storybook test configuration and accessibility settings) and `apps/storybook/tests/*.ts`. Workflow `Protected files` runs on every pull request event, including description edits, and fails when the section `## Protected file changes` does not name each changed protected file. Its job summary lists them. Unit tests: `scripts/protected-files.test.mjs`.
- **2026-09-24 — Required checks.** Auto-merge waits only for required checks. `Story tests, accessibility, visual` and `Protected files` must be required checks on `main`, next to `Install, lint, typecheck`. That is a repository setting, which the owner changes.
- **2026-09-28 — No step timeouts in the test job (P0.4a).** The story tests, accessibility and visual steps each had a 5-minute timeout. With the P2.2a stories the accessibility run needed more than 5 minutes; the runner killed Playwright, but not the `vite preview` server Playwright had started, so the visual step failed on `http://127.0.0.1:6006/index.json is already used` and looked like an environment problem. Now Playwright stops a run itself after 25 minutes (`globalTimeout`), which also stops its server, and only the job has a timeout (90 minutes, for three runs). Story tests took 3.5 minutes on the runner at 2026-09-28; when a run approaches 25 minutes, the runs are split (sharding) rather than the limit raised silently.
- **2026-09-29 — Open item: a screenshot after a failed story interaction.** `tests/visual.spec.ts` takes its screenshot when the story has rendered, whether its `play` function passed or failed, so a failed interaction can be saved as a baseline (it happened once, P2.3d: the right-to-left Keyboard baseline showed a lost arrow key). The story tests do fail in that case, so CI stays red, but the picture is wrong. Decided by the owner: not changed now; the first step of Phase 3 handles it, together with splitting CI across several machines.
- **2026-09-30 — Tests on several machines (P3.0).** Before, one job ran story tests (10 min), accessibility (15 min) and visual (11.5 min) one after another, and a pull request waited about 39 minutes for CI. Now a matrix job (`Story tests (1/4)` … `Visual (light/dark × ltr/rtl) (4/4)`) splits each of the three suites into four parts with Playwright's `--shard`, and the twelve parts run on twelve machines at once (the repository is public, so parallel jobs cost nothing; GitHub allows 20 at a time). Each part starts its own server on the same `storybook-static` and runs in the same pinned image; `fail-fast` is off, so one run shows every problem. The required check keeps its name: a final job `Story tests, accessibility, visual` passes only when every part passed (a skipped or cancelled part fails it), so branch protection and auto-merge are unchanged. On failure each part uploads its results, and the final job joins them into the one artifact `playwright-results`, as before (each part's HTML report in its own folder `playwright-report-<suite>-<part>`). Four parts per suite leave room for the Phase 3 stories; when one part approaches 10 minutes, the count is raised rather than waiting. **Measured on 2026-09-30 (pull request #39, 258 stories and 1 docs page, four modes):** the whole CI run took 6 min 12 s from push to the last check (before: about 39 min). `Install, lint, typecheck` 1 min 11 s; then all twelve parts started together, each including about 1 min for the container, install and download: story tests 3 min 3 s – 3 min 18 s, accessibility 3 min 39 s – 4 min 48 s, visual 3 min 27 s – 4 min 7 s; the final job 4 s.
- **2026-09-30 — The visual test fails on a failed story (P3.0; closes the open item of 2026-09-29).** `tests/visual.spec.ts` now checks the story's outcome first, exactly as the story test does (no errors, status "success"), and takes and compares the screenshot only after that. A failed interaction fails the visual test with its own error, and no picture is written, even with `--update-snapshots` (tested locally by breaking the MonthField "Open" story's play function: the test failed on the interaction and wrote no image).

- **2026-10-06 — What a visual test does not see (P4.0).** `toHaveScreenshot` compares with `maxDiffPixels: 0` but Playwright's per-pixel colour threshold (default 0.2, a perceptual distance): a pixel counts as different only beyond it. Found when a search button changed from 35.5px to 30px high: its light grey border moved by 3px, every moved pixel stayed under the threshold, the test passed and `--update-snapshots=changed` wrote nothing (proved in the CI image: the screenshot differs from the baseline, the comparison passes). So a change of a faint line alone can leave a baseline stale; when a change is meant to move only such lines, regenerate those baselines with `--update-snapshots=all` for the stories concerned. The threshold is a protected setting and is unchanged.
- **2026-10-06 — Baselines on CI's machines (P4.0).** Locally, `scripts/update-baselines.mjs` takes 30–40 minutes on the owner's machine. The workflow `Baselines` (`.github/workflows/baselines.yml`) does the same in the same pinned image on twelve machines: one job builds Storybook, twelve run `test:visual --update-snapshots=changed --shard=N/12`, and each part collects the images Playwright wrote (newer than a marker file taken before the run), so only new and changed images return; a last job joins them into one artifact `baselines` (7 days) and lists them in the run's summary. It starts on a push to a `bp/*` branch whose commit message contains `[baselines]` — the agent needs no permission beyond pushing its branch — or by hand from the Actions tab. A push without `[baselines]` also starts the workflow, which then does nothing; such a run has a concurrency group of its own (its commit), so it never cancels an update in progress (the first version did: two update runs were cancelled by the next ordinary push). It never commits: a commit pushed with the workflow's token would start no CI run, so the pull request's head would carry no checks; the images are reviewed and committed by hand (AGENTS.md, "How to refresh visual baselines").
- **2026-09-30 — Open item: local baselines differ from CI on one page.** `node scripts/update-baselines.mjs` on the owner's Windows machine (Docker Desktop 29.6.2, the pinned image) renders "Foundations/Tokens / Meanings in both themes" 77px shorter (4,185px) than CI does (4,262px, the committed baseline, which CI passes): the first 4,185 rows of pixels are identical, and `main` without any change gives the same 4,185px locally, so the difference comes from the local environment, not from code. The faster way of working (P3.0) relies on local baselines matching CI; until this is understood, a baseline run that changes that page is not committed (P3.1 and P3.2 left those four images out). **To investigate before P3.4** (decided by the owner). Closed on 2026-10-01, next entry.
- **2026-10-01 — Local baselines match CI (P3.0a; closes the open item of 2026-09-30).** **Cause: the system monospace font, not the machine.** The Tokens page writes variable names in `font-mono`, and nothing in A.5's stack existed in the Playwright image, so the generic `monospace` decided. In that image fontconfig answers `monospace` with WenQuanYi Zen Hei Mono (a CJK font of the image), and Chromium used it for the first layout; after the first full-page screenshot (which re-sends the page's settings to the renderer) Chromium used its own Monospace preference, Liberation Mono, and kept it. The two fonts have different line metrics: each of the 40 table rows whose variable name wraps onto two lines grows from 48 to 50px, so the page grows from 4,185 to 4,262px after the first screenshot (measured: page height and the font of one cell via the DevTools protocol, before and after a screenshot). `toHaveScreenshot` takes screenshots until two in a row are equal: on a quiet machine the second and third (4,262px) agree, which is what CI saw (4-core runners, 2 Playwright workers). Locally 8 workers run on 16 threads; a 4,262px capture then takes over two seconds, the 5-second limit runs out between alternating sizes, and an update run wrote the first, 4,185px picture. **Not the cause** (checked): the image digest (the same), Node (24.21.0 in both), the viewport (1280 × 720, scale 1 in both), the operating system (both are the same Linux image; Docker Desktop's kernel only hosts it), and caches (each run installs and builds from scratch). The same test alone in the local container passed against the CI baseline; with 8 workers and 8 repeats it failed in most runs, and with a concrete monospace font (Liberation Mono, as an experiment in a throwaway copy) all 32 runs passed. **Fix:** JetBrains Mono is shipped (Typography, "Monospace is shipped"), so no story depends on the image's fonts or on Chromium's font settings; every story with monospace text got new baselines. **Noticed on the way, not changed:** the Storybook decorator's page wrapper is `min-h-dvh` with `p-4` in content-box sizing, so a short story is always 752px (the 720px viewport plus 32px) — stable, because it does not depend on timing, but it changes every baseline if touched.

## Lessons from the previous Design System

Copied from BUILD-PLAN Appendix B on 2026-09-25. Each was learned in Design System 1.0 by measurement or by a real defect; the step that applies it cites it.

- **2026-09-25 — B.1 Language tags name the script.** Always `sr-Latn` / `sr-Cyrl`, never bare `sr`: `Intl` reads bare `sr` as Cyrillic, which once produced Cyrillic month names on a Latin screen for weeks. Treat `sr-RS` as Latin (everyday Serbian usage).

- **2026-09-25 — B.2 Two locales that barely differ hide bugs.** Bosnian and Serbian share about two thirds of their strings; a stale setting rendered Bosnian for an afternoon while everyone believed it was Serbian. Any language picker shows the current language.

- **2026-09-25 — B.3 Number entry.** A masking input configured for Serbian turned `1234.56` into `123456` — a hundredfold error on the form people type when copying from foreign invoices. Hence no mask: parse the raw text. Accept `1234.56`, `1234,56`, `1.234,56`, `1,234.56`, grouping by space, non-breaking space, thin space or either apostrophe, and Indian 2-2-3 grouping. **The rule:** with both separators present, the last one is the decimal separator; with one kind present, it is a decimal separator when it appears once and grouping when it repeats; the screen's own number scheme is consulted **only** to break a tie the string cannot break itself (a string that is exactly a grouped integer in the scheme's own grouping, e.g. `240.000` on a dot-comma screen, is read as grouped). The alternative "under dot-comma a dot is never a decimal" breaks `1234.56` and was rejected. What the system prints, it must be able to read back — test every scheme's output through the parser.

- **2026-09-25 — B.4 Unreadable is `null`, never `0`.** An unparseable amount counted as zero once made an unbalanced journal entry look balanced. Also: `value * 100` is not a conversion (`1.005 * 100` is `100.49999999999999`); work on the decimal string. This was fixed once and reintroduced by a "simplification".

- **2026-09-25 — B.5 Tables.** Rendering both the desktop table and the phone cards and hiding one with CSS gave 1,592 ms per interaction at 932 rows; real branching plus virtualisation gave 120 ms. `table-layout: fixed` made columns worse. An empty value is an em dash (—), never a hyphen, which reads as a minus sign. Amount and currency joined by a non-breaking space so they never wrap apart. Raw numbers, not formatted text, go into CSV and Excel.

- **2026-09-25 — B.6 Contrast.** Opacity on text enters the contrast ratio (`opacity: 0.85` dropped a bar from passing to 4.31:1) — make quieter text with size and weight. Translucent dark-theme tokens assume the page background; on a coloured surface they mix (a tone on a blue bubble measured 2.34 instead of 6.32) — layer them over an opaque base. Status `solid` colours are for bars and dots, never text; text on a tone is its `fg` on its `bg`. Automatic contrast switching was measured and rejected: the threshold between two families was three thousandths apart. Contrast is measured after layers composite, not per token.

- **2026-09-25 — B.7 Right-to-left.** Three drag handlers (column resize, split divider, their arrow keys) all ran backwards in right-to-left because they computed in physical pixels. Every pointer or keyboard movement measures from the **leading** edge. This is a class of bug, not an incident. Progress bars flip (a bar is a metaphor for reading); prop names like `left`/`right` and symmetric stretches do not.

- **2026-09-25 — B.8 Ways of working that the owner established.**
  - Navigation is a **launchpad of module cards** at the root and **tabs inside a module**, not a sidebar.
  - **Full page** for creating or editing anything with more than about ten fields, tabs or attachments (it has an address, room for errors, and a back button); **drawer** for a short edit with the list still visible; **modal** for one action with one outcome, or a read-only view.
  - Routes: `/things`, `/things/new`, `/things/[id]`, `/things/[id]/part`; breadcrumbs from the third level; the table never decides whether a row opens a page or a drawer.
  - Actions at the top **and** at the bottom of a scrolling form; the bottom bar is sticky only while the form actually scrolls.
  - On a list: search on the start side, filters beside it, actions at the end, paging at the bottom. Once a user knows where search is on one screen, they know it on every screen.
  - One filled button per screen; the main action is last in its group.
  - The error sits next to the field. Success messages disappear; errors wait.
  - Hidden fields are never submitted.
  - A table on a phone is not a table: it is cards.
  - Confirmation does not scale: a bulk action asks once, with the count.

- **2026-09-25 — B.9 Habits.** A prop that is accepted and does nothing was found eleven times, and none failed lint, types, tests or the accessibility sweep — each was found by looking at a screen. Initials come from first and last name ("Ana Jovanović" → AJ, not AN). A finding is a hypothesis until code confirms it. When a check fails on more places than a change could touch, suspect the environment (a crashed dev server once failed 166 of 198 accessibility tests).

- **2026-09-25 — B.10 Packaging.** A package whose `files` field missed a sibling folder worked inside the workspace and returned errors for every consumer; a package marked private could not be installed at all. Workspace links hide both. That is why `consumer-check` installs **packed** tarballs. Every build tool task declares its outputs (a missing declaration once filled a cache to 223 GB).

- **2026-09-25 — B.11 The JMBG sex marker is not gender identity.** Never prefill anything about a person's gender from an identification number. (Domain validation itself is not in the Design System; this is recorded for whoever builds forms on it.)
